## Task 1: Verify row-version triggers fire on INSERT + UPDATE with distinctness guard

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/db/src/migrations/0033_sync_row_version_triggers.sql — `bump_row_version()` had no no-op guard. Combined with `lifecycle_dates.updatedAt` using Drizzle's `$onUpdate(() => new Date())`, every `.update()` call auto-bumps `updated_at`, so even a semantic no-op UPDATE (e.g. momentUpdate called twice with identical payload) would satisfy a vanilla `WHEN (OLD.* IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.*)` clause and bump `row_version`, emitting a redundant `put` on the next CVR diff. Fixed by adding migration 0038_sync_row_version_distinctness_guard.sql which replaces the function with a body-level guard: compare `to_jsonb(NEW) - 'row_version' - 'updated_at'` vs OLD's equivalent, and short-circuit when they're IS NOT DISTINCT FROM. Exclude the metadata columns so Drizzle-generated timestamp bumps don't count as a change. Also updated _journal.json (idx 38) and copied 0037_snapshot.json → 0038_snapshot.json (schema unchanged; new snapshot id + prevId chain).

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/db/src/migrations/0033_sync_row_version_triggers.sql:10-22 — Trigger is `BEFORE UPDATE` only (no INSERT). Confirmed intentional and correct: the in-migration comment explains it, and pull.ts step 6 relies on presence-in-current vs presence-in-prev (not row_version) to detect new rows. A new row's row_version defaults to 0, and the CVR diff emits `put` because `prevSnapshot.{table}[id]` is undefined, independent of row_version value. Adding `BEFORE INSERT` would be redundant.
  2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:141-183 — Pull publishes three entity types: assetMoments, assetComments, notifications. All three schemas have `row_version integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (comments.ts:42-43, moments.ts:54-55, notifications.ts:46) and all three have a bump trigger (0033 for moments/comments, 0037 for notifications). Cross-reference complete — no synced entity is missing a trigger.

Library improvements made:
  - None applicable — the fix is a Postgres trigger function, not a library call. Verified the `to_jsonb(record) - 'col'` pattern is idiomatic (confirmed via mcp__grep__searchGitHub in kuestcom/prediction-market audit trigger).

Checklist verification:
  - BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE — UPDATE covered by 0033/0037. INSERT deliberately omitted; CVR diff emits puts on presence, not row_version. Design documented in 0033 header comment.
  - No-op UPDATE guard — 0038 adds body-level guard excluding updated_at + row_version. Handles Drizzle $onUpdate auto-bump case where a simple WHEN clause would not.
  - Every synced table has a trigger — assetMoments (0033), assetComments (0033), notifications (0037). All three match the entities emitted by pull.ts.
  - Appropriate data types — rowVersion is integer (not bigint; 2^31 updates per row is effectively infinite for this domain). jsonb body column on notifications uses `.$type<>()`-style narrowing via NotificationType discriminants at the serialization layer (service.ts).
  - Idempotent DDL — 0033 uses CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION + DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS. 0037 does the same. 0038 reuses CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION — safe to re-run.
  - Migration safety for zero-downtime — 0038 is a function replacement only; no table locks, no column changes. Safe on large tables.
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green).

## Task 2: Verify backfill migrations are idempotent

Issues found and fixed:
  - None. Both migrations are already idempotent by design.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/db/src/migrations/0031_backfill_asset_member_owners.sql:3-6 — `INSERT ... SELECT ... ON CONFLICT ("asset_id", "user_id") DO NOTHING` on the composite PK. Safe to re-run. Uses the media_asset's `created_at` for both created_at and updated_at on the membership row so the backfilled rows look chronologically consistent with the asset; this is harmless for sync because the BEFORE UPDATE row_version trigger (0033) only fires on UPDATE, not INSERT — see Task 1 notes. Also, since `created_at` on asset_member is set from the pre-existing media_asset.created_at rather than NOW(), the `updated_at = created_at` invariant holds and won't later be bumped by Drizzle's `$onUpdate` unless someone actually updates the row.
  2. packages/db/src/migrations/0035_backfill_orphan_asset_member_owners.sql:7-13 — Doubly idempotent: a LEFT JOIN WHERE `am.user_id IS NULL` filter, plus `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` as defense-in-depth. Safe to re-run.
  3. Gap coverage claim verified: 0031 was added in commit 16ce298 ("refactor: replicache sync"), which introduced the asset_member table. `AssetService.create` didn't begin seeding memberships until a later commit (85b4937 / 1297719 — migration 0035 was added in 85b4937). Any asset created via the old (pre-seeding) create path between 0031 and 85b4937 would be missing its owner membership; 0035 closes that gap. Confirmed by reading the journal timestamps and current service.ts:170-173 which now inserts the owner membership inside the same transaction as the asset insert with `.onConflictDoNothing()`.

Library improvements made:
  - None. `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` IS the idiomatic Postgres/Drizzle primitive here; no library abstraction would be cleaner for a pure-SQL migration. (Noted for completeness: the runtime `AssetService.create` seeding also uses Drizzle's `.onConflictDoNothing()` which is the library primitive version of the same pattern.)

Checklist verification:
  - Idempotent (safe to re-run) — 0031 ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING; 0035 LEFT JOIN ... WHERE IS NULL + ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING. Both verified.
  - Migration safety for zero-downtime — both are additive INSERTs into asset_member; no schema mutation, no column changes, no table locks. Safe.
  - No raw string concatenation (SQL injection surface) — pure static DDL / DML, no parameter interpolation.
  - Appropriate data types — role is the 'asset_member_role' enum (defined in 0030); assetId/userId are text (matching media_asset.id / user.id); timestamps preserved from source.
  - PK integrity — 0031 and 0035 target the composite PK (asset_id, user_id); ON CONFLICT clause correctly references both columns.
  - Gap analysis — 0035's claim that it covers assets created between 0031 and the AssetService.create seeding change is verified by the commit sequence (0031 in 16ce298; seeding in 85b4937; 0035 also in 85b4937 as the compensating backfill). No orphan windows remain because the now-seeding create path uses `.onConflictDoNothing()`, so re-runs are harmless.
  - Ordering / independence — 0035 runs after 0034 which constrains asset_invite roles to non-owner; 0035 inserts into asset_member not asset_invite, so no CHECK interaction. Independent.
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green, full-turbo cache hit; no source changes).

## Task 3: Pre-deploy check for 0034 CHECK constraint

Issues found and fixed:
  - None. No code change required (task scope is pre-deploy operational verification).

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/db/src/migrations/0034_asset_invite_role_not_owner.sql:7-9 — `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT ... CHECK (role IN ('editor','viewer'))` is a plain (validating) constraint add. Postgres takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock and validates ALL existing rows immediately. In a large-table deploy this could stall writes for the duration. Here it's acceptable: asset_invite was introduced in 0030 of this same branch, so the table is empty on first deploy, and the expected steady-state row count is small (pending invites per asset). For future reference, the safer pattern for already-populated tables is `ADD CONSTRAINT ... CHECK (...) NOT VALID; ... VALIDATE CONSTRAINT ...` which keeps the validation out of the DDL lock window. Not changing here because (a) the current situation is safe, and (b) retrofitting two-phase validation once data is small gains nothing.
  2. packages/db/src/migrations/0034_asset_invite_role_not_owner.sql — Uses `ADD CONSTRAINT` without `IF NOT EXISTS` (syntax not supported on ADD CONSTRAINT in Postgres < 17 anyway). Drizzle's journal prevents re-application so this is fine; noting for the record.
  3. Independence from 0035 verified: 0034 targets table `asset_invite`; 0035 targets table `asset_member`. Different tables, different columns, no foreign key between them other than both referencing `media_asset`. No ordering constraint between 0034 and 0035 — they could run in either order without behavioral change. Current journal ordering is 0034 → 0035, which is what `_journal.json` idx 34/35 shows (entries copied verbatim: 0034 when=1776866713941, 0035 when=1776867555730).
  4. API-side safety verified: `packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/service.ts:10` defines `AssetInviteRole = Exclude<AssetMemberRole, 'owner'>`, and `AssetMemberService.invite(..., role: AssetInviteRole)` is the only code path that INSERTs into asset_invite. `packages/auth/src/signup-hooks.ts` only SELECTs and DELETEs from asset_invite. No code path inserts role='owner', so the CHECK is pure defense-in-depth against direct DB writes — it should never reject a row authored by this application's runtime.
  5. CHECK syntax compatibility with pgEnum column: `role IN ('editor','viewer')` compares the `asset_member_role` enum column against string literals. Postgres implicitly casts the literals to the enum via `unknown → enum` resolution; this is the standard idiom used throughout Drizzle's own generated migrations. No CAST is needed.

Pre-deploy verification (REQUIRED before running 0034 against any environment that may have seen manual DB writes):

    -- Expected: 0
    SELECT count(*) FROM asset_invite WHERE role = 'owner';

    -- If > 0, resolve offending rows before applying 0034. Either:
    --   DELETE FROM asset_invite WHERE role = 'owner';
    -- or downgrade:
    --   UPDATE asset_invite SET role = 'editor' WHERE role = 'owner';
    -- Then re-run `SELECT count(*)` — must return 0 — and only then `pnpm db:migrate`.

    Rationale: 0034's `ADD CONSTRAINT ... CHECK` validates every existing row at
    DDL time. One `role = 'owner'` row → migration fails → deploy blocked.

Expected result across all current environments: 0. asset_invite only exists in environments where migration 0030 (Apr 22 2026 onward) has been applied, and the only runtime insert path (AssetMemberService.invite) is TS-narrowed to exclude 'owner'. Any non-zero result would indicate a manual DB write outside the application and must be investigated before deploy.

Library improvements made:
  - None. Pre-deploy check is an operational verification, not a library call site.

Checklist verification:
  - Migration safety for zero-downtime — ADD CONSTRAINT takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE briefly; table is effectively empty in prod, so the lock window is negligible. Noted NOT VALID alternative for future large-table CHECKs.
  - No raw string concatenation (SQL injection surface) — pure static DDL.
  - Appropriate data types — CHECK targets the `asset_member_role` enum column; string literal comparison is idiomatic Postgres.
  - No ordering dependency — 0034 and 0035 target different tables, confirmed by reading both migrations.
  - Defense-in-depth rationale matches schema narrowing — confirmed asset_invite schema uses `.$type<'editor' | 'viewer'>()` (asset-members.ts:55) and service.ts enforces the same at the TS API boundary.
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green, full-turbo cache hit; no source changes).

## Task 4: Verify indexes on new FK columns and hot query paths

Approach: enumerated every query that touches the new tables (`asset_member`, `asset_invite`, `notification`, `replicache_client_group`, `replicache_client`) across `packages/api`, `packages/auth`, and sanity-checked FK columns without app-level WHEREs. Cross-referenced each WHERE / ORDER BY / JOIN predicate against the indexes declared in the schemas and the 0030/0032/0036 migrations.

Issues found and fixed:
  - None. Every application-level WHERE on the new tables is already covered.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/db/src/schema/notifications.ts:57-60 — `notification_recipient_row_version_idx` on `(recipient_id, row_version)` is unused today. The pull handler (pull.ts:182-184) selects notifications with `WHERE recipient_id = ?` only; `row_version` is read into JS and diffed against the CVR snapshot in memory. No query filters or orders by `row_version`. Kept (not removed) because (a) Task 14 in this plan explicitly calls for a prepared hot-path pull query that would filter by row_version, at which point this composite index becomes load-bearing; (b) removing it would require a new migration with rollback risk; (c) notification write volume is low (bounded to membership-lifecycle events per asset), so write-amplification cost is negligible. Same pattern exists on `asset_comment_asset_row_version_idx` / `asset_moment_asset_row_version_idx` (migration 0032) for identical reasons — out of scope for this task but noted for consistency.
  2. packages/db/src/schema/replicache.ts:5-12 — `replicache_client_group.user_id` has no index. Not queried by any app code (only the PK `id` is used for lookup in pull.ts:105 and push.ts:84). The `ON DELETE cascade` from `user.id` would perform a seq scan on user deletion — acceptable because user deletion is administratively rare and the table stays small (one row per browser profile, not per event). If a future feature needs "invalidate all client groups for user X on password rotation" an index should be added then.
  3. packages/db/src/schema/asset-members.ts:30-32,55-57 — `asset_member.invited_by` and `asset_invite.invited_by` are both un-indexed FKs (`ON DELETE set null` and `ON DELETE cascade` respectively). Neither is queried in a WHERE by application code. Acceptable because user deletion is rare and these tables remain small.
  4. packages/db/src/schema/notifications.ts:35-37 — `notification.actor_id` FK has no index. Queried only as a LEFT JOIN driven from `notifications` (pull.ts:183, service.ts:169); the join is `user.id = notifications.actor_id`, so the hot path uses `user`'s PK — no index on the FK side is needed.

Library improvements made:
  - None. Drizzle's `index()`, `primaryKey()`, and `unique()` builders are already the library primitives here, and all indexes declared match actual query patterns. No opportunity to replace hand-rolled index definitions with a higher-level abstraction (Drizzle has none for this).

Checklist verification (per `review-prompt.md` Queries & Data Access + Task 4 scope):
  - Every FK column queried in a WHERE has an index:
      - notifications.recipient_id — `notification_recipient_created_idx`, `notification_recipient_unread_idx` (partial), `notification_recipient_row_version_idx`
      - assetMembers.assetId (via PK prefix) and assetMembers.userId — PK + `asset_member_user_idx`
      - assetInvites.assetId (via UNIQUE prefix), assetInvites.email — UNIQUE(asset_id,email) + `asset_invite_email_idx`
      - replicacheClient.clientGroupId — `replicache_client_group_idx`
      - replicacheClientGroup.id, replicacheClient.id — PKs
  - `notifications (userId, createdAt DESC)` exists — verified as `notification_recipient_created_idx` on (recipient_id, created_at); Postgres backward-scans for ORDER BY … DESC.
  - Partial index on `(userId) WHERE readAt IS NULL` for unread counts — verified as `notification_recipient_unread_idx` with `WHERE "notification"."read_at" IS NULL`. Used by `markAllRead` (service.ts:201-212), `markReadByResource` (service.ts:219-237), and `markRead`'s single-row filter (service.ts:181-197).
  - `asset_member` PK covers `(assetId, userId)` — confirmed; used by `getRole` (both columns), `list`/`listMemberUserIds` (assetId prefix), and `AssetService.list`/`search`/`listPage` join + WHERE (assetId + userId).
  - `asset_member` userId-only lookup has an index — `asset_member_user_idx` (declared at schema line 37, created in migration 0030:30). Used by `getAccessibleAssetIds` (authz.ts:10) and `AssetService.list`/`search`/`listPage` when driven from `mediaAssets` join + userId condition.
  - Replicache CVR / client-group tables are indexed on what pull queries by — `replicacheClientGroup` queried only by PK `id` (pull.ts:105, push.ts:84); `replicacheClient` queried by PK `id` (push.ts:70) and by `clientGroupId` (pull.ts:281, covered by `replicache_client_group_idx`).
  - Pagination on list endpoints — not in scope for Task 4; the notifications list currently returns all user rows unbounded (deferred to Task 21 per plan).
  - Appropriate data types — `row_version` integer, `cvr_version` integer, `last_mutation_id` integer, `created_at`/`updated_at`/`read_at`/`expires_at` timestamp, `body` jsonb, text for ids/emails/roles via enum. All correct.
  - Drizzle relational query API check — the pull + service queries use explicit `.innerJoin`/`.leftJoin` rather than `db.query.table.findMany({ with: ... })`. Acceptable here because (a) the queries return custom projections (selected subset of columns with aliases), not the default entity shape relational queries produce, (b) the per-asset scoping uses `inArray(assetId, assetIds)` with a precomputed list — fine for the current cardinality. Not a Task 4 concern; noted for Task 6 / 15 if revisited.
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green, full-turbo cache hit; no source changes).

## Task 5: Verify `$type<>()` on new jsonb columns

Approach: enumerated every `jsonb(` declaration in the schema directory and filtered to columns that are new on this branch (per `git diff main --`). Cross-referenced each against the discriminated union it serializes to in the API layer.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/db/src/schema/notifications.ts:44 — `body: jsonb('body').notNull().default({})` had no `.$type<>()`. Selects of `notifications.body` into `NotificationService.serialize` arrived as Drizzle's default (`unknown`/loose JSON), forcing `row.body as { role: ... }` and `row.body as { fromRole, toRole }` assertions in the serializer (service.ts:133, 139) with no compile-time guarantee that the in-flight row shape matched. Fixed by (a) defining `NotificationBody` as a discriminated union in the schema module — `{ role }` | `{ fromRole, toRole }` | `Record<string, never>` — using literal role strings (`'editor' | 'viewer'`) rather than importing `AssetRole` from `@milkpod/api`, which would invert the package-layering order. (b) Applied `.$type<NotificationBody>()` to the column. The `.notNull().default({})` chain still works because `{}` satisfies the `Record<string, never>` branch of the union. Confirmed via `pnpm build` in `packages/db` and full-repo `pnpm check-types` (8/8 green). Call sites that read `.body` (pull.ts:175, service.ts:161) now receive `NotificationBody` instead of `unknown`; the existing `as` casts in the serializer remain because they narrow one union branch to a specific shape (a type guard by `row.type`), not because the source is untyped. Updating those to `isNotificationOfType`-style guards is a separate refactor, not a Task 5 concern.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/db/src/schema/moments.ts:50 — `scoreBreakdown: jsonb('score_breakdown')` has no `.$type<>()`. Out of scope: the column exists on `main` (verified via `git show main:packages/db/src/schema/moments.ts`) — only `deletedAt`, `rowVersion`, and the matching row-version index are new on this branch. Task 5 is explicitly "new jsonb columns". Noting it as a latent hygiene item; a future sweep or a Task 30 regression sidebar could strongly-type it once a canonical shape is settled (moments/service.ts builds the payload; grep showed no external consumer that would benefit today).
  2. packages/db/src/schema/comments.ts:37 — `evidenceRefs: jsonb('evidence_refs').$type<{ transcriptSegmentIds: string[]; visualSegmentIds: string[] }>()`. Already typed. The new columns on comments.ts on this branch (`deletedAt`, `rowVersion`) are not jsonb — no action.
  3. packages/db/src/schema/asset-members.ts and replicache.ts — no jsonb columns. Nothing to check.
  4. packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts:59 — `type: input.type as NotificationType` is a redundant cast (input is already the discriminated union `RecordInput` whose `type` field is structurally identical to `NotificationType`). Pre-existing; not introduced or caused by this task. Dropped in favor of keeping scope tight.
  5. packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts:91-148 — `serialize(row)` declares `body: unknown` on its public signature despite `notifications.body` now being typed. Intentionally kept permissive: `serialize` is the serialization boundary between DB rows (where a newer-server `type` could write a body shape this deploy doesn't know about) and the public discriminated union (`Notification`). The per-branch `as` inside each `case` is a local narrowing tied to the discriminant check, not an unsafe cast of untyped input. Tightening would force every caller to first narrow — net loss. Kept as-is.

Library improvements made:
  - Used Drizzle's built-in `.$type<>()` on `jsonb()` — the library primitive for type-safe JSON columns. Confirmed the API by reading `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7*/node_modules/drizzle-orm/column-builder.d.ts:167-172` (the official `$type<TType>(): $Type<this, TType>` declaration with inline JSDoc example `json('details').$type<UserDetails>().notNull()`). No hand-rolled alternative (e.g. `as NotificationBody` on the select, or a runtime Zod reshaper) was written — the Drizzle primitive covers the compile-time need and the DB already enforces JSON validity at the storage layer.
  - The NotificationBody discriminated union type lives in the schema module (co-located with NotificationType) rather than in `@milkpod/api`, preserving the package-layering direction: `packages/db` has no dependency on `packages/api`. Exported via the `schemas.ts` barrel (unchanged — `export * from './schema/notifications'` picks up the new type automatically).

Checklist verification (per `review-prompt.md` Queries & Data Access + TypeScript patterns + Library Leverage):
  - `$type<>()` used on jsonb columns for type-safe access — notifications.body now typed; comments.evidenceRefs was already typed; replicache + asset-members have no jsonb; moments.scoreBreakdown pre-existing and out of scope.
  - Discriminated union over notification type — NotificationBody is a true TS discriminated union (three branches), matching NotificationType's three discriminants one-for-one. Adding a new NotificationType literal forces a compile error in any `switch (row.type)` that doesn't add a branch (e.g. service.ts:128-148) AND forces the body union to be extended — two-sided contract.
  - No `any` types introduced — NotificationBody uses literal string types (`'editor' | 'viewer'`), `Record<string, never>`, and structural object types. `unknown` retained only on `serialize(row: { body: unknown })` as a conscious boundary choice (see issues-noted #5).
  - Appropriate data types — `body jsonb DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb NOT NULL` matches the `Record<string, never>` branch; `row_version integer`, `read_at timestamp` unchanged. No migration generated / needed: `.$type<>()` is a TS-only refinement, not a DDL change (verified by running `pnpm --filter @milkpod/db build` — no migration drift warnings).
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green across @milkpod/{db,ai,auth,api}, server, web). @milkpod/db built first, then downstream consumers resolved the new type from `dist/` per CLAUDE.md workflow.

## Task 6: Audit pull.ts for CVR determinism and per-user visibility filter

Approach: audited pull.ts + cvr.ts + authz.ts against three criteria — (a) non-deterministic sources inside CVR/patch construction, (b) ORDER BY stability on every query whose output order flows into the patch or persisted snapshot, (c) visibility filter correctness for the initial (cold) pull. Grepped for `Date.now|Math.random|new Date(` under modules/replicache/; inspected every `.select()` for ORDER BY; re-read the WHERE clauses against authz.ts.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:142-161, 167-184, 276-282 — None of the three hot-path queries (moments, comments, notifications) and the clients LMID query had an ORDER BY. Iteration order of result rows feeds directly into the patch array (`patch.push` in a `for (const m of currentMoments)` loop) AND into the snapshot's insertion order (`nextMoments[m.id] = ...`), which is JSON-serialized and read back on the next pull. Two pulls on identical (cookie, clientGroupID, userID, DB state) could therefore produce different patch array orders, breaking the task's "deterministic output" requirement. The snapshot keyed by id is lookup-insensitive to order, but (a) the patch array order IS observed by Replicache's client-side JSON log / debugger diffs, and (b) `Object.entries(prevSnapshot.moments)` in the del-loops iterates insertion order, which only stays stable if the write side is stable. Fixed by adding `.orderBy(asc(assetMoments.id))`, `.orderBy(asc(assetComments.id))`, `.orderBy(asc(notifications.id))`, and `.orderBy(asc(replicacheClient.id))`. Added `asc` to the drizzle-orm import. A one-line comment in the moments section explains the determinism rationale so future contributors don't drop the ORDER BY as "unnecessary". Verified API signature via `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7*/node_modules/drizzle-orm/pg-core/query-builders/select.d.ts:527-541` — `.orderBy(...columns)` accepts `asc(col)`/`desc(col)` wrappers exactly as used.
  2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:247-248 — `value: n as unknown as Record<string, unknown>` is a double-cast through `unknown`, the specific anti-pattern flagged by review-prompt.md TypeScript §2 ("No `as unknown as T`"). Notification is a tagged union with a fixed set of flat + nested-object fields, so a structural serializer is cheap. Fixed by adding `serializeNotification(n: Notification): Record<string, unknown>` alongside the existing `serializeMoment`/`serializeComment` helpers — fresh object literal, no cast, TypeScript widens literal → `Record<string, unknown>` via the fresh-literal rule (same mechanism that makes `serializeMoment`'s return assignable). Fields enumerated explicitly so adding a new Notification type / field forces a compile error here (see isNotificationOfType in types.ts:144 for the discriminant helper).
  3. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:210-214, 242, 264-267 — `nextSnapshot.notifications![n.id]` and `nextSnapshot.notifications![id]` used `!` non-null assertions on an optional field even though `nextSnapshot` was locally initialized with `notifications: {}`. Flagged by review-prompt.md TypeScript §2 ("No unjustified non-null assertions"). `notifications` is optional in `CVRSnapshot` for backwards-compat with pre-deploy Redis snapshots (valid), but the local `!` assertion leaks that concern into every write site. Fixed by refactoring `nextSnapshot` assembly: declare three local `Record<string, ...>` accumulators (`nextMoments`, `nextComments`, `nextNotifications`), populate them in the put-loops, then build `nextSnapshot` once at the end as a single object literal. Same pattern `currentLmids` already used for the clients map — this harmonizes the style. Zero `!` assertions in the final code.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:138-184 — Queries inside `db().transaction` are serialized by the underlying pg/postgres-js driver even when wrapped in Promise.all (single connection per tx). `Promise.all` would not speed them up here, would lose the per-query error localization, and would obscure read-order intent. Kept sequential `await`s — correct pattern for the tx-per-pull model.
  2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/authz.ts:6-12 — `getAccessibleAssetIds` returns assetIds in indeterminate order. That's fed to `inArray(assetMoments.assetId, assetIds)` in the WHERE; Postgres treats IN-list order as irrelevant (sets, not sequences) so result order depends only on the moments query's ORDER BY. The assetIds order leaks nowhere else. No need to sort. Task 8 will re-audit authz.ts for coverage, so leaving the query shape as-is.
  3. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:181-184 — Notifications are NOT filtered by `assetIds`. That's correct for the bell: a user dropped from an asset still receives the `asset.member.removed` notification (membership revoked → no longer in asset_member → notification would be hidden if scoped by assetIds). Scoping only on `recipientId = userId` is the right initial-pull filter for user-owned notifications. Cross-referenced with NotificationService.record (service.ts:49-68) — notifications are always written with the recipient as the read-access key, never the asset's member list.
  4. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:152-154 — `prevSnapshot` cold-sync fallback has `notifications: {}`. Unused except for the `prevSnapshot.notifications ?? {}` line (line 271) reading it. Could drop from the literal (since `?? {}` catches undefined), but keeping it documents the expected shape. Minor, not worth touching.
  5. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/cvr.ts:30-40 — `notifications?` and `clients?` are optional in `CVRSnapshot`. Kept optional because (a) Redis-persisted snapshots from a prior schema (before notifications were added) may still be within the 12-hour TTL window at deploy time, (b) the `?? {}` fallback in pull.ts is the intentional boundary, (c) making them required would force either an eager migration or a runtime cast. Local `Record<string, ...>` accumulators now eliminate the read-side `!` assertions (see fix #3) without touching the type. No change.
  6. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:79 — `createdAt: m.createdAt instanceof Date ? m.createdAt.toISOString() : m.createdAt` — the ternary handles both typed `Date` (from a direct tx query) and serialized-string (from a subsequent JSON round-trip). Not a non-determinism source; the branch is taken on row shape, not on runtime clock. Confirmed.

Library improvements made:
  - Used Drizzle's `asc()` builder for ORDER BY rather than `sql\`\"id\" asc\`` raw templating. Verified the primitive in `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7*/node_modules/drizzle-orm/pg-core/query-builders/select.d.ts:540-541` — `.orderBy(...columns: (PgColumn | SQL | SQL.Aliased)[])` signature accepts `asc(col)` directly. No raw SQL needed.
  - Replaced `as unknown as Record<string, unknown>` with an explicit serializer fn (fresh literal) — same pattern as existing `serializeMoment`/`serializeComment`. Preserves TypeScript's structural-type narrowing for the call site and makes schema-evolution compile-visible.
  - Refactored snapshot assembly to use three local `Record<string, ...>` accumulators, eliminating all `!` assertions. This is the pattern Drizzle's own result mappers use (accumulate in a typed Map/object, assemble at the end) — matches the "extract shared patterns into typed utilities" review guideline.

Checklist verification (per `review-prompt.md` Queries & Data Access + TypeScript patterns + Library Leverage + Security):
  - No `Date.now()`, `Math.random()`, `new Date()` inside CVR/patch construction — grepped `packages/api/src/modules/replicache` and confirmed all `new Date()` hits are in push.ts / server-mutators.ts (write path — Task 7 scope), not pull.ts / cvr.ts. Pull handler is pure derivation from DB state + prev snapshot.
  - Every ORDER BY has a stable tiebreak — the four queries with loop/patch-affecting output (moments, comments, notifications, replicacheClient) now ORDER BY the primary id column (text ids, unique by PK). ids are single-column PKs so the PK ORDER BY is itself a total order — no additional tiebreak needed.
  - Initial pull visibility filter — `getAccessibleAssetIds(userId)` returns the user's `asset_member` rows' asset ids. Moments/comments filtered via `inArray(assetId, assetIds)` (nested scoping: user → accessible assets → their moments/comments). Notifications scoped via `eq(recipientId, userId)` (user-direct scoping). No global firehose. Verified by reading authz.ts:1-12 and pull.ts:164-213.
  - Cold sync emits `clear` — confirmed at pull.ts:247 (`if (isColdSync) patch.push({ op: 'clear' });`). isColdSync is true iff `prev == null` which is true iff (cookie is null) OR (cvr cache miss/corrupt). Matches Replicache protocol expectation.
  - Del emission on visibility loss — covered by the `!nextMoments[id]` / `!nextComments[id]` / `!nextNotifications[id]` loops at lines 284-299. A revoked membership removes the asset from getAccessibleAssetIds → its moments/comments drop from `currentMoments` → they're absent in nextMoments → del ops are emitted. Task 9 deep-audits this; today's refactor did not regress it.
  - No `any` types introduced — serializeNotification fully typed (Notification → Record<string, unknown>). imports add `asc`, `CVRRow`, `NotificationCVRRow` — all exported from existing modules, no new type.
  - No unjustified `!` — all removed via local-accumulator refactor.
  - No `as T` / `as unknown as T` — serializeNotification replaces the one cast; no new casts added. Verified via `rg 'as unknown as|as Record<' packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts` → zero hits.
  - Parameterized queries / ORM builders — all queries use Drizzle builders; only raw SQL is the `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(${lockKey}))` call which uses `sql\`\` template tag with `${lockKey}` interpolation → parameterized (confirmed by Drizzle docs: `sql\`\${x}\`` → `$1` bind, not string concat). No injection surface.
  - Appropriate data types — id columns text, rowVersion integer, createdAt timestamp. PatchOp value is `Record<string, unknown>` which matches Replicache's ReadonlyJSONObject expectation.
  - No N+1 — still single query per entity type (moments, comments, notifications, authors, clients). Author hydration batched via `inArray(userTable.id, [...authorIds])`. ORDER BY does not change query shape.
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green). @milkpod/api and downstream (server, web) rebuilt; no regressions.

## Task 7: Wrap push mutations in a transaction; verify mutation-ID monotonicity

Approach: audited push.ts against the three plan criteria — (a) single outer transaction wrapping LMID reads + mutator writes + LMID advances, (b) duplicate-mutation dedup semantics (<= skip silently), (c) LMID advance on mutator failure so the client rebases rather than re-queuing forever. Cross-referenced the LMID upsert against Drizzle's `setWhere` support and verified nested transactions use savepoints.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:133-161 (pre-edit) — `handlePush` was NOT wrapped in a single transaction. Each mutation opened its own `db().transaction(...)`, and `getLMID` + the error-path `advanceLMID` ran on the raw pool connection (no tx at all). This created a monotonicity race window: two concurrent pushes from the same clientID could each `getLMID → 5`, both see `mutation.id = 6 > 5`, both enter separate transactions, and the later-to-commit would overwrite the LMID with whatever value it had locally. If two requests arrived in reverse order (push2 with id=7 committed first, push1 with id=6 committed second), the LMID would regress from 7 to 6 — Replicache's client would then re-queue 7, and the server could either dedup or apply twice depending on mutator idempotence. Fixed by wrapping the entire push handler (clientGroup bind, mutation loop, all LMID reads and writes) inside a single `db().transaction(...)` with per-mutation savepoints for isolation. See fixes #2 + #3 below for the monotonicity guard and savepoint strategy.
  2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:53-64, 149-160 (pre-edit) — `advanceLMID`'s `onConflictDoUpdate({ set: { lastMutationId: newId } })` had no monotonicity guard. Even within a single transaction, an out-of-order LMID write would regress the row. Fixed by adding Drizzle's `setWhere: sql\`${replicacheClient.lastMutationId} < ${newId}\`` — the UPDATE only applies when the new id strictly advances the current value. Verified the API in `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7*/node_modules/drizzle-orm/pg-core/query-builders/insert.d.ts:61-68` (`PgInsertOnConflictDoUpdateConfig.setWhere?: SQL`) — it compiles to a standard Postgres `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE ... WHERE` and keeps the upsert atomic. Unified the single `advanceLMID` helper to take `tx` as its first arg so both the error-path and success-path advances run inside the outer transaction with the monotonicity guard applied consistently.
  3. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:133-161 (pre-edit) — No isolation between mutations. With per-mutation transactions, a crash in mutation 3 would roll back only mutation 3. But in a single outer transaction, any throw would roll back ALL prior mutations in the batch — violating Replicache's rebase contract (the client expects mutations with advanced LMIDs to be either applied or explicitly rejected, not silently reverted). Fixed by wrapping each mutator call in a nested `tx.transaction(async (subTx) => { ... })` — Drizzle's nested transaction issues `SAVEPOINT spN` / `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT spN` / `RELEASE SAVEPOINT spN` under the hood (verified in `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7*/node_modules/drizzle-orm/node-postgres/session.js:206-220`). If a mutator throws, only its writes roll back; the outer tx stays alive, and the subsequent LMID advance commits with the rest of the batch.
  4. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:170-184 (pre-edit) — `console.error('mutator crashed', name, err instanceof Error ? err.message : err)`. The fallback branch logs the raw `err` (which is `unknown` from the catch binding). Per review-prompt.md §Common Patterns #1 (real-world finding: `err` can contain connection strings, SQL state, stack traces), changed to `String(err)` for the non-Error path. Same pattern applied to the user-poke catch block at what is now line 227-230.
  5. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:75-99 (pre-edit) — ClientGroup bind/create (the trust boundary check) ran on the raw pool connection, outside any transaction. Fixed by moving it inside the outer `db().transaction(...)` so the check and any subsequent LMID writes in this push see consistent state. If the clientGroup creation races another push, `onConflictDoNothing` absorbs the duplicate. Forbidden returns early with a typed discriminant (`{ forbidden: true }`) that escapes the transaction without committing any work.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:168-172 — The `serverMutators[mutatorName] as (tx, args: unknown, ctx) => Promise<void>` cast cannot be eliminated without a per-mutator switch statement (8 cases). The args-per-name correlation across the MutatorName union is not expressible in TypeScript: `parsed.data` is a union of all possible args shapes, `serverMutators[mutatorName]` is a union of all mutator signatures, and TS cannot correlate the two branches on the same literal name without an explicit discriminant. The Zod schema validation one line above (`schema.safeParse(mutation.args)` with `schema = mutatorArgsSchemas[mutatorName]`) provides the runtime guarantee that parsed.data matches the target mutator's args — this is the right boundary for the cast. Kept with a justifying comment.
  2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts:38-48 — `requireEditor` uses `AssetMemberService.isEditor(assetId, userId)` which calls `db()`, not the transaction. Inside a mutator now wrapped in a savepoint of the outer tx, this auth check runs on a SEPARATE pool connection. Consequences: (a) the check doesn't see uncommitted writes from earlier mutations in this push (fine — none of our mutators modify membership), (b) the check could see a concurrent revocation that committed during this tx (also fine — at worst we apply one extra write before the next pull rebases). Out of scope for Task 7 per the plan's own task ordering — Task 8 (authz.ts coverage audit) is where this will be revisited. Leaving as-is preserves the current service API.
  3. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:204 — `(parsed.data as { assetId: string }).assetId` is a structural cast that could be replaced with a branded discriminant on the args-schemas side. Not worth the refactor — every non-user-scoped mutator's args includes assetId by contract (enforced by the zod schemas in packages/sync/src/mutators/*.ts lines 20-35, 57-64, 86-90 for moments; equivalent for comments), the USER_SCOPED_MUTATORS set explicitly partitions the ones without, and this call site is the single consumer. A tighter type would require adding a tag to every args schema. Kept with the existing narrow.
  4. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:123-156 — No gap detection in LMID progression. If `mutation.id > lastMutationId + 1` (client jumped forward), we silently advance to mutation.id, skipping the intermediate mutations. Per Replicache's push protocol, mutations are submitted in strict order per clientID and a gap would indicate a client-side bug. Current behavior (advance to latest, log nothing, trust the protocol) matches Rocicorp's reference row-version strategy implementation. If we later add observability, this would be a good place to emit a counter.
  5. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:151 — `getLMID` runs once per mutation, inside the tx. For a batch of 100 mutations this is 100 SELECTs in series. Acceptable because (a) pushes are small (Task 12 will cap at 100), (b) the SELECT is on `replicacheClient.id` PK so each query is an index lookup <1 ms, (c) the read must be inside the tx anyway to be consistent with concurrent writes. A future optimization: fetch all LMIDs for the client group in one query before the loop, then track progress in a Map. Not worth the complexity today.
  6. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:215-232 — Pokes fan out AFTER the outer transaction commits (post-`await db().transaction(...)`). Task 10 explicitly audits this; verified here as unchanged and correct. No poke is emitted inside the transaction body.

Library improvements made:
  - Replaced hand-rolled monotonicity logic with Drizzle's `onConflictDoUpdate({ setWhere })` primitive. Without `setWhere`, the alternative would have been a raw SQL `UPDATE ... WHERE last_mutation_id < $1` + separate INSERT-on-not-exists, or an application-level read-compare-write that doesn't survive concurrent transactions. `setWhere` compiles to `ON CONFLICT ("id") DO UPDATE SET last_mutation_id = excluded.last_mutation_id, last_modified = excluded.last_modified WHERE replicache_client.last_mutation_id < $newId` — a single atomic statement that Postgres guarantees is linearizable under serializable isolation.
  - Used Drizzle's nested-transaction primitive (`tx.transaction(...)`) rather than hand-rolled `tx.execute(sql\`savepoint X\`)` / `rollback to savepoint X` / `release savepoint X`. Verified the implementation at `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7*/node_modules/drizzle-orm/node-postgres/session.js:206-220` — Drizzle auto-generates unique savepoint names (`sp${nestedIndex+1}`), handles both the happy path (RELEASE) and error path (ROLLBACK TO + rethrow), and maintains the outer tx's liveness correctly. No chance of savepoint name collision or leaked savepoints.
  - Unified `advanceLMID` into a single tx-aware helper (was previously duplicated logic between inline tx.insert + the helper that used the pool `db()`). One call site, one monotonicity invariant — harder to skip by accident.

Checklist verification (per `review-prompt.md` Queries & Data Access + Security + Resilience + TypeScript patterns + Library Leverage + Common Patterns):
  - Transactions for atomic operations — entire push handler (clientGroup bind, each mutation's writes, LMID advance) runs in a single `db().transaction(...)`. Per-mutation isolation via nested transactions (savepoints). Verified by reading push.ts:97-211.
  - No duplicate mutations errored — `if (mutation.id <= lastMutationId) continue;` at push.ts:152-156. Silently skipped per Replicache's retry-produces-duplicates design invariant.
  - LMID monotonicity — `setWhere: sql\`${replicacheClient.lastMutationId} < ${newId}\`` on every upsert (helper at push.ts:70-74, used from all call sites). Postgres enforces this atomically via the ON CONFLICT UPDATE predicate.
  - Error path still advances LMID — push.ts:196 (`advanceLMID` after the try/catch) runs regardless of whether the savepoint rolled back. Client rebases via next pull instead of re-queuing forever. Task note: Replicache does not yet support "error record" metadata per LMID (that's a v2 protocol extension); today's behavior matches Rocicorp's reference server.
  - No raw SQL injection — `sql\`${replicacheClient.lastMutationId} < ${newId}\`` uses Drizzle's parameterized template tag (verified elsewhere: `${x}` binds `$N`, not string-concat). `newId` is a TypeScript `number`, narrowed from `mutation.id` which was validated as `t.Number()` at the Elysia route (index.ts:52).
  - No sensitive data in logs — err fallback branch now uses `String(err)` instead of raw `err` (push.ts:187, 229). Matches review-prompt.md Common Patterns #1.
  - No secrets in error responses — the only return to the client is `{}` (success) or `{ forbidden: true }` → 403 at the route (index.ts:38-42); no upstream errors bubble.
  - Proper HTTP status codes — 200 on success, 403 on forbidden clientGroup bind. (The rest is Elysia's default.)
  - Idempotency — mutation.id dedup + `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the create mutators (server-mutators.ts:72, 133) + `setWhere` monotonicity guard together make the push handler idempotent under retry.
  - Input validation — Elysia TypeBox validates the push body shape at index.ts:47-58 (pushVersion, clientGroupID length, mutations array shape). Per-mutation args validated at push.ts:139-140 against the `mutatorArgsSchemas[name]` zod schema. Unknown mutator names are logged + LMID-dropped (push.ts:124-131) rather than crashing the batch.
  - Auth / authorization — route is `.use(authMacro)` + `auth: true` (index.ts:11, 46); userId comes from the authed session and is passed into every mutator ctx. ClientGroup userId pinning enforced at push.ts:106-107.
  - Rate limiting — not in this task's scope. Task 13 covers rate-limiter registration for /api/replicache/pull and /api/replicache/push.
  - No non-null assertions — zero `!` in push.ts after the refactor (verified: `grep '!' packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts` returns only logical-not operators like `if (!group)` and `if (!isKnownMutator(...))`).
  - No `any` types — one justified `any` alias `type DbTx = any` for the Drizzle tx handle (already existed in server-mutators.ts), with a comment explaining why (driver-version variance). Not introduced by this task.
  - No `as` / `as unknown as T` — one structural cast `as { assetId: string }` on parsed.data, one dispatch cast on `serverMutators[mutatorName]`. Both justified in-code with comments (see issues-noted #1, #3).
  - Exhaustive switch — no switch statements in push.ts; the dispatch is by keyed record (mutatorName → runner). Adding a new mutator name requires adding to both `serverMutators` (server-mutators.ts) AND `mutatorArgsSchemas` + `clientMutators` (packages/sync/src/mutators/index.ts), and any name in MutatorName that isn't in USER_SCOPED_MUTATORS falls to the assetId-poke branch — which will throw at runtime if the args schema lacks an assetId field. Could be made compile-visible with a branded args type; noted in issues-noted #3.
  - Library leverage — `setWhere` (Drizzle), nested `tx.transaction` (Drizzle savepoints), `onConflictDoNothing`/`onConflictDoUpdate` (Drizzle), `safeParse` (Zod). No hand-rolled alternatives.
  - Poke emission AFTER commit — push.ts:215-232 runs after `await db().transaction(...)` resolves. No poke is emitted inside the transaction body.
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green across @milkpod/{env,sync,db,ai,auth,api}, server, web). No regressions.

## Task 8: Audit authz.ts coverage for read (pull) and write (push)

Approach: enumerated every synced entity (moments, comments, notifications — nothing else is published to the client via pull.ts), mapped each to its read-filter (pull) and write-check (push), and looked for divergence between the two sides. Confirmed every mutator in `serverMutators` re-checks permissions on the server before writing. Traced `requireEditor`'s dependency graph (lived in server-mutators.ts, delegates to `AssetMemberService.isEditor`, which calls `getRole`) to verify there was no parallel implementation.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/authz.ts (pre-edit was 12 lines) — authz.ts exposed only the read-side helper (`getAccessibleAssetIds`). The write-side auth primitive (`requireEditor` + `MutatorForbiddenError`) lived in server-mutators.ts, and push.ts imported the error class from there too. Plan wording explicitly calls for authz.ts to cover both sides: "don't let pull/push reimplement checks divergently". Fixed by consolidating into authz.ts: moved `MutatorForbiddenError` and `requireEditor` from server-mutators.ts into authz.ts, and documented the full entity matrix (moment / comment / notification × pull-read / push-write) in the module header so future contributors see at a glance which authz primitive applies where. Updated server-mutators.ts to `import { requireEditor } from './authz'` (dropping its own `AssetMemberService` import — the service is now reached transitively through authz) and updated push.ts to `import { MutatorForbiddenError } from './authz'` (previously from './server-mutators'). Net effect: one file, one module, one set of rules — pull.ts and server-mutators.ts can no longer drift because they both reach through the same choke point. `requireEditor` still delegates to `AssetMemberService.isEditor` so the role-threshold definition still lives in one place (the membership service); authz.ts is the Replicache-specific facade over it.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:182 — Notifications are filtered inline via `eq(notifications.recipientId, userId)`, not through an authz.ts helper. Considered adding a `notificationFilter(userId)` SQL-fragment helper. Rejected because (a) the filter IS a single column comparison expressed in Drizzle's query DSL, not a decision that needs a separate query — wrapping it adds indirection without eliminating the call-site repetition; (b) the identical `eq(notifications.recipientId, ctx.userId)` clause also appears in both notification mutators (server-mutators.ts:158 and 180) — so the rule is applied in three places with the same literal-column predicate, and divergence would be a visible code-review issue (not a subtle behavior delta). Documented this design choice explicitly in the authz.ts header block.
  2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/authz.ts:62 — `requireEditor` still calls `AssetMemberService.isEditor` which uses `db()` (the pool), not the push transaction. Means the auth check runs on a SEPARATE connection from the mutator's writes. This is safe TODAY because no mutator modifies asset membership — a user's editor status is invariant over a single push batch. If a future mutator adds or revokes memberships, the check must be revised to accept the tx and query inside it (otherwise prior mutations in the same push could "reauthorize" a user whose membership was just removed, or vice versa). Documented the invariant in authz.ts:61-65. No code change today.
  3. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/authz.ts:48-53 — `getAccessibleAssetIds` queries `assetMembers` directly rather than going through an `AssetMemberService.listUserAssetIds(userId)` method. Considered adding the method to the service for consistency. Rejected because (a) no other call site in the codebase needs "all assets for user X" as a distinct operation (`AssetService.list` uses a JOIN + WHERE against `assetMembers`, not a preliminary id fetch), (b) adding the method solely to reach through it from authz.ts is indirection without benefit. If a second consumer appears, extract then. Kept the direct query.
  4. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts — `notificationMarkRead` / `notificationMarkAllRead` enforce ownership via the WHERE clause (`eq(notifications.recipientId, ctx.userId)`) rather than an explicit `requireOwner(notificationId, userId)` check. This is the stronger pattern: the ownership check and the write are atomic in a single SQL statement — there's no TOCTOU window between "verify I own this" and "modify it". Prefer this over a separate SELECT + UPDATE that would require a transaction or optimistic-lock to stay consistent. No helper abstraction needed.
  5. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:164 — moments/comments filter uses `inArray(assetId, assetIds)` where `assetIds` comes from `getAccessibleAssetIds`. A sufficiently determined attacker who spoofs `assetIds` cannot — the function is called server-side with the session's `userId`, and the only path from route → handler → filter is `handlePull(user.id, body)` where `user.id` is derived from the auth middleware. Confirmed at index.ts:14 and push.ts:88 that client-supplied body fields are never substituted for the authed user id.
  6. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/index.ts:38 — push route returns `{ forbidden: true }` as a 403 when the clientGroup is bound to a different user. This is a trust-boundary check (not content-level authz), verified at push.ts:106-107. It catches the case where an attacker who has a valid session steals another user's clientGroupID and tries to push into it. The check + rejection is in the right layer.

Library improvements made:
  - Reused the existing Drizzle patterns (no library change needed): `eq`, `inArray` for WHERE clauses; `AssetMemberService.isEditor` as the canonical role-threshold query. Consolidation was a pure TypeScript module-boundary refactor — the library surface didn't need to change. Verified Drizzle's `db().select().from().where()` call pattern in `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7*/node_modules/drizzle-orm/pg-core/db.d.ts:281` (`transaction<T>(transaction: (tx: PgTransaction<...>) => Promise<T>, config?: PgTransactionConfig): Promise<T>`) — the tx handle has the same query-builder surface as `db()` itself, which is why the existing `requireEditor` using `db()` rather than tx is a behavioral choice (cross-connection, not a library limitation). Noted in issues-noted #2 for future follow-up.

Checklist verification (per `review-prompt.md` Security + Queries & Data Access + Code Quality + TypeScript patterns + Library Leverage):
  - Auth and authorization checks on every endpoint, not just the router level — verified by enumeration:
      - pull.ts: user-id comes from auth middleware (index.ts:14, `user.id`), clientGroup → user binding check at pull.ts:105-118, moments/comments visibility filter via `getAccessibleAssetIds(userId)`, notifications via `recipientId = userId`.
      - push.ts: user-id from auth middleware (index.ts:37), clientGroup → user binding check at push.ts:106-107, every mutator invokes `requireEditor` (moments/comments) or relies on an atomic-WHERE ownership filter (notifications).
      - Every mutator in serverMutators re-checks ownership — all 6 asset-scoped mutators call `await requireEditor(args.assetId, ctx.userId);` as the first statement; both notification mutators include `eq(notifications.recipientId, ctx.userId)` in the UPDATE WHERE.
  - Input validation on every route — pull.ts route has TypeBox validator at index.ts:25-31 (pullVersion literal, clientGroupID length, cookie nullable number). push.ts route at index.ts:47-61 (pushVersion literal, clientGroupID length, mutations array with per-mutation Object schema). Per-mutation args validated against mutatorArgsSchemas[name] at push.ts:139-140 before dispatch.
  - No sensitive data in logs — MutatorForbiddenError construction uses its own message string (`User ${userId} is not an editor of asset ${assetId}`) — ids are not secrets and don't leak tokens or PII. push.ts catch block uses `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)` (verified at push.ts:187).
  - No secrets in error responses — 403 response at index.ts:38 returns `{ message: 'Client group is bound to another user' }`. No database internals, no ACL rule details, no stack traces bubbled.
  - Proper HTTP status codes — 403 for cross-user clientGroup attempt (both routes). 200 for mutator-forbidden (per design: push response is per-mutation via advanced LMID + no patch; not a route-level 403). Matches Replicache's protocol.
  - No `any` types introduced by this task — `DbTx = any` alias stayed in server-mutators.ts (see Task 7 notes for justification — driver-version variance); `requireEditor` is fully typed (`assetId: string, userId: string` → `Promise<void>`). The `MutatorForbiddenError` class extends `Error` with no type shortcuts.
  - Exhaustive switch statements — no switch over the mutator union; dispatch is by record lookup (`serverMutators[mutatorName]`). Enforced at the type level: adding a mutator requires extending `clientMutators`, `serverMutators`, and `mutatorArgsSchemas` in lockstep (packages/sync/src/mutators/index.ts and packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts). Missing from any of the three produces a TS error at push.ts.
  - No magic numbers — clientGroupID min/max enforced at the TypeBox level (1, 200) in index.ts; push mutations array has no hard cap yet (Task 12 will add one).
  - Reuse existing utilities — `requireEditor` uses `AssetMemberService.isEditor` (canonical role check, shared with REST routes at asset-members/index.ts:21,46,68). No duplicate implementation.
  - Extract duplicated blocks — merged by this task: `MutatorForbiddenError` + `requireEditor` now live in one module (authz.ts) and are imported by both sides. Zero duplication.
  - Coverage matrix for every synced entity — documented in-code as the authz.ts header block:
      - moment: pull via `getAccessibleAssetIds` ∋ assetId, push via `requireEditor(assetId, userId)`
      - comment: pull via `getAccessibleAssetIds` ∋ assetId, push via `requireEditor(assetId, userId)`
      - notification: pull via `recipientId = userId`, push via atomic-WHERE `recipientId = userId`
      - assets / asset_members: NOT published via sync, authz enforced at REST (documented).
  - Library leverage — no library primitive was missed. The consolidation was a module-boundary refactor, not a library-level change.
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green across @milkpod/{env,sync,db,ai,auth,api}, server, web). No regressions. @milkpod/api and downstream re-ran due to source changes; @milkpod/{db,sync,ai,auth} cache-hit.

## Task 9: Emit `del` patches on visibility loss

Approach: traced every way a row can leave the user's visible set (physical delete, soft delete via dismissedAt/deletedAt, membership revocation) and verified each converges on the same CVR-diff mechanism in pull.ts. Cross-checked that the del loop reconstructs the correct Replicache key for each entity (uses `row.a` for asset-scoped prefixes, bare id for user-scoped notifications). Re-read AssetMemberService.removeMember to confirm a Replicache poke fires post-commit so the revoked user pulls immediately. Ran `pnpm check-types` to verify the `DbHandle` union type plays nicely with the existing tx handle.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/authz.ts:46-52 + packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:164 — `getAccessibleAssetIds` ran on `db()` (the pool) from inside the pull transaction, opening a narrow race window: if a membership revocation committed between the membership read (pool connection) and the subsequent moments/comments reads (tx connection), the current pull would return the revoked asset's moments/comments as "current" — delaying the `del` ops by one pull cycle. Fixed by adding a second parameter to `getAccessibleAssetIds` typed as `DbHandle = ReturnType<typeof db> | Parameters<Parameters<ReturnType<typeof db>['transaction']>[0]>[0]` (a union that accepts either the pool or a tx), defaulting to `db()` for backwards-compat. `pull.ts` now passes its tx, so the membership read is scheduled on the same connection as the moment/comment queries. The remaining READ-COMMITTED race (a revocation could still commit between two separate SELECTs within the tx) is self-healing: the next pull picks up the revocation via getAccessibleAssetIds and emits the correct `del` ops. Documented this tradeoff inline on both files. Alternative considered and rejected: `INNER JOIN assetMembers` on each entity query — would fully eliminate the cross-statement race per query, but requires `getTableColumns(...)` projection to keep the flat Moment/Comment result shape, and still leaves the moments-vs-comments cross-query race without repeatable-read isolation. The tx-handle change is the minimal, principled fix that improves consistency without introducing transaction-isolation complexity.
  2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:283-301 — Del loop had a one-line comment ("Deletions: rows present in prev snapshot but not current") that didn't enumerate the scenarios it covers. Expanded to a block comment documenting all three paths (physical delete, soft delete via dismissedAt/deletedAt, membership revocation), how each manifests as "absent from next snapshot", and why `row.a` is necessary (to reconstruct `moment/<assetId>/<id>` keys after the user has already lost access to the asset). Also called out that notifications are user-scoped (no assetId segment in the key). This makes the CVR-diff correctness argument visible to future readers without them needing to re-audit the whole pull handler.
  3. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:158-170 — Added a block comment before the `getAccessibleAssetIds` call noting that assets themselves are NOT published via sync (per packages/sync/src/keys.ts — only moment/comment/notification are in `SYNC_ENTITIES`). Prevents a future contributor from thinking "why don't we emit a `del` for the asset too?" — the answer is that REST cache invalidation handles asset-level disappearance, not the CVR diff.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:283-301 — The del loop correctly handles the case where `prevSnapshot.moments` contains a row whose asset the user no longer has access to. `row.a` is carried in the CVRRow record specifically so the key can be reconstructed from the prev snapshot alone, without re-querying the asset. This is the full purpose of the `a` field on CVRRow — verified by reading cvr.ts:5-10 and confirming it's populated at snapshot write time (pull.ts:254, 265).
  2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:195-216 — Notifications have NO soft-delete column (no dismissedAt, no deletedAt on the notifications schema) and no mutator currently hard-deletes them (grepped `packages/api/src` for `delete(notifications)` / `DELETE FROM notification` — zero hits). In practice, notifications accumulate until the next pull reconfigures the recipientId filter (never happens — recipientId is immutable) or the server's `NotificationService.serialize` returns null for a row (only when the row's discriminant `type` doesn't match a known variant — a forward-compat scenario for a newer-server writing a type this deploy doesn't know). The del branch for notifications is therefore future-proofing; it would fire on (a) a future manual hard-delete path or (b) schema evolution where a row falls out of the known-types union. Keeping it correct by the same "absent from next" rule.
  3. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:164 — The `assetIds` variable still flows into `inArray(assetMoments.assetId, assetIds)` rather than being replaced by an `INNER JOIN assetMembers` predicate. The JOIN approach would eliminate the within-tx cross-statement race (a membership revoke committing between the moments SELECT and the comments SELECT). Rejected because (a) requires `getTableColumns(assetMoments)` projection to preserve the flat Moment result shape, adding boilerplate, (b) the remaining race is extremely narrow (sub-millisecond window on the same pool connection), (c) self-heals in the next pull, (d) Replicache's design tolerates eventual consistency across pull boundaries. The tx-handle change (fix #1) captures the practical benefit without the complexity. Documented this tradeoff in authz.ts's function docblock.
  4. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/cvr.ts — No changes required. The Redis snapshot store is authz-agnostic; it persists whatever pull.ts writes. The CVR diff logic (which entities to del) lives in pull.ts, not cvr.ts. Re-read cvr.ts:1-85 to confirm: pure KV-over-Redis with JSON codec and 12h TTL — no state machine concerns, no authz concerns.
  5. Same READ-COMMITTED race exists in authz.ts's `requireEditor` for push (documented in Task 8's own notes and in authz.ts:60-65). Out of scope for Task 9 (write side, not visibility-loss). Left as-is; Task 8's comment explicitly acknowledges the current behavior is safe because no mutator modifies membership.

Library improvements made:
  - Used Drizzle's `Parameters<Parameters<ReturnType<typeof db>['transaction']>[0]>[0]` pattern to derive the tx type automatically from the transaction signature — same idiom as packages/api/src/modules/podcasts/service.ts:15. This tracks the Drizzle version (0.44.7) without a manual import chain through `NodePgDatabase` / `PgTransaction<...>` generic parameters, and stays correct through driver upgrades.
  - Wrapped the tx + pool acceptance in a named `DbHandle` union type so future authz helpers can follow the same pattern. Exported implicitly via the function signature; no need to promote it to a public type.
  - No library primitive was missed for the del-emission logic itself. Replicache's row-version-based pull strategy (Rocicorp's reference) is exactly this "diff prev-snapshot against current-set" mechanism — cross-referenced with the design intent in sync-engine-plan.md and with the `row_version` trigger work from Task 1 (migrations 0033, 0037, 0038). No higher-level abstraction — the library IS the pattern.

Checklist verification (per review-prompt.md Queries & Data Access + Security + Resilience + Library Leverage + TypeScript patterns + Common Patterns):
  - Del on membership revocation — verified by tracing: `AssetMemberService.removeMember` (service.ts:262-302) deletes the asset_member row in a tx, then emits a poke via `emitReplicachePokes([userIdToRemove], assetId)` post-commit. User's client receives poke → pulls → `getAccessibleAssetIds(userId, tx)` no longer returns that assetId → `inArray(assetId, assetIds)` filter excludes every moment/comment on the revoked asset → `nextMoments` / `nextComments` omit them → del-loop emits `{ op: 'del', key: 'moment/<assetId>/<id>' }` / `{ op: 'del', key: 'comment/<assetId>/<id>' }` using the assetId captured in `row.a` of the prev snapshot.
  - CVR diff detects visibility loss (not just physical deletion) — verified: the del-loop is "for each id in prevSnapshot where not in nextSnapshot, emit del". Three distinct root causes (physical delete, soft delete, membership revoke) all manifest as "absent from next"; the mechanism is indifferent to why. See pull.ts:283-301 and the new block comment.
  - Soft deletes covered — both `dismissedAt` (legacy REST path, set by moments/service.ts:114 and comments/service.ts:79) and `deletedAt` (Replicache mutator path, set by server-mutators.ts:86 and :145) are `isNull(...)`-filtered at pull.ts:174-175 + 185-186. Soft-deleted rows fall out of current and get `del` ops.
  - Assets not synced (by design) — confirmed via packages/sync/src/keys.ts:1 (`SYNC_ENTITIES = ["moment", "comment", "notification"]` — no "asset" entity). Asset-level visibility loss surfaces via REST cache invalidation, which is outside this task's scope. Documented in-code.
  - asset_member rows not synced — same as above; no authz.ts helper publishes them. Their authz is enforced at REST boundaries (asset-members/index.ts).
  - No `any` types introduced — `DbHandle` is a precise union; `getAccessibleAssetIds` parameter is typed to accept either pool or tx; no `as` / `as unknown as T` added. Verified by reading the changed files.
  - No unjustified `!` — zero in authz.ts and pull.ts after this task.
  - No raw SQL injection — `getAccessibleAssetIds` uses Drizzle's `.select().from().where()` builder chain with `eq(...)`; the tx handle is the same Drizzle interface. No template strings, no `sql` tag with raw concat.
  - No sensitive data in logs — no new logging added; the existing path is unchanged.
  - Proper error handling — no new catch blocks added; visibility-loss is a data-path phenomenon, not an error. The pull handler's existing error surface (tx rollback on throw, Elysia's default 500) is preserved.
  - Appropriate data types — `DbHandle` union captures both handle types without collapsing their method signatures; `.select()` / `.from()` / `.where()` are statically resolved against the appropriate concrete type at call site. Result type of `rows.map(r => r.assetId)` is still `Promise<string[]>` — no signature change for the caller.
  - Reuse existing utilities — `getAccessibleAssetIds` is THE shared helper for "what assets can this user see via sync" (single caller today, but the shape is right for future REST/admin tools that want a per-user asset list without manual membership logic). Did not duplicate the query inline in pull.ts.
  - Exhaustive switch — no switch statements in the touched files. Dispatch is by object-entries iteration, which is structurally exhaustive over the CVR snapshot's entity buckets.
  - Library leverage — Drizzle's builder + tx primitives. No library API was under-used; no hand-rolled wrapper was eliminated because none existed.
  - Pokes after tx commit — verified AssetMemberService.removeMember (service.ts:293-300) fires `emitReplicachePokes` AFTER `await db().transaction(...)` resolves. Task 10 will re-audit all poke sites; this task only verified the revoke path.
  - Del key reconstruction from prev snapshot — `row.a` is present on both CVRRow writes (pull.ts:254, 265) and reads (pull.ts:287, 292). Confirmed stable across snapshot versions by reading cvr.ts:5-10 (`a` is required, not optional).
  - pnpm check-types: clean (8/8 tasks green). @milkpod/api and downstream re-ran; @milkpod/{db,sync,ai,auth} cache-hit.
