## Task 16: Audit invite flow safety

Approach: audited the full invite lifecycle from creation → email → signup claim. The three deliverables per spec: (1) invite creation owner-only, (2) invite "token" properties (random/expiry/single-use/timingSafeEqual), (3) email template HTML-escapes user-supplied fields, (4) signup-hooks handles redeemed/expired/revoked gracefully. Needed to verify the "token" model because the code path isn't classical — there's no URL-embedded secret token the invitee redeems. The "secret" is proof-of-email-ownership via Better Auth's OTP flow.

Library / design verification before touching code:
  - Read `packages/auth/src/index.ts:62-79`: `databaseHooks.user.create.after` wires `claimPendingInvitesOnSignup` to fire after a user row is committed by Better Auth. With the emailOTP plugin active (lines 37-60), user rows are only created after the invitee enters the OTP mailed to their address — so email ownership IS verified by the time the hook fires. The invite "token" model is therefore: `asset_invite.email` as the claim key + OTP proof as the authentication. This is a valid design; no URL-embedded invite token is needed.
  - Read the Better Auth `databaseHooks` surface via `node_modules/.pnpm/better-auth@1.3.34*/node_modules/better-auth/dist/db/index.d.ts:14,22` to confirm the `after` hook signature fires post-commit with the created user row. The hook's return value is not consumed, so a thrown error here would NOT roll back the user insert in Better Auth's current implementation — existing code wraps everything in try/catch and logs, which is the correct posture (signup must not fail on a downstream invite-claim error).
  - Read `packages/db/src/helpers.ts:16-22`: `createId('ainv')` uses `customAlphabet('0123456789abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz', 12)` → ~62 bits of entropy per id. Adequate for a public identifier (not a secret, since revocation uses router-authz + owner check, not a token compare). Task spec's "crypto.randomUUID() / randomBytes" criterion is interpreted as "unguessable" which this nanoid configuration satisfies.
  - Read `packages/db/src/migrations/0034_asset_invite_role_not_owner.sql:7-9`: DB-level CHECK enforces `role IN ('editor', 'viewer')` independently of app code. Covers the Task-16 "role validation" axis without needing app-level re-checks (the service layer's `.$type<'editor' | 'viewer'>()` narrowing is already consistent).
  - Read `packages/auth/src/invite-email-template.ts:17-24`: `escapeHtml()` covers `&`, `<`, `>`, `"`, `'`. Correct set for attribute and text content. Actor name + asset title are escaped at lines 27-28. The `role` value is a typed literal (`'editor' | 'viewer'`), safe from injection. The `subject` string interpolates unescaped `actorName` and `assetTitle` — BUT subjects are plain text; Resend's SDK (MIME-encodes headers internally) handles CR/LF stripping. Not an HTML-injection surface.
  - Read `packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/service.ts:262-273` (from Task 15's updates): the email `targetUrl` is built from `serverEnv().CORS_ORIGIN` + fixed path + `encodeURIComponent(email)` + `encodeURIComponent(assetId)`. encodeURIComponent encodes `"` → `%22` but LEAVES `'` unescaped. Since the href uses `"`-delimited attributes, `'` in a URL doesn't break HTML. Safe at present. Flagged: if the URL construction ever adds an unencoded non-ASCII-safe field, the href becomes vulnerable. Escaping defensively is cheap.
  - Verified DELETE RETURNING is a Drizzle primitive: `rg '\.delete\(.*\.returning\(' packages/api/src/modules` → 10 hits, the pattern is idiomatic here. `grep` confirmed `collections/service.ts:110-112` uses the same shape.
  - Ran `rg '1000 \* 60 \* 60 \* 24|24 \* 60 \* 60|expires' packages` to check if there's an existing invite-expiry constant to reuse. Found unrelated expiry constants (session cache, summary cache, shares) but nothing invite-specific — introduced a new module-level constant rather than borrowing an unrelated one.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/auth/src/signup-hooks.ts:50-86 — TOCTOU: the original flow did `SELECT pending invites (outside tx)` → `for each: begin tx → SELECT existing member → maybe INSERT → unconditionally DELETE invite`. If an owner revoked an invite (deleted it) between the outer SELECT and the inner tx, the tx would still INSERT the membership and the final DELETE would no-op. Net: a revoked invite could still grant access. Similarly, an invite that crossed its `expiresAt` between the outer SELECT (which filters on expiry) and the inner tx would still be claimed. Replaced the inner logic with an atomic `DELETE ... WHERE id = $1 AND (expiresAt IS NULL OR expiresAt > NOW()) RETURNING id`. If the returning array is empty, the invite was already gone (revoked / expired / claimed by a concurrent signup of the same email — impossible in practice, but paranoid is free here) — bail without inserting membership. This is the "atomic claim" pattern: the DB guarantees only one caller observes a non-empty returning, so the claim is truly single-use.
  2. packages/auth/src/signup-hooks.ts:68-83 — The member insert used `SELECT existing; if not, INSERT` — another TOCTOU window. If two hook invocations fired concurrently for the same user (unlikely given Better Auth's single-user-per-signup guarantee, but defensive: an asset could be shared with the same email twice via two separate invites, and both invites could claim in parallel because they target the same assetId + userId), both see null and both attempt insert → unique violation on the (assetId, userId) PK → bubbles as an error through the outer try/catch, but only the FIRST transaction's notification is inserted. Replaced with `.onConflictDoNothing().returning()` — if concurrently added, returning is empty and we skip the notification (no duplicate). The invite delete still commits, consuming the invite correctly.
  3. packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/service.ts:14 (new constant `INVITE_EXPIRY_MS`) + service.ts:209 (use in invite creation) — Task-16 spec explicitly lists "expires" as a required invite property. The schema supported expiry via `assetInvites.expiresAt` (nullable), but `invite()` never set it. Result: invites never expired; abandoned pending invites accumulate and could grant access to email addresses that get reused months later. Added a module-level constant `INVITE_EXPIRY_MS = 14 days` and set `expiresAt: new Date(Date.now() + INVITE_EXPIRY_MS)` on the insert. Chose 14 days after comparing GitHub (~7d), Linear (30d), Slack (no default) — 14 is long enough for realistic email delivery + human delay, short enough to limit stale-invite blast radius. Doc comment on the constant explains rationale for the future reader.
  4. packages/auth/src/invite-email-template.ts:29-34 — Defense-in-depth: `input.targetUrl` was interpolated into `<a href="${input.targetUrl}">` without HTML escaping. Today's callers build the URL from `CORS_ORIGIN` + fixed path + encodeURIComponent-wrapped email/assetId, so `"` is guaranteed %-encoded and the href can't be broken from a `"`-delimited attribute. But the coupling is load-bearing: if any future caller adds an unencoded field (e.g. an inviter display name in a deep-link preview), the href becomes injectable. Added `const href = escapeHtml(input.targetUrl);` and use `href` in the interpolation. Zero behavior change today; one class of future bug closed.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. Invite creation still doesn't use `timingSafeEqual` on redemption. Noted because the spec lists it; verified not applicable to this design. The invite claim path is `WHERE email = $1 AND (expiresAt IS NULL OR expiresAt > NOW())` — a parameterized equality on a non-secret key (email), followed by DELETE RETURNING. No plaintext secret is compared. The "secret" granting access is proof-of-OTP-delivery, enforced by Better Auth's emailOTP plugin before the hook fires. Timing attacks are only relevant when comparing a client-supplied secret against a server-stored secret; here, the server's secret-equivalent is the email's OTP, already handled by Better Auth.
  2. Invite "tokens" (the row `id`) use nanoid-12 (`createId('ainv')`) from the generic db helper. nanoid's customAlphabet with a 36-char set and length 12 yields ~62 bits of entropy. Spec suggests `crypto.randomUUID()` / `randomBytes`. crypto.randomUUID is ~122 bits; randomBytes is arbitrary. Left as-is because (a) the id is a public identifier used only by the owner to revoke via router + getRole() check (not a redemption token), (b) changing createId() for this one call site would fork the id pattern for no security benefit, (c) 62 bits is well past guessability for a public ID (~4 * 10^18 search space). Documented here so future reviews don't re-flag.
  3. The `subject` string at invite-email-template.ts:37 interpolates raw `input.actorName` and `input.assetTitle`. Not fixed because (a) subjects are plain-text headers, not HTML — there's no HTML rendering context, (b) Resend's SDK encodes non-ASCII / CRLF appropriately (confirmed by checking the raw `@resend/sdk` RFC-5322 implementation in their source — they use `Buffer.from(subject, 'utf8').toString('base64')` for encoded-words when needed), (c) the template already uses smart quotes U+201C/U+201D in the subject literal, which would double-break if we escaped. The HTML escape is correctly applied to the HTML rendering path (lines 27-28), which is the actual injection surface.
  4. The `buildInviteEmail` role interpolation (`<strong>${role}</strong>`) uses the raw value from the typed parameter `'editor' | 'viewer'`. Safe because the type is a literal union, narrowed at every entry point (router validator + service signature + schema $type + DB CHECK). Escaping it adds noise without reducing real risk.
  5. `AssetMemberService.revokeInvite` authorizes via `getRole(assetId, revokedBy) !== 'owner'` (from Task 15's service-layer authz extraction). The Task-16 "timingSafeEqual on redemption" axis is the INVITE CLAIM, not the revoke — revoke is a router-authz action, not a secret compare. Correct as-is.
  6. `claimPendingInvitesOnSignup` runs a small number of per-invite transactions in series. Could parallelize with `Promise.all(pending.map(i => claim(i)))` to reduce signup latency for users with many pending invites. Skipped because the realistic p99 is 1-2 invites per new user; parallelizing creates 2 tx handles on the pool for negligible wall-clock gain. Left serial.
  7. The `emailOTP` plugin at packages/auth/src/index.ts:38-60 and the `sendVerificationOTP` handler re-creates a Resend instance via the outer `new Resend(env.RESEND_API_KEY)` AND the `sendInviteEmail` path at packages/auth/src/invite-email.ts:10-14 creates its own `_resend` singleton. Two Resend instances per process is wasteful but not a Task-16 issue; flagged for a generic Resend-singleton extraction in a cleanup task.
  8. Invite acceptance is not explicitly confirmed by the invitee — signup silently claims any pending invite for that email. Some products require an "Accept invite" click. Left as-is because (a) it matches the design (email OTP is the acceptance signal), (b) the invite mail CTA "Accept invite & sign up" communicates this to the user, (c) adding an explicit accept step is a product decision, not a security/correctness fix. Noted for product review.

Library improvements made:
  - Replaced the `SELECT existing → INSERT maybe → DELETE always` TOCTOU-prone sequence with Drizzle's native `.delete(...).where(...).returning()` atomic-claim + `.insert(...).onConflictDoNothing().returning()` idempotent-insert pair. Matches review-prompt.md's library leverage nudge: "Are there `INSERT ... check for existing ... INSERT` patterns that should be `db.insert().onConflictDoNothing()` or `onConflictDoUpdate()`?" The pattern is now: the DB arbitrates both "did anyone else claim this invite?" (delete-returning) and "was this membership concurrently inserted?" (onConflictDoNothing), zero application-level locking.
  - Used the same `or(isNull, gt)` expiry predicate inside the tx's DELETE that the outer SELECT used for consistency. Drizzle's `and/or/isNull/gt` compose into a single WHERE clause that PG evaluates atomically — no window of drift.
  - The email-template escape now covers all three user-reachable interpolation sites in the HTML (actor, title, href). Plain-text and subject paths are intentionally unescaped per the rationale above.

Checklist verification (per review-prompt.md Backend + Library Leverage + Common Patterns):
  - Input validation on every route — AssetMemberModel.invite validates `email: t.String({ format: 'email', maxLength: 320 })` and `role: Union(editor, viewer)`. DB CHECK constraint (0034) independently rejects owner role. Layered.
  - Auth on every endpoint — `.guard({ auth: true })` at router; service-layer `getRole(assetId, invitedBy) === 'owner'` enforcement on invite / remove / revoke (from Task 15). Owner-only on invite creation confirmed.

The `to` address is the invitee's email — this IS recipient PII that goes into logs. Considered masking but left as-is because (a) it matches the existing OTP send-failure logger pattern at auth/src/index.ts:50-56, (b) the invitee's email is already explicitly tracked in DB rows adjacent to these logs, so masking here without masking DB doesn't reduce exposure, (c) operational debuggability wins. Resend errors are caught in `sendInviteEmail` (invite-email.ts:38-44) and never bubble to the caller's response path (fire-and-forget via `void` at service.ts:185,215).

Service layer returns discriminated `{ kind: 'forbidden' | ... }` for expected-fail paths, letting the router map to HTTP codes.

## Task 17: Add timeout and generic failure path to invite email send

Approach: single-file task (packages/auth/src/invite-email.ts). Task spec has two deliverables — (1) wrap the provider call in `AbortSignal.timeout(30_000)` and (2) catch provider errors with safe logging + generic client message. The second was already satisfied by Task 16's predecessor code (safe `error instanceof Error ? ... : String(error)` logger, plus a fire-and-forget caller pattern that guarantees no Resend error bubbles to the HTTP response). The first required verification of how Resend's SDK handles cancellation.

Library / design verification before touching code:
  - Read `node_modules/.pnpm/resend@6.9.3/node_modules/resend/dist/index.d.mts:1230-1237` for the Emails class signature: `send(payload: CreateEmailOptions, options?: CreateEmailRequestOptions): Promise<CreateEmailResponse>`.
  - Read lines 185-198 + 344 for `CreateEmailRequestOptions extends PostOptions, IdempotentRequest`. Public surface is `{ query?: Record, headers?: HeadersInit, idempotencyKey?: string }`. No `signal` field.
  - Searched `resend/resend-node` on GitHub for `signal` via mcp__grep__searchGitHub — zero matches in the source. Searched the Resend SDK for an AbortSignal pattern generally: no hits. Confirmed the SDK does not accept a signal through its public API.
  - Read the SDK's internal `post()` at `resend/resend-node/src/resend.ts:136-162`. The options spread into `requestOptions` before `fetchRequest` calls native `fetch(url, requestOptions)`. So in principle a `signal` smuggled via a type cast would reach fetch — but this is undocumented and brittle (a future refactor that validates the options shape would silently drop it).
  - Searched the codebase for existing timeout patterns (`rg 'AbortSignal\.timeout|Promise\.race|withTimeout' packages`): the project uses `AbortSignal.timeout(ms)` exclusively on raw fetch calls (polar.ts, assemblyai.ts, youtube.ts, rss.ts). No existing `Promise.race` helper. No generic withTimeout wrapper. No hidden convention to borrow.
  - Decided: `Promise.race` with a setTimeout that clears in a `finally`. Rationale: (a) Resend doesn't take signal; smuggling via cast is fragile, (b) introducing a repo-wide `withTimeout` helper for a single site would be premature abstraction (per review-prompt.md §"Don't add features… beyond what the task requires"), (c) the function is already fire-and-forget so there is no caller waiting on true cancellation — the only semantic that matters is "this promise resolves within 30s so the timer+handler don't leak."

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/auth/src/invite-email.ts:27-45 (pre-task) — no wall-clock bound on the Resend call. If Resend's API hangs (network partition, provider incident), `await sendInviteEmail(...)` sits pending forever. Caller is `void AssetMemberService.sendInviteEmailIfPossible(...)` — the unsettled Promise holds its closure (including `to`, `templateInput`, template strings) plus an open HTTP connection in Node's undici pool. Under any sustained provider outage this leaks memory and exhausts the pool, eventually wedging the process. Fixed by racing the send against a 30s setTimeout-backed rejection, and clearing the timer in `finally` so the success path doesn't leak the timer object.
  2. Same file, added a module-level `INVITE_EMAIL_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000` with a doc comment explaining why we can't use AbortSignal here (SDK limitation). Matches the review-prompt.md value for "30s for APIs" and mirrors the project's existing 30s choice in packages/api/src/index.ts:103 and polar.ts:{128,154,178}.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. Same two-Resend-instances issue flagged at Task 16 (packages/auth/src/index.ts:16 + invite-email.ts:12). Not fixed in Task 17 — out of scope, and the fix is a broader auth-module refactor (extract a shared `getResend()` or move both call sites through one module). Re-flagged for completeness.
  2. The `setTimeout` is not `.unref()`'d. In a long-running HTTP server this is moot — the process is kept alive by the HTTP listener, not this timer. `.unref()` only matters for graceful shutdown with background tasks in flight. The finally-clear is the real guarantee; `.unref()` would be belt-and-braces with no functional benefit here. Skipped.
  3. The timeout error is generic (`"Invite email send timed out after 30000ms"`) and logged via the same safe path as any other provider error. No client ever sees this — the caller is `void ...` — so there's no generic-message-to-client concern beyond what the fire-and-forget design already provides.
  4. Could have passed `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(ms)` via a `as unknown as CreateEmailRequestOptions & { signal: AbortSignal }` cast, relying on Resend's internal options spread. Considered and rejected: (a) depends on SDK internals (`...options` in resend.ts post()) staying this way, (b) review-prompt.md TS section says `as` casts must be justified and this isn't — Promise.race is a standard-library primitive with no casting, (c) the cancellation benefit is marginal because the function is fire-and-forget and the fetch connection will be GC'd eventually regardless. Promise.race wins.
  5. The outer try/catch already caught errors safely — the new try/finally is nested around the existing try/catch flow (try { race } catch { log } finally { clearTimeout }). JS's try/catch/finally semantics guarantee the finally runs on both the resolution and rejection paths.

Library improvements made:
  - None library-sourced. Resend doesn't provide a cancellation primitive. The implementation uses Node/web-standard `Promise.race` + `setTimeout` — there's no library-native version of this for this SDK. Review-prompt.md's library-leverage nudge for timeouts is "every `fetch()` needs `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(ms)`"; that applies when the caller owns the fetch, which is not the case here. For SDK-wrapped calls without signal support, Promise.race is the idiomatic fallback.

Checklist verification (per review-prompt.md Backend + Library Leverage + Common Patterns):
  - No N+1 queries — N/A (no DB access in this file).
  - Appropriate database indexes — N/A.
  - Pagination on list endpoints — N/A.
  - Transactions for atomic operations — N/A.
  - Use parameterized queries or ORM methods — N/A.
  - Appropriate data types in schemas — N/A.
  - Input validation on every route — N/A (this is a helper, not a route). The caller `sendInviteEmailIfPossible` at service.ts:248 validates via the service boundary + `AssetInviteRole` union narrowing.
  - Auth on every endpoint — N/A.
  - Rate limiting — N/A (no route added). Invite email is triggered by /api/assets/:id/members, which is in the crud bucket (verified Task 15).
  - No sensitive data in logs — the catch block logs `to` (recipient email, intentional — needed for operator debug), `actorName` (user-supplied string, intentional — needed to identify which invite failed), and `errorMessage` via the safe `error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)` pattern. The full `error` object is never logged — Resend SDK errors can carry HTTP request bodies and response headers that we don't want in log shipping.
  - No secrets in error responses — function returns `void`; no error reaches the client. Fire-and-forget design preserved.
  - Proper HTTP status codes — N/A (no response).
  - Webhook endpoints — N/A.
  - Timeouts on external API calls — NOW PRESENT. 30s Promise.race-based bound. Matches the 30s convention for non-streaming APIs in the codebase.
  - Idempotency — invite-email send has no idempotency key. Resend's SDK accepts one via `options.idempotencyKey`. Not added here because Task 17's spec is narrowly "timeout + generic failure path." Idempotency for send retries would require a stable key (likely `invite_id` if we have it, but this function doesn't receive that; it's keyed on to+actor+asset). Noted for future: the service-layer `invite()` has an `invite_id` via the asset_invite row; plumbing it into `sendInviteEmailIfPossible` would enable Resend-side dedup. Out of scope here.
  - Connection pooling — unchanged. The Resend SDK uses the global undici pool.
  - Environment variables — `RESEND_API_KEY` and `AUTH_FROM_EMAIL` read via `serverEnv()`. Unchanged.
  - Reuse existing utilities — `buildInviteEmail` + `InviteEmailInput` from `./invite-email-template` are reused. Resend singleton pattern (`getResend`) is preserved. No new utility introduced — Promise.race is a language primitive.
  - Exhaustive switch statements — N/A (no switches in this file).
  - No magic numbers — `INVITE_EMAIL_TIMEOUT_MS` is a named constant with a doc comment.
  - Proper error handling — try/catch logs safely, finally clears the timer. No errors propagate. The behavior contract (fire-and-forget) is unchanged; only the maximum wall-clock time is now bounded.
  - Migrations safe — no schema changes.
  - Common Patterns #1 (full-error logging) — already safe from Task 16 (uses `error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)`). Unchanged.
  - Common Patterns #2 (missing timeouts) — RESOLVED for this file. This was the specific gap Task 17 was scoped to close.
  - Common Patterns #3 (rate limiter) — N/A (not a route).
  - Common Patterns #6 (duplicated logic) — none added. Resend-singleton-duplication across auth/src/index.ts and auth/src/invite-email.ts remains (noted Task 16); still out of scope for Task 17.
  - Common Patterns #8 (async patterns) — the Promise.race does not create an unhandled rejection: both arms of the race reject via the same try/catch. The setTimeout is cleared in finally, so no dangling timer survives. No state updates after unmount (server-only, N/A).
  - No `any` — zero. `ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>` is the idiomatic cross-platform type for the timer handle (Node + DOM both fit).
  - No unjustified `!` — zero.
  - `as` casts — zero added. Explicitly chose Promise.race over a `signal`-smuggling cast.
  - SSR / frontend — N/A (server-only module in @milkpod/auth, used by @milkpod/api).
  - pnpm check-types: clean. 8/8 tasks green. @milkpod/auth + @milkpod/api cache-miss (source changes); @milkpod/server + @milkpod/web cache-miss downstream. @milkpod/{env,db,sync,ai} cache-hit.

## Task 18: Verify last-owner protection

Approach: single-file task (packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/service.ts — index.ts + model.ts read but not modified). Spec has three surfaces to verify: member removal, role change, asset delete. Audited each against the "refuse to leave 0 owners" + "check inside tx, not app logic" bar. Also audited assets/service.ts:478 `remove()` even though it was not in the task file list, because the spec names "asset delete" explicitly.

Library / design verification before touching code:
  - Read `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7_.../drizzle-orm/pg-core/query-builders/select.types.d.ts:60-72` to confirm Drizzle ships `.for('update' | 'no key update' | 'share' | 'key share', config?)`. Signature at `select.d.ts:586`: `for(strength: LockStrength, config?: LockConfig): PgSelectWithout<this, TDynamic, 'for'>`. This is the library-native row-lock primitive — no need to drop to raw `sql\`FOR UPDATE\`` or a separate advisory-lock call.
  - Searched the codebase for prior `.for('update')` usage: zero. This is the first row-lock in the repo, so no existing convention to align with. Adopted the Drizzle builder form since the project already uses Drizzle-native primitives (`onConflictDoNothing`, `returning()`, etc.) and review-prompt.md explicitly calls out "prefer library primitives over hand-rolled wrappers."
  - Re-read `packages/db/src/schema/asset-members.ts:20-39` to confirm PK is `(assetId, userId)` — so `FOR UPDATE` on the filtered SELECT locks exactly one row, the one about to be deleted. No other tx can mutate this row concurrently until commit/rollback.
  - Grepped for any code that inserts `role: 'owner'`: only two sites — `AssetService.create` at packages/api/src/modules/assets/service.ts:172 and `podcasts/episode-pipeline.ts:67`. Both set the single primary owner at asset creation. No path adds a second owner.
  - Grepped for `updateRole | changeRole | set.*role | assetMembers\.role` across packages: no update/change API exists. The only places `assetMembers.role` is read are authz reads (`getRole`, `isOwner`, `isEditor`, list queries, search filter). Confirmed via `replicache/authz.ts`, `comments/service.ts`, `moments/service.ts` — none mutate `assetMembers.role`.
  - Noted: the notification type union does include `asset.member.role_changed` (sync/src/types.ts:60, api/src/types.ts:131, notifications/service.ts:23, db/schema/notifications.ts:20). The scaffolding anticipates a future role-change API even though the mutator + service method do not exist today. The hardening below is sized for that future.
  - `assetInvites.role` is `$type<'editor' | 'viewer'>()` in the schema AND enforced by migration 0034's CHECK constraint (`role IN ('editor','viewer')`). The HTTP model at `asset-members/model.ts:6-7` further narrows the invite body to the same union. So the invite path cannot produce a second owner under any caller — verified end-to-end.
  - Confirmed `assets/service.ts:478 remove()` deletes the asset row itself; FK `asset_member.asset_id → media_asset.id ON DELETE CASCADE` (schema line 25) removes all membership rows in one DB-side step. This is not a "leave 0 owners" path — the asset and every membership row disappear atomically, so the invariant "every live asset has exactly one owner row" is preserved. No change needed.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/service.ts:286-331 (pre-task) — target-role check happened OUTSIDE the transaction that deleted the membership and wrote the `asset.member.removed` notification. The existing code did `await AssetMemberService.getRole(assetId, userIdToRemove)` via a separate connection, branched on `role === 'owner'`, then opened a tx and deleted. Today the single-owner invariant is preserved by construction (no role-change API exists, and the caller authz limits concurrent writers to owners), so the race doesn't manifest in production — but the moment an `asset.member.role_changed` mutator lands (the notification type + sync type are already scaffolded), two concurrent owner-removal/demotion requests could each read `role === 'editor'` or `role === 'owner'` from the stale pre-tx SELECT and both proceed, potentially leaving the asset ownerless. The review-prompt.md line is explicit: "Check inside the transaction, not in application logic alone — two concurrent role changes could each see 1 owner and both proceed." Fixed by moving the target-row SELECT inside the transaction and adding `.for('update')` so the row is exclusively locked from the SELECT through the DELETE/NOTIFY/COMMIT. Used Drizzle's library-native `.for()` builder (see verification above) rather than raw SQL.
  2. Same file, same site — hoisted the ifs returning `not_found` / `owner_protected` into the tx callback so the RemoveMemberResult flows back through the transaction boundary. Used a typed return annotation `async (tx): Promise<RemoveMemberResult>` so TS narrows the callback's return type; keeps the caller-side `if (!result.removed) return result;` exhaustive without any `as` cast.
  3. Same file, same site — moved `emitReplicachePokes` outside the tx (it was already outside, but the surrounding control flow changed). Confirmed the poke only fires when `result.removed === true`. The existing try/catch around the emit is preserved so a transient realtime-bus failure still returns success for the DB-committed change (lossless revocation; the user will see `del` patches on their next organic pull).
  4. Added an inline comment (7-line block at lines ~293-305) documenting the single-owner invariant and explicitly calling out that this service is the "choke point" that preserves it. This is a non-obvious WHY — per review-prompt.md §"Don't explain WHAT the code does… only the WHY is non-obvious."

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. No `updateRole` / `changeRole` service method exists. The notification-type scaffolding (`asset.member.role_changed`) is present but no mutator, no service method, no HTTP endpoint, no sync mutator writes this. Last-owner protection is therefore vacuously satisfied for role changes today. When the API is added, it must: (a) lock the target row with `.for('update')` (same pattern applied here); (b) if the target's current role is 'owner' AND the new role is not 'owner', check inside the same tx that at least one other `role = 'owner'` row exists for this asset — else return `owner_protected`; (c) record the role_changed notification and poke in the same tx/post-commit flow as removeMember. Noted here so the future implementer has a ready recipe.
  2. Asset delete at packages/api/src/modules/assets/service.ts:478-484 does not need last-owner protection. The delete is keyed on `(mediaAssets.id, mediaAssets.userId)` where `mediaAssets.userId` is the immutable primary owner column. Deleting the asset cascades to `asset_member` via the FK `ON DELETE CASCADE` — the asset AND all memberships go together, preserving the invariant "every live asset has one owner row" by atomic removal. No code change.
  3. `revokeInvite` has no last-owner concern — invites are pre-membership; revoking one doesn't reduce the active member/owner count.
  4. The `callerRole` check at line 293 remains outside the transaction. This is a permission check, not the last-owner invariant. Moving it inside would require an extra round-trip SELECT-FOR-SHARE on the caller's row and buys nothing today (no role-change API; a caller demoting themselves mid-request is impossible). Task 15 already covered caller-authz tightness. Kept outside for clarity and to fail fast before opening a tx for unauthorized callers.
  5. Did not add a DB-level CHECK/trigger for "every asset must have exactly one owner row." Would require a trigger (CHECK can't reference row counts across the table). Out of scope for a verify task; the service choke point + `onConflictDoNothing` on the PK already give us correctness at the current concurrency bar. Future hardening if multi-owner is ever supported.

Library improvements made:
  - Replaced the prior app-layer check-then-tx pattern with Drizzle's library-native `.for('update')` row lock. This is the exact primitive the review-prompt.md "Library Leverage" section recommends ("prefer library composition over custom orchestration"). No hand-rolled `sql\`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE\`` string; no advisory-lock helper; no raw `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` call. The DB-level lock is held for the minimum scope (SELECT → DELETE → INSERT notification → COMMIT) and released automatically on commit/rollback.

Checklist verification (per review-prompt.md Backend + Library Leverage + Common Patterns):
  - No N+1 queries — the tx runs exactly 3 statements (SELECT FOR UPDATE, DELETE, INSERT notification). No loop, no per-iteration query.
  - Appropriate database indexes — SELECT and DELETE both use the `(assetId, userId)` PK; the DB uses the index and locks exactly one row. `asset_member_user_idx` is unused here (by design — we're keyed on the composite PK, not the single-column index).
  - Pagination on list endpoints — N/A (mutation endpoint).
  - Transactions for atomic operations — NOW CORRECT. SELECT-FOR-UPDATE + DELETE + notification insert commit together. Before this fix the target-role read was outside the tx.
  - Use parameterized queries or ORM methods — Drizzle builder end-to-end (`.select().from().where().for('update')` / `.delete().where()` / `NotificationService.record(tx, ...)`). Zero raw SQL.
  - Appropriate data types in schemas — unchanged (no schema edit).
  - Input validation on every route — caller provides no body; path params `assetId` + `userIdToRemove` are validated by Elysia route typing. Authz is service-layer (callerRole !== 'owner' → forbidden).
  - Auth and authorization checks on every endpoint — yes. Route is under `.guard({ auth: true }, ...)` at index.ts:8, plus the service-layer owner check. Verified by reading index.ts.
  - Rate limiting on sensitive endpoints — /api/assets/* is already in the crud bucket (verified Task 15). Removal endpoint inherits that.
  - No sensitive data in logs — the only log site is the post-commit poke failure at line ~355 (`console.warn('[asset-members] revocation poke failed:', err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err))`). Already safe per prior reviews.
  - No secrets in error responses — the RemoveMemberResult discriminated union returns `'forbidden' | 'not_found' | 'owner_protected'`. Router maps these to 403/404/400 with generic messages. Nothing from the provider/DB bubbles up.
  - Proper HTTP status codes — router at asset-members/index.ts:48-71: forbidden → 403, owner_protected → 400, not_found → 404. (The task suggests "409 or similar" for owner_protected. 400 is acceptable here — "bad client request: you can't remove the owner." 409 would imply a concurrent-state conflict, which this is not — it's a business rule.) No change to status code.
  - Webhook endpoints — N/A.
  - Timeouts on ALL external API calls and LLM invocations — N/A (DB only; no fetch, no LLM).
  - Idempotency — the operation is naturally idempotent. Re-issuing removeMember after success returns `not_found` (row already deleted). The row lock prevents double-delete races producing a spurious 500.
  - Connection pooling — `db().transaction()` uses the package-level pool. `.for('update')` is released on commit/rollback; no lock leak on the error path (Drizzle's tx wrapper rolls back on thrown error).
  - Environment variables — unchanged.
  - Reuse existing utilities and middleware — `NotificationService.record(tx, ...)`, `emitReplicachePokes(...)`, `AssetMemberService.getRole(...)` all reused. No duplication introduced. The `RemoveMemberResult` discriminated union was already exported; re-used it as the tx callback's return type.
  - Exhaustive switch statements — router switch at index.ts:55-70 covers `forbidden | owner_protected | not_found` with a `default: const _exhaustive: never = result.reason; throw...`. Adding a new reason forces a TS error. Verified.
  - No magic numbers — N/A (no numbers added).
  - Proper error handling — the tx throws on DB error → the caller (router) surfaces a 500 via Elysia default. The post-commit poke's try/catch is best-effort: a realtime-bus failure does not undo the DB commit, and the user's next organic pull picks up the change.
  - Database migrations are safe — no migrations in this task.
  - TypeScript: no `any` added (the `DbTx` type alias in notifications/service.ts is a known pre-existing `any` — noted, not in scope for Task 18). No `!` asserts added. No `as` casts added. The tx callback uses an explicit `Promise<RemoveMemberResult>` return type to keep narrowing precise.
  - Common Patterns #1 (full-error logging) — the only new log site reuses the safe `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)` form. No full-object logs.
  - Common Patterns #2 (missing timeouts) — N/A (no external calls).
  - Common Patterns #3 (rate limiter) — /api/assets/* is already registered.
  - Common Patterns #6 (duplicated logic) — the DELETE WHERE clause `and(eq(assetMembers.assetId, assetId), eq(assetMembers.userId, userIdToRemove))` is duplicated between the SELECT and the DELETE. Minor — extracting a local `const where = and(...)` would save 2 lines but add a variable. Kept inline for readability; both sides stay in view at the call site.
  - Common Patterns #7 (exhaustive switches) — verified above.
  - Common Patterns #8 (async patterns) — not applicable (no useEffect). The tx callback is a pure async function; errors propagate up to Drizzle's tx rollback.
  - SSR / frontend — N/A (server-only module in @milkpod/api).
  - pnpm check-types: clean. 8/8 tasks green. @milkpod/api cache-miss (source change); @milkpod/server + @milkpod/web cache-miss downstream. @milkpod/{env,db,sync,ai,auth} cache-hit.

## Task 19: Audit share dialog + collaborators-section UI

Approach: two-file task (apps/web/src/components/share/share-dialog.tsx — modified from its pre-Replicache version into a Tabs layout with People + Public link; apps/web/src/components/share/collaborators-section.tsx — new). Also read share/shared-chat-panel.tsx and share/shared-view.tsx for context but they are not in the task scope. Walked each checklist item: role-dropdown / input-label / button-type / catch-swallow / dangerouslySetInnerHTML / keyboard-focus-reveal.

Library / design verification before touching code:
  - Read `apps/web/src/components/ui/button.tsx:44-49` — `Button` is a thin wrapper over `<button>` (or Radix Slot when `asChild`). It does NOT default to `type="button"`. HTML spec makes a bare `<button>` inside a `<form>` default to `type="submit"`; outside a form it's effectively inert but the convention in the rest of this codebase (comment-card.tsx:79, etc.) sets `type="button"` explicitly.
  - Read `apps/web/src/components/ui/select.tsx:27-49` — `SelectTrigger` forwards all props via `React.ComponentProps<typeof SelectPrimitive.Trigger>` to Radix. So `id` + `aria-label` pass through, which I needed to associate the "Expires" label with its select and to label the Role select when it has no visible label.
  - Read `apps/web/src/components/ui/input.tsx:1-21` — `Input` forwards all `React.ComponentProps<'input'>` to the native input. `aria-label` passes through.
  - Searched the codebase for existing `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100` patterns: `apps/web/src/components/comments/comment-card.tsx:77` uses `md:opacity-0 md:group-hover:opacity-100 md:group-focus-within:opacity-100` and `apps/web/src/components/chat/thread-sidebar.tsx:224` uses the same triple. Both follow review-prompt.md §"Keyboard accessibility" convention — `group-focus-within:opacity-100` on the container so the hidden control reveals when any focusable child (including the control itself) gets focus. The pre-fix collaborators-section used `focus-visible:opacity-100` on the button itself — functionally it works (the button is in the tab order even at opacity 0, so tabbing to it triggers :focus-visible → reveal), but the `group-focus-within` form is the established project convention and reveals the whole row including adjacent metadata, which is the friendlier pattern. Adopted `group-focus-within` to match.
  - Read `apps/web/src/lib/auth/client.ts:6-9` — `authClient` is the Better Auth client for the web app; `authClient.useSession()` returns `{ data, isPending, error, refetch }` with `data.user.id`. Already used in `dashboard-shell.tsx:442`, `layouts/dashboard.tsx:23`. Re-used here so the component can read the caller's userId and cross-reference against the `members` list to decide owner-only controls. No new auth primitive needed.
  - Read `packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/service.ts:131-166,223-239,290-298,348-352` to confirm every mutating endpoint (`invite`, `removeMember`, `revokeInvite`) already rejects non-owners at the service layer with `kind: 'forbidden'` / `reason: 'forbidden'` (Task 15 + Task 18). So the UI gate is purely cosmetic — server authz is the source of truth. The comment in collaborators-section.tsx:130-133 now documents this explicitly so a future reader doesn't assume the UI check is load-bearing.
  - Read `packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/index.ts:10-14` — the `GET /:id/members` handler calls `AssetMemberService.getRole(...)` first and returns 404 if the caller is not a member. So any caller who successfully reads `data.members` is guaranteed to appear in that list, making `members.find(m => m.userId === currentUserId)` a reliable way to infer the caller's role without adding a new endpoint or a separate "whoami" roundtrip.
  - Searched `apps/web/src/components/share/` for React Query options used elsewhere: `share-dialog.tsx:166-175` uses plain `useQuery`, same pattern adopted in collaborators-section.tsx:52-61. The project's global `queryClient.queryCache.onError` (api.ts:45-60) only fires for `useQuery` errors, NOT for ad-hoc mutation fetches. So each mutation handler MUST catch its own throws — the pre-fix `try { … } finally { … }` blocks had no `catch` clause, which means a raw fetch rejection (network failure, CORS error, gateway 502 with non-JSON body) would bubble as an unhandled promise rejection and show no toast. Review-prompt.md §"Error Handling & UX" / Common-Pattern #5 specifically flags this silent-swallow pattern.
  - `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` search: `rg 'dangerouslySetInnerHTML' apps/web/src/components/share` → zero. Every interpolation (`{m.name}`, `{m.email}`, `{inv.email}`, `` Share "{resourceName}" ``, link URLs via `{getShareUrl(link.token)}`) goes through React's default auto-escape. No HTML injection surface. Verified.
  - Grepped for `onKeyDown.*Enter` patterns in the pre-fix code: the Invite Input has `onKeyDown={(e) => { if (e.key === 'Enter' && !inviting) handleInvite(); }}`. Considered wrapping the invite row in a `<form onSubmit>` instead (would give Enter-to-submit "for free" + let me use `type="submit"` semantically) but rejected: the row is inside a Dialog and a Tabs panel, and Radix's Dialog content doesn't expect a form as an immediate child — nesting forms creates edge cases with submit bubbling into the Tabs. The explicit `onKeyDown` is small and works. Out of scope to rewrite.
  - Read the `isLoading` vs `data?.members` branching at collaborators-section.tsx:155-159: the pre-fix rendered the invite input unconditionally (even while the members query was loading). After the fix, the owner-gated branch renders ONLY when `isOwner` is truthy, which requires both `session.user.id` and the members list to have resolved. Until then the invite form is hidden. This is the "pessimistic gating" comment at line 130-133. For an owner viewing their own asset, the flash is imperceptible because both the session and members queries typically resolve in tens of ms; for a non-owner it correctly never appears.
  - Considered whether `data?.members` might legitimately lack the caller (e.g. admin impersonation). Searched for admin share-override flows: none exist. Admins open the app as themselves; they don't have magic access to other users' assets via this dialog. So `members.some((m) => m.userId === currentUserId && m.role === 'owner')` is complete — there is no lurking admin-override codepath that would want the invite form visible without an owner membership row.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. apps/web/src/components/share/collaborators-section.tsx:119-153 (pre-fix) — the invite input + role select + invite button rendered unconditionally. A viewer/editor opening the share dialog saw the form and got a 403 on submit. Fixed by gating the invite row behind `isOwner`. The server authz remains the source of truth; this is the UX layer.
  2. Same file, :80-95,97-114 (pre-fix) — the `handleRemove` and `handleRevokeInvite` buttons rendered unconditionally for every non-owner member / every pending invite. Fixed by gating both buttons behind `isOwner`. A viewer/editor no longer sees mute/delete controls they can't use.
  3. Same file, :80-114 (pre-fix) — all three handlers used `try { … } finally { … }` with no catch. A raw fetch rejection (network, CORS, non-JSON response) would bubble as an unhandled promise rejection with no user feedback (the global `queryClient.queryCache.onError` at api.ts:45 only fires for `useQuery`, not for one-shot mutation fetches). Added `catch (err) { toast.error(toToastErrorMessage(err, 'Failed to …')); }` to each — same fallback-message pattern the component already uses for Eden-returned `error.value`. Closes review-prompt.md Common-Pattern #5 (silent error swallowing).
  4. Same file, :122 (pre-fix) — the email Input had a `placeholder="Add people by email"` but no label or `aria-label`. Screen readers don't expose placeholder as an accessible name reliably (especially after the user starts typing, the placeholder disappears). Added `aria-label="Invitee email"`. review-prompt.md §"Accessibility" / "Every input has a label".
  5. Same file, :138 (pre-fix) — the Role `SelectTrigger` had no accessible name. The trigger shows `<SelectValue />` ("Editor" or "Viewer") but SR users who tab to the trigger hear only the current value, with no indication that the control's purpose is "invitee role". Added `aria-label="Invitee role"`.
  6. Same file, :189-192,222-225 (pre-fix) — remove/revoke buttons used `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus-visible:opacity-100`. The `focus-visible` form technically reveals the button when a keyboard user tabs to it, but the project's convention (comment-card.tsx, thread-sidebar.tsx) uses `group-focus-within:opacity-100` on the same element so focus anywhere in the row reveals the controls. Extracted a single class constant `REVEAL_ON_HOVER_FOCUS` (lines 29-30) for the three call sites; swapped to `group-hover:opacity-100 group-focus-within:opacity-100` per review-prompt.md Common-Pattern #4 + §"Keyboard accessibility".
  7. Same file, all three mutation buttons (Invite / Remove / Revoke) had no `type` attribute. Fixed — added `type="button"` explicitly. Minor preventive fix: if any future refactor wraps this in a `<form>`, clicking any of these would submit the form; explicit `type="button"` closes that future footgun.
  8. Same file, :240 (pre-fix) — the "Only you can see this" hint rendered for any viewer of a solo-owner asset. A viewer/editor viewing such an asset would see "Only you can see this" which is factually wrong from their POV. Gated with `isOwner &&` so only the actual owner sees the invite-more-people nudge.
  9. apps/web/src/components/share/share-dialog.tsx:109 (pre-fix) — the DialogTrigger-wrapped "Share" button had no `type`. Since `DialogTrigger asChild` replaces the button with a Radix Slot, the HTML element is still `<button>` and the same risk applies. Added `type="button"`.
  10. apps/web/src/components/share/share-dialog.tsx:272 (pre-fix) — the "Expires" `<Label>` had no `htmlFor`. The Radix SelectTrigger is a `<button>` that participates in the label-click contract only if `htmlFor`/`id` match. Added `htmlFor="share-expiry"` on the Label and `id="share-expiry"` on the SelectTrigger, so clicking "Expires" focuses the trigger.
  11. apps/web/src/components/share/share-dialog.tsx:287,326,341 — the Create / Copy / Link-options buttons had no `type`. Added `type="button"` to all three. The Copy and Link-options buttons already have `aria-label` (copy + link options) and the Create button has a visible text label ("Create link"), so a11y coverage was already correct; this closes only the implicit-submit-type concern.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. The pre-fix code used `focus-visible:opacity-100` on the button itself, which DOES reveal the button when a keyboard user tabs to it (button is in the tab order even at opacity 0). Swapped to `group-focus-within` per codebase convention; the original would have met minimum a11y, but the convention is better UX (whole row reveals, so neighboring metadata isn't hidden while a keyboard user operates the action). Noted here so a future reader knows the pre-fix was not blindly broken.
  2. The invite email Input's `onKeyDown` Enter handler is a one-off instead of wrapping the row in a `<form onSubmit>`. Considered switching to a form for free Enter-to-submit + semantic `type="submit"`. Rejected: the row is inside a Radix Dialog + Tabs; nested forms inside Dialog can cause submit-bubbling edge cases and the current code is clear and correct. Out of scope.
  3. The "People" tab is the default `defaultValue="people"` even for non-owners — meaning a viewer opening the share dialog sees the member list first, then can switch to "Public link" to see any public links. For non-owners this is fine: they just see who else has access, no write UI. If product wanted viewer-specific tabs, that would be a design change, not a review fix.
  4. The `getInitials()` helper at line 32 splits on `[\s@.]+`. For an email like `a@b.com`, it would produce `AB` ("a" + "b"). For a pasted display name with only one part ("Yash"), it produces `Y`. Edge case: a name that contains emoji or CJK characters — the current split is whitespace-separated, which handles CJK OK (no whitespace → single token → single initial). Good enough. No change.
  5. `setActiveShareLinkCount(links.length)` inside the queryFn at share-dialog.tsx:170 is a side-effect in a data-fetching callback. Noted but out of scope — predates this task and is used by the plan-cache module. A future refactor could move it to a `useEffect` watching `data`, but it's working code.
  6. No ownership check is made on `fetchShareLinks()` in share-dialog.tsx:169 — the server is authoritative; any public-link endpoint already scopes to the caller's owned assets/collections. Confirmed by convention, not re-verified here.
  7. The Revoke-link DropdownMenuItem uses `variant="destructive"` from shadcn DropdownMenu. The destructive variant provides red text + hover styling; no explicit `aria-label` needed because the item text "Revoke link" is sufficient.
  8. Could have wrapped the Input + Select + Button trio in a `<form onSubmit>` to get native submit semantics. Intentionally not done — see note (2) above.
  9. The `DialogContent className="grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)] sm:max-w-lg"` uses an arbitrary value `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)]`. This is a Tailwind arbitrary value for a grid template; there's no theme token that expresses `minmax(0,1fr)` directly, so the arbitrary is justified per review-prompt.md §"Tailwind v4" ("text-[10px] is acceptable if no theme token exists"). Same pass for `text-[10px]` on avatar fallback + pending-invite subtitle — no `text-2xs` or similar in the theme. Kept.
  10. The `text-xs capitalize` on a non-owner member's role displays "editor" / "viewer" — it relies solely on text, not color, so review-prompt.md §"No color-only indicators" is satisfied.

Library improvements made:
  - Re-used `authClient.useSession()` (Better Auth) instead of threading `userId` through props or reading a custom context. Already the established pattern in this codebase (dashboard-shell.tsx, layouts/dashboard.tsx). Avoided introducing a parallel "current user" context.
  - Re-used `toToastErrorMessage(err, fallback)` for the new catch blocks — the helper already handles `Error`, `string`, `{ message }`, `{ error }`, URL scrubbing, length clipping (api.ts:10-33). No need to duplicate that logic per handler.
  - Extracted `REVEAL_ON_HOVER_FOCUS` as a single string constant at module scope. This is a local convenience (three call sites in one file) — didn't promote it to a shared `utils.ts` since only this file uses the exact combination and the comment-card / thread-sidebar variants use `md:` prefixes that this row doesn't need.
  - Did NOT introduce a new "useCurrentMember" hook. The `members.some(...)` line is 2 lines and only used in one place; a hook would be premature abstraction per review-prompt.md §"Don't add features… beyond what the task requires".

Checklist verification (per review-prompt.md Frontend + Library Leverage + Common Patterns):
  - No redundant state — `isOwner` is derived from `members` + `currentUserId`, not stored. `inviting` / `removingUserId` / `revokingInviteId` are per-button in-flight flags, can't be derived.
  - Proper cleanup in useEffects — no useEffects in this task.
  - Unnecessary re-renders / useMemo — nothing to memoize. `isOwner` is O(members.length), typically < 20 rows, cheap. The handlers are closures over state that would need useCallback ONLY if passed to `React.memo`-wrapped children; they're not, so no memoization needed.
  - Stable keys — `m.userId` for members, `inv.id` for pending invites. Both are DB-generated IDs. Verified.
  - Guard browser-only APIs — no `window`/`document`/`navigator` access in the new collaborators-section. `share-dialog.tsx` uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText` inside an event handler (user-action scope, safe — runs post-mount) + `window.location.origin` inside `getShareUrl` which is guarded with `typeof window !== 'undefined'` (share-dialog.tsx:85). No new SSR risk.
  - No dangerouslySetInnerHTML — zero in both files. React auto-escape on all interpolations.
  - Remove dead code — `Trash2` import is still used twice; `Mail` still used. `Check, Copy, Link2, Share2` still used. No orphans.
  - No `any` — zero. `setRole(v as InviteRole)` cast is pre-existing and narrow (Radix gives a string from `onValueChange`, and the only two `<SelectItem>` values are `'editor'` and `'viewer'`). Could be tightened with a type guard but it's a 1-liner with no bug surface; out of scope.
  - No unjustified `!` — zero in either file.
  - Exhaustive switches — no switch in either file.
  - Tailwind — `size-*` used (lines 170, 192, 197, 243, 253). No separate `w-8 h-8`. `inset-*` not applicable. Arbitrary values (`text-[10px]`, `grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)]`) justified in notes (9-10) above.
  - ARIA / semantic HTML — Every icon-only button has `aria-label`. Buttons all carry `type="button"`. Email input has `aria-label`. Role select has `aria-label`. Expires select associated to its label via id+htmlFor. No role="radiogroup" needed (Radix handles Select internals). Verified.
  - Keyboard accessibility — all `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100` now carry `group-focus-within:opacity-100`. Focus styles inherit from the Button component's focus-visible ring. Verified.
  - No color-only indicators — role shown as capitalized text ("Editor" / "Viewer" / "Owner"), not a color pill. Pending invite labeled "Pending invite · {role}", not just a mail icon.
  - No console.log — zero new. `rg 'console\.' apps/web/src/components/share` → zero hits in both files.
  - Proper error handling — every catch calls `toast.error(...)`. Every Eden `error` branch calls `toast.error(...)`. No empty catch. No "handled by global handler" comment without a global handler.
  - Handle loading / error / empty / populated — loading: `isLoading` → spinner. Error: queryCache.onError at api.ts:45 shows a retry toast (the queryFn throws, which React Query catches and routes through the cache onError). Empty: the `isOwner && members.length === 1 && pendingInvites.length === 0` branch shows the invite nudge. Populated: member + pending rows render. Verified all four states.
  - No secrets in client code — none added. `authClient` only holds the server URL (public) and session cookie (client-managed).
  - Common Pattern #1 (full-error logging) — no `console.error` added; the one we'd have needed is handled by `toToastErrorMessage` which scrubs URLs and clips length.
  - Common Pattern #2 (missing timeouts) — no new fetches added; share-dialog.tsx's fetchShareLinks + `api.api.*.delete()` are inherited from pre-existing code. The Eden treaty client handles network defaults; timeouts on the server side (Task 17 pattern) are separately owned.
  - Common Pattern #3 (rate limiter) — no new routes added. /api/assets/* is already in the crud bucket (Task 13/15).
  - Common Pattern #4 (keyboard-invisible interactive elements) — FIXED on the three reveal-on-hover buttons.
  - Common Pattern #5 (silent error swallowing) — FIXED on the three async handlers in collaborators-section.
  - Common Pattern #6 (duplicated logic) — extracted `REVEAL_ON_HOVER_FOCUS` locally; avoided over-extracting into a shared utility for one file.
  - Common Pattern #7 (exhaustive switch) — N/A.
  - Common Pattern #8 (useEffect async) — no useEffects touched.
  - SSR — `'use client'` directive present. `authClient.useSession()` is a client-only hook. `navigator.clipboard` only runs in the copy handler. Safe.
  - pnpm check-types: clean. 8/8 tasks green; 7 cached, @milkpod/web cache-miss (source change) and compiled successfully. No regressions elsewhere.
