## Task 20: Insert notifications inside the triggering transaction

Approach: pure verification task. The four files in scope were added entirely on this branch (all four register as `new file` in `git diff main`). Walked every INSERT-into-notifications site, cross-referenced its triggering mutation, and confirmed both land in the same `db().transaction(async (tx) => { ... })` block. No code change produced — Tasks 16 (invite flow) and 18 (removeMember) already landed the tx pattern; signup-hooks shipped with the tx pattern from day one. The audit is the deliverable.

Verification before touching any code:
  - `rg 'insert\(notifications\)|NotificationService\.record'` across the branch → exactly THREE insert sites: asset-members/service.ts:184 (invite, existingUser branch), asset-members/service.ts:334 (removeMember), signup-hooks.ts:92 (claimPendingInvitesOnSignup). Every other notifications touch is an UPDATE (markRead/markAllRead/markReadByResource/server-mutators) — no new-row side effects, so tx-with-trigger is moot for those.
  - Comments module (`packages/api/src/modules/comments/`): `rg -i 'notification'` → zero hits. Comments never insert notifications in v1. The plan's "Comment create + notification insert must share a transaction" is forward-looking (applies when/if a mentions feature lands), not an existing code path. Same for moments — `rg -i 'notification' packages/api/src/modules/moments/` → zero hits. So the comments/moments files listed in the task scope have nothing to fix today; they are listed defensively in case a mention/reply notification was inserted inline.
  - Read `packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts:49-68` (`NotificationService.record`). Signature: `record(tx: DbTx, input: RecordInput): Promise<string>`. The `tx` is the REQUIRED first arg — the service has no other `insertNotification` helper that would bypass tx, so every caller must either be in an open tx or pass `db()` explicitly. Verified by grepping `NotificationService.record(` across the whole repo — only the two asset-members call sites, both pass the tx handle from the enclosing `db().transaction(async (tx) => ...)`.
  - Read `packages/auth/package.json` and `packages/api/package.json` — @milkpod/api depends on @milkpod/auth (api imports sendInviteEmail from auth), but @milkpod/auth does NOT depend on @milkpod/api. Confirmed the direction: signup-hooks CANNOT import NotificationService without creating a cycle. The direct `tx.insert(notifications).values({...})` at signup-hooks.ts:92-99 is therefore intentional — it mirrors NotificationService.record's shape rather than calling through it. Documented this observation under Library Improvements.
  - Read `node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.2/node_modules/drizzle-orm/pg-core/db.d.ts` for `.transaction()` signature to confirm rollback semantics: the callback's thrown error aborts the tx and re-throws to the caller. Drizzle does NOT auto-commit on partial success — the tx commits only when the callback resolves without throwing. So if the notification insert throws (FK violation, connection loss, etc.), the asset_members INSERT also rolls back. This is the "no phantom notification" guarantee verbatim.
  - Read the `for await` loop at signup-hooks.ts:50-100: each pending invite gets its OWN `db().transaction(async (tx) => { ... })`. Per-invite tx isolation means a failure on invite #3 doesn't undo the successful commits of invites #1, #2. Good pattern — each invite is atomically claim-or-skip.

Site-by-site proof that notification + trigger share a tx:

  Site 1 — asset-members/service.ts:172-192 (invite, existingUser branch):
    ```ts
    const inserted = await db().transaction(async (tx) => {
      const [row] = await tx
        .insert(assetMembers)
        .values({ assetId, userId: existingUser.id, role, invitedBy })
        .onConflictDoNothing()
        .returning({ userId: assetMembers.userId });
      if (!row) return null;                                // already a member → no notification
      await NotificationService.record(tx, {                // passes the SAME tx handle
        type: 'asset.member.added',
        recipientId: existingUser.id,
        actorId: invitedBy,
        assetId,
        body: { role },
      });
      return row;
    });
    ```
    → Trigger (INSERT assetMembers) + notification (INSERT notifications) commit atomically. If the notification insert throws, the assetMembers insert rolls back. If assetMembers conflicts (`onConflictDoNothing` returns no row), the notification is correctly skipped — no duplicate bell entry for a re-invited existing member. The post-commit poke + email send happen OUTSIDE the tx (lines 195-202), which is correct per Task 10.

  Site 2 — asset-members/service.ts:308-342 (removeMember):
    ```ts
    const result = await db().transaction(
      async (tx): Promise<RemoveMemberResult> => {
        const [target] = await tx
          .select({ role: assetMembers.role })
          .from(assetMembers)
          .where(and(eq(assetMembers.assetId, assetId), eq(assetMembers.userId, userIdToRemove)))
          .for('update');                                   // Task 18 row lock
        if (!target) return { removed: false, reason: 'not_found' };
        if (target.role === 'owner') return { removed: false, reason: 'owner_protected' };
        await tx.delete(assetMembers).where(and(...));      // trigger: DELETE
        await NotificationService.record(tx, {              // SAME tx — notification
          type: 'asset.member.removed',
          recipientId: userIdToRemove,
          actorId: removedBy,
          assetId,
        });
        return { removed: true };
      },
    );
    ```
    → SELECT-FOR-UPDATE + DELETE + notification INSERT in one tx. If the notification insert throws, the delete rolls back — the removed user stays a member, which is correct (if we can't tell them they were kicked, don't kick them). Post-commit poke at lines 352-359 runs AFTER `await db().transaction(...)` resolves, so clients never pull pre-commit state.

  Site 3 — auth/src/signup-hooks.ts:56-100 (claimPendingInvitesOnSignup):
    ```ts
    await db().transaction(async (tx) => {
      const [claimed] = await tx
        .delete(assetInvites)
        .where(and(eq(assetInvites.id, invite.id), or(isNull(expiresAt), gt(expiresAt, new Date()))))
        .returning({ id: assetInvites.id });               // trigger: DELETE invite
      if (!claimed) return;
      const [inserted] = await tx
        .insert(assetMembers)
        .values({ assetId, userId: newUserId, role: invite.role, invitedBy: invite.invitedBy })
        .onConflictDoNothing()
        .returning({ userId: assetMembers.userId });        // trigger: INSERT member
      if (!inserted) return;                                // concurrent direct-add → skip notification
      await tx.insert(notifications).values({               // SAME tx — notification
        recipientId: newUserId,
        type: 'asset.member.added',
        actorId: invite.invitedBy,
        resourceType: 'asset',
        resourceId: invite.assetId,
        body: { role: invite.role },
      });
    });
    ```
    → Invite DELETE + member INSERT + notification INSERT share one tx. If any step throws, all three roll back. If assetMembers conflicts (user was concurrently added by the invite flow's existingUser branch between these two hooks — edge case, but possible), notification is skipped. No poke is emitted (the new user's client hasn't initialized yet — its first pull picks everything up naturally), per the top-of-file comment.

Issues found and fixed:
  (none — all three notification-insert sites were already inside their triggering transaction before Task 20 started. Tasks 16 and 18 closed the last two tx gaps; Task 20 is the post-facto verification that ratifies those fixes plus the signup-hooks pattern that shipped correctly from the start.)

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. packages/auth/src/signup-hooks.ts:92-99 — the direct `tx.insert(notifications).values({...})` duplicates the shape encoded in `NotificationService.record` (recipientId, type, actorId, resourceType='asset', resourceId, body). If NotificationService.record ever tightens its input shape (e.g. adds a denormalized column, validates body against type discriminant), this mirror will drift. BUT the fix — `signup-hooks → NotificationService.record` — creates a package cycle (@milkpod/auth → @milkpod/api → @milkpod/auth), which is worse than the duplication. Three alternatives considered and rejected:
     (a) Move `NotificationService.record` into `@milkpod/db` as a helper — forces the db package to know about notification shapes and types, violating layering.
     (b) Move `claimPendingInvitesOnSignup` into `@milkpod/api` and have `@milkpod/auth` call it via a function-injection hook — better-auth's `databaseHooks.user.create.after` is registered at auth() construction in packages/auth/src/index.ts, so the hook body must live in @milkpod/auth OR the auth plugin definition moves to @milkpod/api. Non-trivial refactor, out of scope for a verify task.
     (c) Accept the duplication, document the mirror. ← chosen. The only-two-fields-that-could-drift (resourceType, body shape) are already type-narrowed by `NotificationBody` in `@milkpod/db/schema/notifications.ts`, which BOTH call sites import transitively. A future migration that adds a new notification type with a stricter body shape would force both sites to update (via the TypeScript `NotificationBody` union). So the duplication is shape-safe against additive changes even without the service call.
  2. packages/auth/src/signup-hooks.ts:50-100 — the `for (const invite of pending)` loop does one tx per invite. If invite #3's tx throws mid-loop, the outer `try { ... } catch (err) { ... }` at line 102-111 catches it and EXITS the loop — invites #4, #5, etc. never get processed (silently skipped). Each completed invite commits atomically (correct), but the user may end up with a partial set of memberships. Task 20 scope is "notification inside triggering tx", which is satisfied — the phantom-notification class of bug doesn't apply. Whether to isolate per-invite failures is a separate robustness question; a future hardening pass could wrap each tx in its own try/catch so invite #3's failure logs but doesn't abandon #4/#5. Not changed in Task 20 to keep the scope narrow and because the partial-success window is tiny (realistically one user has <5 pending invites, and transient DB errors usually hit all or none).
  3. packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts:49-68 — `NotificationService.record` returns `Promise<string>` (the inserted id), but BOTH call sites ignore the return (asset-members/service.ts:184, :334). Could be simplified to `Promise<void>` plus dropping the `.returning({ id: notifications.id })` clause (saves a trivial round-trip of bytes). Left as-is to keep the API open for a future caller who wants the id — e.g. a "send email with deep link to this exact notification" flow. Micro-optimization not worth taking.
  4. packages/api/src/modules/comments/service.ts and packages/api/src/modules/moments/service.ts — no notification insert exists (v1 has no mentions, replies, or comment-resolved events). The plan lists these as scope-in files for Task 20 forward-lookingly. Any future mention/reply notification MUST be added inside the `CommentService.insertMany` / `MomentService.insertMany` path as a `db().transaction(...)` wrapping both the content INSERT and the notification INSERT, with the poke+email side effects moved outside. Documented here so the convention is captured for the future author.
  5. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts:125-195 — the commentCreate/commentUpdate/commentDelete and momentCreate/momentUpdate/momentDelete mutators run inside the push tx (see push.ts for the outer `db().transaction()`). They do NOT insert notifications today. If a comment-create mutator is extended to emit a mention notification, the existing push-tx already guarantees tx containment — no code change needed at the server-mutator level. Verified push.ts wraps the mutator dispatch in a transaction at the outer scope.
  6. packages/auth/src/signup-hooks.ts:106-110 — the error log includes `email: normalized`. Email is PII per review-prompt.md §"No sensitive data in logs". However, this is diagnostic context for a failed signup-invite claim, mirrored by the same pattern in other server logs (invite-email.ts:58 logs `to` for send failures — pre-existing pattern). Masking would require project-wide convention alignment. Out of scope for Task 20. A future log-hygiene pass could hash or truncate the email; not blocking merge.

Library improvements made:
  - None. The existing code already uses the intended library primitive: Drizzle's `db().transaction(async (tx) => ...)` for multi-statement atomicity, with the `tx` handle threaded into `NotificationService.record(tx, ...)`. No hand-rolled savepoint, no manual BEGIN/COMMIT, no advisory-lock helper. Task 18 previously replaced the earlier check-then-tx pattern with Drizzle's `.for('update')` row lock inside the removeMember tx, which is the natural composition of `transaction` + `for('update')` that the library provides. The signup-hooks direct `tx.insert(notifications)` is the only duplication, and the cross-package cycle blocks consolidating it (see Issues Noted #1).

Checklist verification (per review-prompt.md Backend + Library Leverage + Common Patterns):
  - No N+1 queries — verified. Each of the three notification-insert sites is exactly one statement inside its tx. The signup-hooks loop issues N transactions for N pending invites, which is correct (each is a distinct trigger with a distinct recipient); batching into one tx would make a single failure abort all claims, which is the worse tradeoff.
  - Appropriate database indexes — verified at Task 4. `notification_recipient_created_idx`, `notification_recipient_unread_idx` (partial), `notification_recipient_row_version_idx` all live on the recipient-keyed queries that `listForUser` / `markRead` / Replicache pull perform. Insert path uses the primary key (id via $defaultFn).
  - Pagination on list endpoints — N/A (Task 21 scope).
  - Transactions for atomic operations — VERIFIED for all three notification-insert sites. Every INSERT INTO notifications is bundled with its triggering mutation in one `db().transaction()` scope. No phantom-notification class bug.
  - Use parameterized queries or ORM methods — all three call sites use Drizzle's `.insert(table).values({...})` builder — no raw SQL, no string concatenation. `NotificationService.record` + `signup-hooks` direct insert both.
  - Appropriate data types in schemas — `notifications.body` is `jsonb().$type<NotificationBody>()`, `notifications.type` is `text().$type<NotificationType>()` — both narrowed at the TS layer. `rowVersion` is `integer`, `readAt` is `timestamp`. Verified at Task 5.
  - Input validation on every route — N/A (this task audits service-layer tx structure; router-layer validation covered by Tasks 12, 15, 16).
  - Auth and authorization checks on every endpoint — the two service-layer insert paths (invite, removeMember) each enforce owner-role callerRole at line 157, 294 BEFORE opening the tx. The signup-hooks path is invoked from better-auth's `databaseHooks.user.create.after` — authority is the signup mechanism itself (the user just proved email ownership via OTP). Verified.
  - Rate limiting on sensitive endpoints — Task 13/15/16 already registered /api/asset-members in the crud bucket. Signup-hooks is invoked on signup (ratelimited by /api/auth/sign-up/email-otp in the auth bucket).
  - No sensitive data in logs — all three notification-adjacent catch blocks use the safe `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)` form: asset-members/service.ts:106, 282, 357; notifications/service.ts:80; signup-hooks.ts:109. Email is logged in signup-hooks error log — noted as Issues Noted #6, not fixed in Task 20.
  - No secrets in error responses — N/A (Task 20 touches internal service code; router-layer responses verified in Task 15).
  - Proper HTTP status codes — N/A (router already uses the discriminated-union `result.kind` / `result.reason` mapping; Task 15/18 verified).
  - Webhook endpoints — N/A.
  - Timeouts on external API calls — `NotificationService.poke` calls `emitReplicachePokes` which is an in-process event-bus emit (no network). `sendInviteEmail` (called from asset-members, NOT from notification path) has its 30s `Promise.race` timeout from Task 17.
  - Idempotency — the invite flow's `onConflictDoNothing` on (assetId, userId) PK prevents duplicate member rows. The signup-hooks `onConflictDoNothing` on the same PK prevents duplicate member rows on concurrent signup hooks. Re-running `claimPendingInvitesOnSignup` on the same user is a no-op (the invite DELETE is gone after the first run). All three notification inserts are not idempotent by row (no natural unique constraint prevents two `asset.member.added` notifications for the same (recipient, actor, asset)), but callers never re-invoke record for the same logical event: invite's tx body early-returns when the member row already existed; removeMember's row lock prevents duplicate delete; signup-hooks' DELETE ... RETURNING ensures the invite is claimed exactly once. So duplicates can't happen at the service boundary.
  - Connection pooling — every tx uses `db().transaction()`, which checks out one connection for the duration. No connection leak on thrown path (Drizzle's tx wrapper rolls back and releases on error).
  - Environment variables — unchanged.
  - Reuse existing utilities and middleware — `NotificationService.record`, `db().transaction`, `onConflictDoNothing`, `.for('update')`, `emitReplicachePokes` all library-native primitives reused. The signup-hooks direct `tx.insert(notifications)` is the only divergence — documented cross-cycle reason above.
  - Exhaustive switch statements — `NotificationService.serialize` at line 128-148 switches on `row.type` (NotificationType union). Three cases + default → null. `default` returns null rather than throwing, which is the intentional "skip rows from a newer server" pattern documented at line 105-106. If the union grew without updating the switch, the new variant would return null instead of the correct branch, but since serialize receives only v1 `asset.member.*` rows today, this is safe. When adding a fourth notification type, MUST update this switch. A `default: const _exhaustive: never = row.type; return null;` with a runtime fallback would be stricter and forward-compatible — noted as a future hardening, not fixed (serialize is not in Task 20's file list).
  - No magic numbers — N/A. `INVITE_EXPIRY_MS = 14 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000` has a full comment at asset-members/service.ts:17-25 explaining the 14-day choice.
  - Proper error handling — verified. Every catch uses the safe error-message form. No empty catches. No silent swallows.
  - Database migrations are safe — migration 0037 (notification row_version trigger) covered at Task 1. Migration 0038+ (notification table + index) covered at Tasks 4, 5.
  - TypeScript: NotificationService.record's `tx: DbTx` is `any` (eslint-disabled) at line 12 — same pattern as server-mutators.ts. Known pre-existing any, tracked as a tech-debt item in serviceworker-style helpers. Not a Task 20 regression. `NotificationType` and `NotificationBody` are properly discriminated. Signup-hooks' `invite.role` is `'editor' | 'viewer'` via $type<>() narrowing.
  - Common Patterns #1 (full-error logging) — verified clean across all three sites.
  - Common Patterns #2 (missing timeouts) — N/A (no external API in the tx path).
  - Common Patterns #3 (rate limiter) — N/A (no new routes).
  - Common Patterns #4 (keyboard-invisible) — N/A (backend only).
  - Common Patterns #5 (silent error swallowing) — the signup-hooks outer catch at line 102-111 logs but doesn't re-throw. Intentional: signup itself must not fail because an invite claim hit a transient error. Users get a signed-up account; invites stay in pending for manual re-processing. Documented at line 103-105.
  - Common Patterns #6 (duplicated logic) — the signup-hooks direct `tx.insert(notifications)` duplicates NotificationService.record's shape. Blocked by package cycle. Documented.
  - Common Patterns #7 (exhaustive switches) — see note above on `NotificationService.serialize`. Not a Task 20 file.
  - Common Patterns #8 (useEffect async) — N/A.
  - Library Leverage / Drizzle — `db().transaction()`, `.onConflictDoNothing()`, `.for('update')`, `.returning()` are all native Drizzle primitives used correctly in the three sites. No hand-rolled BEGIN/COMMIT/SAVEPOINT SQL anywhere.
  - pnpm check-types: clean. 8/8 tasks green; all cached (no code change produced by Task 20 — pure verification).


==============================================================================
Task 21: Paginate the notifications list endpoint
==============================================================================

Scope reconciliation:
  The plan text calls for a cursor-based REST `list()` endpoint returning
  `{ items, nextCursor }`. The actual architecture delivers notifications
  exclusively via Replicache pull — there is no REST list route, no
  cursor, no nextCursor. The equivalent "bounded result set" control in a
  Replicache architecture is a server-side LIMIT on what the pull emits
  to the client; older entries fall out of the sync window as newer ones
  arrive, and the CVR diff emits `del` patches automatically (same "was
  in prev snapshot, absent from next" mechanism Task 9 verified for
  membership revocation).
  
  Evidence that `listForUser`, `markRead`, `markAllRead`,
  `markReadByResource` are all dead code in the branch diff:
    rg 'NotificationService\.(listForUser|markRead|markAllRead|markReadByResource)' packages apps
      → zero matches in source. Only mentions are in replicache-progress.txt
        (historical doc).
    rg 'notifications/service' packages/api/src
      → 2 importers: asset-members/service.ts, replicache/pull.ts.
        Both import only { NotificationService } and call only
        `.record(tx, ...)` and `.poke(...)` / `.serialize(...)`.
    grep -n 'from.*notifications/service' (server app, apps/web): no hits
      that reach the dead methods.
  Server mutators `notificationMarkRead` / `notificationMarkAllRead` in
  packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts:159-191 inline the
  update directly against `notifications` — they do NOT delegate to
  NotificationService.markRead. So the four NotificationService methods
  had zero callers end-to-end.

Library verification done before fixing:
  - Drizzle pg `.limit()` signature: node_modules/.pnpm/drizzle-orm@0.44.7/
    …/drizzle-orm/pg-core/query-builders/select.d.ts:558 →
    `limit(limit: number | Placeholder): PgSelectWithout<this, TDynamic, 'limit'>;`.
    Accepts a literal number, and chains onto `.prepare('name')` — the
    limit gets baked into the prepared plan text, so no placeholder is
    needed for a fixed constant.
  - Drizzle `.orderBy()` takes multiple expressions; `desc(col)` +
    `asc(col)` compose without fuss. The existing prepared statement
    already chains `.orderBy(asc(notifications.id))`; verified the same
    builder accepts `.orderBy(desc(createdAt), asc(id))`.
  - PostgreSQL uses `notification_recipient_created_idx` (btree on
    `(recipient_id, created_at ASC)` from schema line 67-70) by scanning
    backward for `ORDER BY created_at DESC`. Covers both the filter
    (recipient_id = $1) and the sort — index-only eligible at the cost
    of a heap visit for the actor_id lookup, but the pull join to `user`
    on `actorId` means the heap fetch happens regardless. No new index
    needed.
  - `satisfies never` for exhaustive checking in a switch `default` is
    the documented TypeScript-5 pattern for "compile-time exhaustive,
    runtime tolerant" switches (discriminated unions with forward-compat
    skips). Verified works correctly with the `NotificationType` union's
    three variants.

Changes made:

1. packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts
   
   Added: `export const NOTIFICATION_SYNC_LIMIT = 200;` at module scope
   with a docstring explaining the rationale (replaces cursor pagination
   for a Replicache architecture; bounded client sync window).

   Deleted (dead code — zero callers in branch):
     - `listForUser(userId)` — service method that returned all
       notifications for a user unsorted by createdAt DESC. No route
       wires it up.
     - `markRead(userId, notificationId)` — server mutator inlines the
       same UPDATE directly; service method unused.
     - `markAllRead(userId)` — same as above.
     - `markReadByResource(userId, resourceType, resourceId)` — no
       callers anywhere in source.

   Removed now-unused imports: `db` (listForUser was the only user of
   `db()` inside the file), `user as userTable` (only listForUser joined
   it), `and`, `desc`, `isNull` (used only by the deleted methods).
   Kept: `notifications`, `NotificationType`, `Notification`, `AssetRole`,
   `AssetOwner`, `emitReplicachePokes` — all still referenced by the
   remaining `record` / `poke` / `serialize` methods.

   Hardened `serialize`'s switch default (which was a silent `return
   null` on unknown type). New form:
     ```ts
     default: {
       // Compile-time exhaustiveness: adding a new branch to
       // NotificationType without a matching `case` errors here.
       // Runtime stays tolerant — skip rows written by a newer server
       // rather than mis-shape them.
       row.type satisfies never;
       return null;
     }
     ```
   This satisfies review-prompt.md §TypeScript "Exhaustive switch
   statements with `never` checks on discriminated unions" while
   preserving the forward-compat skip-newer-types behavior documented
   in the prior comment (lines 113-114). A new `NotificationType`
   variant now forces a compile error in this switch, caught at
   `pnpm check-types` rather than silently shipping as a skipped row.

2. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts

   Imports: added `desc` from drizzle-orm (was missing) and
   `NOTIFICATION_SYNC_LIMIT` from `../notifications/service`.

   Updated `buildNotificationsPullStmt()`:
     - Changed `.orderBy(asc(notifications.id))` →
       `.orderBy(desc(notifications.createdAt), asc(notifications.id))`
     - Added `.limit(NOTIFICATION_SYNC_LIMIT)` before `.prepare(...)`.

   Semantic change: the pull now returns at most the 200 newest
   notifications for the recipient. Older rows become invisible to the
   client and are `del`-patched out of IndexedDB by the existing CVR
   diff mechanism (prev snapshot's Object.entries iterated, any id not
   in nextNotifications gets a `del` emitted — pull.ts:366-370). The
   DB row is unchanged — we only cap what syncs to the client.

   Updated the prepared-statement docstring to reflect the new ordering
   and the LIMIT rationale (bounded churn, bounded per-pull work,
   uses `notification_recipient_created_idx` backward).

3. apps/web/src/components/notifications/notifications-page.tsx
   
   No change required. The page renders `useSubscribedNotifications()`
   output directly, which reads the prefix `notification/` scan over
   IndexedDB. Since the pull now caps the synced set at 200, the
   client's IndexedDB never exceeds that size for this user, and the
   component's infinite list becomes a bounded list by construction —
   no cursor, no "load more" button needed. Users who want the
   historical tail beyond 200 would need a dedicated REST query which
   doesn't exist and is not needed for v1 (the notifications flow is
   bell-badge + mark-as-read, not long-term audit).

Issues found and fixed:
  1. packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts:67 (build) and
     packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts:152-175 (list):
     Before the fix, the notifications pull did a full scan of every
     row for the recipient with no LIMIT. A user with N accumulated
     notifications paid O(N) on every pull — unbounded result set per
     review-prompt.md §Queries "Never return unbounded result sets".
     Fixed by bounding to 200 most recent via the prepared statement's
     LIMIT + createdAt DESC order.
  2. packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts — four
     dead-code methods (listForUser, markRead, markAllRead,
     markReadByResource) removed. CLAUDE.md: "delete unused code".
     Zero callers verified with rg across packages/ and apps/.
  3. packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts:146 — the
     switch `default: return null;` was semantically correct but
     did not catch future NotificationType variants at compile time.
     Replaced with `row.type satisfies never; return null;` so
     adding a new variant to NotificationType errors here, while
     keeping runtime tolerant.

Issues noted (no fix applied):
  1. apps/web/src/components/notifications/notifications-page.tsx:24-26,
     34-37 — `catch { // Non-fatal. }` pattern on Replicache mutator
     calls. This is defensible for Replicache: `rep.mutate.X()` rejects
     only on client-side mutator throw (pure set/get against IDB); the
     push pipeline has its own retry loop, and the next pull reconciles
     server-authoritative state. Changing to `console.warn` or
     `toast.error` would be the review-prompt checklist §Error Handling
     position, but a toast on a phantom failure would be worse UX than
     silent recovery. Deferring to Task 34 (catch-block sweep) for a
     consistent repo-wide decision — fixing half now creates drift
     against notification-bell.tsx:38-40 which has the same pattern.
  2. apps/web/src/components/notifications/notifications-page.tsx:51-58
     — `<Button onClick={handleMarkAllRead}>` has no explicit `type=`
     attribute; the shadcn `Button` wrapper also does not set a default
     `type="button"`. HTML default is `submit` when inside a `<form>`,
     and this Button is not inside a form, so behavior is benign.
     Review-prompt §Accessibility recommends explicit types regardless.
     Same pattern exists across the notification-bell.tsx, collab UI,
     share dialog, etc. Out of Task 21 scope; a dedicated pass on the
     shadcn wrapper (defaulting `type="button"` unless caller overrides)
     would fix it everywhere in one change.
  3. `NOTIFICATION_SYNC_LIMIT` lives in the API service file, not in
     `@milkpod/sync`. Moving it to sync would let the frontend render a
     "Showing 200 most recent" hint when `items.length === LIMIT`. For
     v1 the limit is generous enough that hitting it is rare, and the
     UI hint adds complexity for an edge case. If a future user surfaces
     the "where are my older notifications?" question, promote the
     constant to `@milkpod/sync/keys` and add the hint.
  4. The pull.ts prepared statement's LIMIT is a literal 200 baked into
     the plan text — if `NOTIFICATION_SYNC_LIMIT` ever needs to be
     caller-adjustable (per-user admin override, test fixture override),
     we'd need to re-`.prepare()` per limit OR use `sql.placeholder()`.
     Right now a single constant across all pulls is correct. Noted for
     future adjustability if needed.

Library improvements made:
  - Replaced a hand-rolled unbounded SELECT with Drizzle's native
    `.limit(NOTIFICATION_SYNC_LIMIT)` primitive chained onto the
    existing `.prepare('replicache_pull_notifications')` statement. The
    LIMIT is part of the prepared plan, so no runtime SQL text
    manipulation, no Query-time LIMIT parameter marshalling, and the
    plan cache stays warm across pulls.
  - Used Drizzle's multi-column `.orderBy(desc(createdAt), asc(id))`
    rather than constructing a single raw-SQL ordering clause. The
    drizzle builder produces the correct `ORDER BY a DESC, b ASC`
    text internally.
  - Used TypeScript's `satisfies never` (TS 4.9+) rather than the
    older `const _exhaustive: never = row.type;` incantation for the
    exhaustive check. Shorter, reads as "assert this is never-typed
    at this point", avoids the unused-variable lint.

TASK 22 — Exhaustive switch on notification type in row renderer
================================================================

File in scope:
  - apps/web/src/components/notifications/notification-row.tsx

Reviewed the full file + the SyncedNotification union in
packages/sync/src/types.ts + the db-side NotificationType in
packages/db/src/schema/notifications.ts. The type has three variants
today: 'asset.member.added', 'asset.member.role_changed',
'asset.member.removed' (aligned across db, sync, and UI).

Issues found and fixed:
  1. apps/web/src/components/notifications/notification-row.tsx:12-22
     — `renderMessage` switches on `n.type` and covers all three
     variants today, but has no `default: never` branch. If a new
     notification type is added to the `SyncedNotification` union
     (e.g. 'comment.mention' or 'invite.accepted'), this function
     will silently return `undefined` at runtime, which would render
     as the literal string "undefined" in the UI.
     Fix: added the standard exhaustive-check pattern used elsewhere
     in the codebase (see packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/
     index.ts:39, packages/api/src/modules/quota/service.ts:142):
         default: {
           const _exhaustive: never = n;
           throw new Error(
             `Unknown notification type: ${JSON.stringify(_exhaustive)}`,
           );
         }
     Because the whole `n` is narrowed to `never` in the default
     branch, assigning it to a `never`-typed variable forces a TS
     compile error the moment a new discriminant is added but the
     switch isn't updated. Matches the guidance in
     docs/typescript-patterns.md §1 and the `const _exhaustive: never
     = result` form. Using `JSON.stringify(_exhaustive)` (rather than
     `_exhaustive.type`) because accessing properties on `never`
     would require a cast.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. notification-row.tsx:48 — `notification.actor?.name?.charAt(...)`
     has a redundant `?.` after `name` because `SyncedNotificationActor.
     name: string` is non-nullable. Cosmetic, not Task 22 scope; leaving
     for a broader cleanup sweep.
  2. notification-row.tsx:68,74 — `text-[10px]` and `text-[11px]`
     arbitrary font sizes. Review-prompt §Tailwind v4 permits these
     when no theme token exists for the size. No theme tokens in this
     project for 10px/11px (would require a new `@theme` entry to
     retire). Not Task 22 scope.
  3. notification-row.tsx:44-85 — The `<button>` is clickable only
     when `onClick` is passed; without `onClick` the button does
     nothing but is still rendered as an interactive button (no
     `disabled`, no aria-hidden). Real use sites (notification-bell
     and notifications-page) always pass onClick, so this is
     consumer-contract-only, but strictly speaking the prop could be
     non-optional or the button could fall back to a non-interactive
     element when no handler is provided. Out of Task 22 scope.
  4. notification-row.tsx:78-83 — The unread indicator dot has
     `aria-hidden` and no text alternative. `aria-label` or
     visually-hidden text like "Unread" on the button itself would
     announce unread state to screen readers. Not Task 22 scope;
     candidate for Task 33/34 accessibility sweep.

TASK 23 — Derive unread count; mark-as-read via Replicache mutator
================================================================

Files in scope:
  - apps/web/src/components/notifications/notification-bell.tsx
  - packages/sync/src/mutators/notifications.ts

Also examined for context (not in Task 23 scope but confirmed the
architecture end-to-end):
  - apps/web/src/lib/replicache/hooks.ts (useSubscribedNotifications)
  - packages/sync/src/mutators/index.ts (registration)
  - packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts
    (notificationMarkRead, notificationMarkAllRead server parity)
  - packages/api/src/modules/replicache/pull.ts (serializeNotification
    shape match)
  - apps/web/src/components/notifications/notifications-page.tsx
    (same pattern — deferred to Task 34 sweep)

Library verification (read before fixing):
  - node_modules/.pnpm/replicache@15.3.0/.../chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts:
    verified WriteTransaction.set(key, ReadonlyJSONValue), tx.get<T>()
    generic form, tx.scan({prefix}).entries() async-iter, and the
    MakeMutators type that drives rep.mutate typing from the
    `mutators` constructor option. The bell's `rep.mutate.
    notificationMarkRead({ id, readAt })` goes through the
    MakeMutator<F> mapping and is strictly typed off
    clientMutators.notificationMarkRead's signature — so any drift
    in the client mutator's args would be a compile-time error here.
  - Verified `ReadonlyJSONValue` requires an index-signature object,
    which is why the `as unknown as ReadonlyJSONValue` double-cast
    is consistent across every client mutator that sets a value
    (see moments.ts:49,79; comments.ts; notifications.ts:33,63).
    Not a local issue to fix.
  - Search target: Replicache's docs/examples for mark-read / list
    fanout patterns. Confirmed via the .d.ts that the standard
    pattern is exactly `tx.scan({ prefix }).entries()` + per-entry
    `tx.set()` — there is no bulk-set primitive. The current
    implementation uses the correct library pattern.

Task-specific requirements (both pass as-written):

  1. "Unread count derived from notifications list (no separate
     useState)":
     VERIFIED in apps/web/src/lib/replicache/hooks.ts:84-87. The
     `useSubscribedNotifications` hook computes unreadCount via a
     pure `useMemo` reduction over the subscribed items array — no
     useState, no separate REST call, no secondary state. The bell
     (line 24) and notifications page (line 15) both consume
     `{ items, unreadCount, ready }` from the same hook. The count
     updates automatically whenever the Replicache subscription
     fires (add / mark-read / remove — any change to readAt on any
     notification row recomputes the count).

  2. "Mark-as-read is a Replicache mutator (optimistic, server-
     authoritative) — not a REST fetch racing with pull":
     VERIFIED. notification-bell.tsx handleItemClick (line 34)
     calls `rep.mutate.notificationMarkRead({ id, readAt })` — a
     registered Replicache mutator (packages/sync/src/mutators/
     index.ts:40). notification-bell.tsx handleMarkAllRead (line 49)
     calls `rep.mutate.notificationMarkAllRead({ readAt })`.
     Neither falls back to `fetch` or `api.api.notifications.*`
     (confirmed with `grep 'api\.api\.notifications\|fetch' on
     apps/web/src/components/notifications/* = 0 hits).
     The server counterparts in packages/api/src/modules/replicache/
     server-mutators.ts (lines 159-190) mirror both mutators with
     the same argument shape; the recipientId WHERE clause + the
     isNull(readAt) WHERE clause make the server update atomic with
     its authorization check (no separate auth query needed), so
     the "optimistic / server-authoritative" contract holds end-
     to-end with no race against pull.

Issues found and fixed:
  1. apps/web/src/components/notifications/notification-bell.tsx:33-
     40, 48-52 — Both catch blocks for `rep.mutate.*` were empty:
         } catch {
           // Non-fatal — navigation still happens. Next pull will
           // reconcile.
         }
     This matches the exact anti-pattern review-prompt.md §Common
     Patterns #5 calls out: silent catch with an explanatory
     comment but no diagnostic. The operation itself IS truly
     non-fatal (the optimistic local apply already succeeded before
     the catch fires, and Replicache handles server-push retry
     internally with backoff per its docs), so a toast.error on
     every transient failure would be wrong UX — but devs need
     some signal when the mutator body itself throws (e.g.,
     TransactionClosedError, corrupt local value surfaced by the
     `as SyncedNotification` cast, a future regression in the
     args schema). Fix: both catches now capture the error and
     emit a SAFE-FORMAT console.error (per review-prompt §Common
     Patterns #1: `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)`
     — the raw `err` object can carry stack traces / internal
     Replicache state, so it's masked). User experience is
     unchanged (no toast, no blocked navigation); only developer
     visibility improves. Comment rewritten to explain WHY silent
     is acceptable (optimistic already applied + background retry),
     not just to assert it.

Issues noted (no fix needed):
  1. apps/web/src/components/notifications/notifications-page.tsx:19-
     26, 33-37 — Identical empty-catch pattern with "Non-fatal"
     comments. Out of Task 23 scope (not in the task's file list)
     and explicitly covered by Task 34 ("Sweep catch blocks for
     silent error swallowing"). Flagged here so Task 34 doesn't
     need to re-discover it.
  2. packages/sync/src/mutators/notifications.ts:25, 33, 56, 63 —
     `as SyncedNotification | undefined` and `as unknown as
     ReadonlyJSONValue` casts. These are consistent with every
     other client mutator in the package (moments.ts:49,71,79 and
     comments.ts) and are structurally unavoidable: ReadonlyJSONValue
     has an index-signature constraint that SyncedNotification (a
     discriminated union over fixed keys) doesn't satisfy
     structurally, and tx.get's generic overload requires `T
     extends JSONValue` which a non-index-signature object also
     fails. A cleaner alternative would be a typed wrapper over
     tx.get/tx.set in packages/sync (e.g., `getSynced<T>(tx, key)`
     + `setSynced(tx, key, value)` that encapsulate the cast once),
     but that's a cross-package refactor affecting every mutator
     — out of Task 23 scope. Candidate for a later cleanup.
  3. packages/sync/src/mutators/notifications.ts:31, 61 — The
     client optimistically bumps `rowVersion: existing.rowVersion
     + 1`. The server's DB trigger (0033_sync_row_version_triggers.
     sql) bumps row_version on UPDATE WHERE OLD IS DISTINCT FROM
     NEW, so the server-side increment is +1 as well, yielding
     parity. No risk of divergence or UI flicker on reconciliation.
     Documented explicitly here because it's a non-obvious
     invariant that ties two subsystems (trigger + mutator) to
     the same integer rule.
  4. apps/web/src/components/notifications/notification-bell.tsx:28-
     29 — `const visible = items.slice(0, 10)` and `const hasMore
     = items.length > 10` are recomputed every render without
     useMemo. For a 10-element slice this is negligible and the
     outputs feed into children that aren't memoized, so memoizing
     here would be pure overhead. Leaving.

  Backend / Queries & Data Access
    - N/A (Task 23 is client-side; server side was touched/audited
      under Tasks 7, 8, 12, 14, 20, 21).
  Backend / Security
    - N/A.
  Backend / Resilience
    - N/A.
  Backend / Code Quality
    - Exhaustive switch on server mutator dispatch: not in this
      task's files, audited under Tasks 7 & 8 already. The client
      Replicache mutator map and server mutator map are keyed by
      the same const string literals and both typed as `const`
      records, so any name mismatch between client and server
      mutators would surface at compile time through
      ClientMutators / ServerMutators typing — verified above.

  Frontend / React Patterns
    - No redundant state: VERIFIED. useSubscribedNotifications
      derives `sorted` (useMemo) and `unreadCount` (useMemo) from
      the subscribed `items`; no separate state for the count.
      The bell has one local state (`open` for the popover) which
      is genuine UI state, not derivable.
    - Proper cleanup in useEffects: VERIFIED. The only effects
      touched by this task are in hooks.ts (usePrefixSubscription)
      and context.tsx (SSE + Replicache init). Both return
      unsubscribe / close on unmount. The handlers in the bell
      (handleItemClick, handleMarkAllRead) are plain async fns,
      not inside useEffect — no cleanup needed.
    - Unnecessary re-renders: bell renders only on open/close,
      items change (Replicache subscribe), or unreadCount change.
      All three are semantically required re-renders. No memo
      opportunity for the visible/hasMore derivations worth the
      noise (see Issues Noted #4).
    - Keys: `<li key={n.id}>` uses the stable notification id
      (UUID-shaped per schema). Not array index. VERIFIED.
    - SSR guards: 'use client' already present (line 1). The hook
      that touches IndexedDB lives in hooks.ts which is also
      'use client' via its users; the bell itself doesn't touch
      IndexedDB directly — it goes through the Replicache
      instance which is only instantiated inside useEffect in
      context.tsx. VERIFIED.
    - No dangerouslySetInnerHTML: VERIFIED.
    - Dead code: VERIFIED — every import in bell is used, every
      export in the mutator file is reachable through
      clientMutators and mutatorArgsSchemas.

  Frontend / TypeScript
    - No `any`: VERIFIED in both files.
    - No unjustified non-null assertions: VERIFIED (no `!` in
      either file).
    - Exhaustive switch statements: N/A (no switch in either
      file; the renderMessage switch lives in notification-row.tsx
      and was handled in Task 22).
    - Prefer type guards and discriminated unions: the two `as`
      casts in the mutator are justified (see Issues Noted #2) —
      Replicache's API surface requires them.

  Frontend / Tailwind v4
    - No arbitrary values where a theme token exists: VERIFIED.
      The bell uses `size-9`, `size-4`, `size-2`, `text-sm`,
      `text-xs`, `bg-destructive`, `bg-muted/60`, `ring-background`
      — all theme tokens, no arbitrary values.
    - `size-*` over w-/h-: VERIFIED.
    - `inset-*`: N/A (no all-four sides set).

  Frontend / Accessibility
    - ARIA + semantic HTML: VERIFIED.
        * PopoverTrigger wraps a <Button> (shadcn — semantic
          button underneath).
        * The bell button has aria-label that reflects unread
          count ("Notifications (N unread)" vs "Notifications"),
          so screen readers announce the state even though the
          unread dot has aria-hidden.
        * The "Mark all read" button has `type="button"` and a
          disabled state.
        * Each notification in the list is a <button type="button">
          inside a <li>, which is the correct interactive
          semantic (the Popover's <ul> is the content list).
    - Keyboard accessibility: VERIFIED. All interactive elements
      are actual <button>s, not divs. No opacity-0 hover-only
      tricks in this component (no group-focus-within audit
      needed here; pattern #4 sweep handled in Task 19/33 for
      other files).
    - No color-only indicators: the unread dot is color-only
      (bg-destructive) BUT the button's aria-label encodes unread
      status textually, so the count is announced; this is fine.

  Frontend / Error Handling & UX
    - No console.log: VERIFIED.
    - Proper error handling beyond console.log: FIXED — both
      catches now use the safe console.error format. The reasoning
      for not surfacing a toast is documented inline (optimistic
      already applied; background retry).
    - Handle all states: VERIFIED. The popover content handles
      !ready (loading), visible.length === 0 (empty), and
      populated list. The "View all" link appears only when
      hasMore.
    - No secrets in client code: VERIFIED.

  Library Leverage & Composability

  Drizzle: N/A (client file; server mutators audited under Task 7).

  Replicache (the library being leveraged in this task):
    - rep.mutate.* vs. manual fetch: USING PRIMITIVE. The bell
      dispatches via `rep.mutate.notificationMarkRead(...)` — the
      library's own mutator system — instead of POSTing to a REST
      endpoint and racing with pull. This is the whole point of
      Task 23's second bullet and is already correctly in place.
    - useSubscribe for derived count vs. separate fetch: USING
      PRIMITIVE. The unread count is computed from the live
      subscription (rep.subscribe via hooks.ts) rather than a
      dedicated /notifications/unread-count endpoint.
    - tx.scan({prefix}).entries() for bulk update: CORRECT. This
      is the canonical Replicache pattern for iterating a key
      prefix and mutating; there is no `setMany` or `deleteMany`
      primitive (verified by reading the WriteTransaction
      interface in chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts:1000-1023 — only get, set,

      not fixable locally.

      because the downstream usage site (rep.mutate.*) does the

    - Generic constraints: N/A here.
    - Branded types: N/A.
    - AsyncState<T>: N/A — Replicache provides its own equivalent
      via the `ready` flag on the subscription.

Files audited:

  - apps/web/src/lib/replicache/context.tsx (diff vs main: new file, 71 lines)
  - packages/env/src/client.ts (touched for dead-code cleanup tied to
    a deprecated Replicache option — see issue #1 below)

Reference consulted:
  - node_modules/.pnpm/replicache@15.3.0/node_modules/replicache/
    out/replicache.d.ts (Replicache class, constructor options,
    lifecycle callbacks, helpers like getDefaultPuller, makeIDBName)
  - .../out/chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts (ReplicacheOptions, RequestOptions,
    HTTPRequestInfo, PullerResultV1, Puller, Pusher types)
  - apps/web/next.config.ts (reactCompiler: true; reactStrictMode
    default-on since Next 13 means useEffect cleanups double-invoke
    in dev)

    wraps the whole dashboard with userId from getServerSession)

Issues found and fixed:

  1. apps/web/src/lib/replicache/client.ts:1,10 +
     packages/env/src/client.ts:4-8,19-20
     — Code set `licenseKey` on the Replicache options, reading
     `NEXT_PUBLIC_REPLICACHE_LICENSE_KEY` with a fall-through to
     `TEST_LICENSE_KEY`. Per the ReplicacheOptions `.d.ts` (chunk-
     C1TWiB5U.d.ts:1256-1260) AND the `TEST_LICENSE_KEY` export
     (replicache.d.ts:401-402), BOTH symbols are marked
     `@deprecated` in v15.3.0: "Replicache no longer uses a license
     key. This option is now ignored and will be removed in a
     future release."
     Fix: removed the `licenseKey` option from createMilkpodReplicache,
     removed the `TEST_LICENSE_KEY` import and its hand-written
     explainer comment, dropped `NEXT_PUBLIC_REPLICACHE_LICENSE_KEY`
     from the clientEnv Zod schema (packages/env/src/client.ts —
     that env var had a single consumer and it was going away). The
     @milkpod/env package was rebuilt (pnpm --filter @milkpod/env
     build) so downstream web app sees the updated client.d.ts.
     Library-leverage note: we are no longer passing a deprecated
     option; the library will enforce its own future removal without
     affecting us. Before cleanup: 3 lines of code and 3 lines of
     comment dedicated to an option the library explicitly ignores.
     After: 0 lines. Pattern #6 from prior reviews (duplicated /
     leftover logic).

  2. apps/web/src/lib/replicache/client.ts:31-39 (puller) +
     :48-60 (pusher)
     — Neither the custom puller's fetch nor the custom pusher's
     fetch had a timeout. This is exactly pattern #2 from the high-
     signal nudges ("ANY external HTTP call without a timeout is a
     bug"). Replicache's own `RequestOptions` (.d.ts:1054-1065)
     only exposes `minDelayMs` / `maxDelayMs` — those gate retry
     backoff, NOT the per-request timeout — so we have to bake the
     timeout into the puller/pusher fetch calls ourselves.
     Consequence of no timeout: under a hung connection (a routing
     black hole or a misbehaving proxy) the fetch can stall
     indefinitely; Replicache's retry backoff only starts once a
     request RESOLVES, so the sync pipeline freezes.
     Fix: added `signal: AbortSignal.timeout(REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS)`
     to both fetches with a module-scope constant
     `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000` (30s — matches the 30s/60s/300s
     values the checklist prescribes for APIs).
     Verified via node_modules lib check: `AbortSignal.timeout` is
     Baseline-Widely-Available and resolves in the project's TS
     build (pnpm check-types clean).

  3. apps/web/src/lib/replicache/client.ts:30-45 (puller),
     47-61 (pusher)
     — The fetches were called WITHOUT a try/catch wrapper. If the
     fetch throws (DNS failure, offline, aborted by new timeout,
     etc.) the puller/pusher itself throws. Replicache wraps a
     thrown puller in a `PullError` (replicache.d.ts:392-399) and
     does retry internally — but that path logs a noisy error for
     every transient failure, and it doesn't give the library the
     structured `httpRequestInfo` it prefers for backoff decisions.
     The library's Puller contract (chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts:425-444)
     makes the `response` field optional precisely so a puller can
     signal failure via `httpRequestInfo` while still cleanly
     returning.
     Fix: wrapped each fetch in try/catch. On catch, return
     `{ httpRequestInfo: { httpStatusCode: 0, errorMessage: err
     instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) } }` — matches
     the HTTPRequestInfo type shape (chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts:345-348),
     uses status 0 as the conventional "never got a response"
     marker, and applies the same safe-error-format as pattern #1
     (never dump the raw err object which might serialize into
     logs the DB URL, request state, etc.).

Issues noted (no fix applied — deliberate decisions):

  1. apps/web/src/lib/replicache/context.tsx:26-42 — The effect
     uses a `lastUserIdRef` plus the guard `if
     (lastUserIdRef.current === userId && rep) return;` to avoid
     recreating a Replicache instance when React re-runs the effect.
     I analyzed this against React StrictMode (default-on in Next
     13+, which this app runs) and the React Compiler (enabled in
     next.config.ts):
       - In StrictMode dev-mode remount simulation, the effect body
         re-runs with the SAME state snapshot (`rep` = null from
         closure). The guard's `&& rep` clause therefore fails, so
         the effect DOES create a new instance — after the previous
         one was closed by the cleanup. Net result: one live
         instance per cycle. Correct.
       - On genuine userId change, `lastUserIdRef.current` still
         holds the OLD userId, so the guard's first clause fails
         and we fall through to creation. Correct.
       - The ref therefore doesn't actually prevent StrictMode-
         induced double-creation, because rep is null in the closure
         during those re-runs. The useRef is effectively dead
         plumbing — it's neither correctness-preserving nor harmful.
     I considered removing the ref and simplifying to just the
     standard create-on-userId-change pattern, but leaving it
     matches the author's defensive intent and avoids churn; touching
     it would also invalidate the `// eslint-disable-next-line
     react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` comment which guards intentional
     stale-`rep` access. Out of scope for Task 24 (the gate is
     "memoize provider; close on unmount/user change; per-user DB
     name" — all satisfied).

  2. apps/web/src/lib/replicache/context.tsx:44-62 — The SSE-to-
     poke effect sets `es.onerror` to a no-op with a comment
     explaining that EventSource auto-reconnects. Long term, a
     permanently unreachable server means the browser silently
     retries forever with exponential backoff and the user never
     sees a "disconnected" indicator. We COULD surface this (e.g.
     a toast, or wire it into a larger "sync status" UI), but that
     requires UX design that's out of Task 24's scope. Noted here
     so Task 33/34 / a future UX pass can pick it up.

  3. apps/web/src/lib/replicache/context.tsx:38-40 — Cleanup is
     `void instance.close()` (fire-and-forget). `close()` is async
     (replicache.d.ts:275: `close(): Promise<void>`). If userId
     changes immediately, the new `createMilkpodReplicache` is
     called before the old instance finishes closing. Replicache
     tolerates two instances on the SAME `name` (they cooperatively
     share the IDB database per chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts:1114-1122), so
     there's no corruption risk. Swallowing the close() promise is
     the same pattern the Replicache React examples use. Leaving.

  4. apps/web/src/lib/replicache/client.ts:8 — `const env =
     clientEnv();` runs at module scope. Because client.ts is
     imported only from context.tsx (which has `'use client'`),
     Next.js bundles it client-side; the module-scope call only
     runs in the browser. Even if it ran on the server during
     build-time analysis, `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` vars are inlined at build
     time so the call is safe. Keeping the env read at module scope
     because the value is constant for the process lifetime and
     pulling it into each call would mean re-running Zod parsing
     on every instantiation.

  5. apps/web/src/lib/replicache/client.ts:25-26 — We pass both
     `pullURL` and `pushURL` AND a custom `puller`/`pusher`. Per
     the library, when custom puller/pusher are provided,
     pullURL/pushURL become informational only (readable via
     rep.pullURL getter). They're harmless. Leaving for discoverability
     — someone looking at the constructor config immediately sees
     the endpoints.

Library-leverage review (applied while reading the .d.ts):

  - `getDefaultPuller` (replicache.d.ts:18-21) — available but takes
    `{ pullURL, auth }` and uses the `auth` header, not
    `credentials: 'include'`. Our server expects session cookies
    via Better Auth's cookie — not a bearer token in `auth`. So a
    custom puller IS justified. Verified by reading the function
    signature.
  - `RequestOptions.minDelayMs / maxDelayMs` (.d.ts:1054-1065) —
    intentionally not tuned; defaults are fine for our SSE-driven
    pull cadence (we call rep.pull() manually on poke and on init,
    not on an interval).
  - Lifecycle callbacks we could wire for richer UX (out of Task
    24 scope):
      - `onUpdateNeeded` (.d.ts:206-225) — default reloads the
        page on schema/version mismatch. Fine for now.
      - `onClientStateNotFound` (.d.ts:194-204) — default reloads.
        Fine for now.
      - `onOnlineChange` (.d.ts:250-262) — could surface a "sync
        paused" indicator. Deferred to a UX pass.
      - `onSync(bool)` — could power a subtle spinner. Deferred.
    Noting these so a future task (e.g. Task 34's UX sweep or a
    polish task after merge) has the menu of hooks pre-surveyed.
  - `makeIDBName` (replicache.d.ts:117-123) — not needed by us
    directly; Replicache derives the IDB database name from `name`
    + schemaVersion automatically. Our `milkpod-${userId}` already
    produces per-user isolation via this function.

================================================================================
Task 25: Stabilize `useSubscribe` dependencies
================================================================================

Scope: apps/web/src/lib/replicache/hooks.ts (only file in the task). Verify
every subscription hook's useEffect deps are stable primitives / stable
references, and that inline object/array args don't cause resubscribe storms.
Also audit callers (notifications-page.tsx, notification-bell.tsx,
moments-tab.tsx) for unstable args passed to these hooks.

Pre-fix library-API verification:
  - Read replicache.d.ts:357 — `subscribe<R>(body, options | onData)`:
    `options: SubscribeOptions<R> | ((result: R) => void)`. The function-
    shorthand form exists, so we don't have to always pass an options
    object.
  - Read chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts:884-903 — SubscribeOptions<R> has `onData`,
    optional `onError: (unknown) => void`, optional `onDone`, optional
    `isEqual` (defaults to JSON deep equality).
  - Read replicache.d.ts:346-347 — "If an error occurs in the body the
    onError function is called if present. Otherwise, the error is logged
    at log level 'error'." Confirmed by tracing @rocicorp/logger — the
    default LogSink (consoleLogSink) calls `console.error(...full-args)`,
    which means the raw `err` object is bubbled to the console. This is
    the pattern our checklist flags as common-issue #1 (full-error-object
    logging can leak connection strings, raw SQL, stack traces).
  - Read chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts:730-750 — ScanResult exposes a library
    primitive `toArray(): Promise<V[]>` that's equivalent to the manual
    `for-await ... acc.push(value)` loop. The generic form
    `scan<V extends ReadonlyJSONValue>(options)` returns
    `ScanResult<string, DeepReadonly<V>>` so values come back typed.
  - Confirmed replicache-react is NOT a dependency (not in
    apps/web/package.json) — the project rolls its own useEffect-based
    subscription wrapper. All references to "useSubscribe" in the task
    brief refer to this wrapper (usePrefixSubscription), not a library
    hook.
  - Read moments-tab.tsx:75 — `useRecentlyChanged(syncedMoments)` passes
    the stable array from `useSubscribedMoments(assetId)`; no inline
    filter/map creating a new ref per render.
  - Read notification-bell.tsx:24 and notifications-page.tsx:15 —
    `useSubscribedNotifications()` takes no arguments; nothing to
    stabilize.

Issues found and fixed:

  1. hooks.ts:32-38 — Manual for-await scan loop with per-element `as T`
     cast. The library exposes `.toArray()` (see chunk-C1TWiB5U.d.ts:749)
     which is both idiomatic and slightly more efficient (internal fast
     path, no per-yield await overhead). Replaced the whole body with
     `(await tx.scan({ prefix }).toArray()) as T[]`. One cast at the
     array level instead of one cast per item; behaves identically at
     runtime (values come back as the same ReadonlyJSONValue subset we
     previously pushed element-by-element).

  2. hooks.ts:32-46 — No `onError` on rep.subscribe. Per
     replicache.d.ts:346-347, absence of onError means the library logs
     the raw err object via console.error (via @rocicorp/logger's
     consoleLogSink). Common-pattern #1 in review-prompt.md flags
     full-error-object logging as a security concern — the err may carry
     stack traces, internal state, etc. Added an `onError` that applies
     the `err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)` pattern used
     across the codebase; prefix is included in the log message so
     debugging is still tractable.

Issues noted (no fix needed):

  1. hooks.ts — The `as T[]` cast at line 33 is the only remaining
     type assertion. It's justified: Replicache's scan generic
     (`scan<V extends ReadonlyJSONValue>`) returns
     `ScanResult<string, DeepReadonly<V>>`. Even if we plumb the generic
     through we'd still need to strip the `DeepReadonly` wrapper to match
     the `T[]` consumers expect (SyncedMoment / SyncedComment /
     SyncedNotification are structurally JSON-safe but not marked
     readonly). Stripping happens at exactly one site (the generic
     function); no cast at call sites. The runtime shape is produced by
     our own mutators (see packages/sync/src/mutators/*) so we know the
     values match T. Equivalent discipline to the `as unknown as
     ReadonlyJSONValue` casts those mutators use going the other way.

  2. hooks.ts:96-135 (useRecentlyChanged) — Deps are
     `[items, highlightMs]`. `items` is a `ReadonlyArray<{id, rowVersion}>`
     whose only caller (moments-tab.tsx:75) passes the stable
     subscription result. If a future caller passes
     `syncedMoments.filter(...)` the reference would change every render
     and the effect would re-fire constantly. Two mitigations are
     already in place that make the behavior safe even then:
       a) The effect returns early when `newlyChanged.length === 0`
          without scheduling a timer, so at worst a reference-only
          change costs one Map rebuild. No cascading setState.
       b) The setRecent updates use functional setters (`setRecent(cur =>
          ...)`), so a no-op round-trip still reuses the same Set ref
          and doesn't retrigger downstream consumers.
     Adding a defensive `useMemo` around items in consumers is an
     option but would trade one memo'd ref for another. Leaving as-is.

  3. hooks.ts:76-89 (useSubscribedNotifications) — Two `useMemo`s wrap
     sort + unread-count derivations. React Compiler is enabled in
     next.config.ts, which would auto-memoize these expressions; the
     hand-written useMemo is therefore redundant. Not removing: the
     manual memo is self-documenting ("this derivation is intentionally
     stable") and removing it would save a few lines at the cost of
     future-reader clarity. Not Task 25's concern either way.

  4. hooks.ts:32 — The async body function and the options object are
     recreated on every useEffect run. They're created INSIDE the effect
     body, not in the hook scope, so they only get allocated when
     `[rep, prefix]` changes. Deps are both stable primitives /
     context refs. No resubscribe storm possible from this module. This
     is the correctness guarantee Task 25 asks us to verify.

Library-leverage review:

  Replicache v15.3.0:
    - `tx.scan(opts).toArray()` (library primitive) used instead of
      hand-rolled for-await + push loop (issue #1).
    - Options-object form of `rep.subscribe` used (the function-shorthand
      form is shorter but can't carry `onError`). Trade-off chosen so we
      can defensively handle the error callback and avoid the library's
      default full-err console.error.
    - `isEqual` option deliberately left at the default (JSON deep
      equality). For arrays of ~hundreds of notifications / moments the
      cost is trivial; customizing would be premature. The default also
      ensures that identical `rowVersion` snapshots don't refire onData
      even if the underlying key set shuffled.
    - `onDone` not wired — Replicache calls it when the subscription is
      explicitly removed via our `unsubscribe()` return, which is exactly
      what our cleanup does. No additional book-keeping needed.
    - `experimentalWatch` noted in replicache.d.ts:358-370 as a
      lower-level primitive with "better performance (but worse dx)".
      For our prefix-scoped subscriptions with derived UI state, the
      subscribe/scan model is the right ergonomic fit. Not switching.

  React:
    - No `useSubscribe` from replicache-react because that dependency
      isn't installed. Rolled-our-own hook is correctly structured —
      one useEffect per subscription, clean unsubscribe on dep change.
    - `useMemo` in useSubscribedNotifications is a redundant-but-clear
      memo given React Compiler is on; not touching.
    - `useEffect` deps arrays use primitives / stable context refs
      throughout; no inline object/array deps anywhere in this file.

  Drizzle / Elysia / AI SDK / Eden Treaty: N/A (client-only file).

Checklist verification:

  Backend (Queries, Security, Resilience, Code Quality):

Task 26: Enforce packages/sync client-safety
================================================================================

Scope: packages/sync/src/**/* + packages/sync/package.json. Verify the sync
package stays client-safe so apps/web can import the barrel without dragging
server-only code (drizzle, @milkpod/db, @ai-sdk/*, node: builtins) into the
browser bundle. Per docs/package-boundaries.md, every src/*.ts must be free of
server imports since the `./*` wildcard in exports exposes every file.

Pre-fix library-API verification:
  - Read packages/config/tsconfig.base.json — `verbatimModuleSyntax: true`
    means `import type` statements are erased at build time. Confirmed by
    reading the compiled dist/moments-*.js (head -5):
      `import { i as momentKey } from "./keys-...js";`
      `import { z } from "zod";`
    …no runtime replicache import.
  - Grep'd the compiled dist/**/*.js for replicache|drizzle|@milkpod/db|
    @ai-sdk → zero matches. Type-only imports of replicache correctly drop
    out of the runtime bundle.
  - Read packages/sync/package.json. `dependencies: { zod: catalog: }`.
    `devDependencies: { @milkpod/config, replicache ^15.3.0, tsdown }`.
    `peerDependencies: { replicache ^15 (optional), typescript ^5 }`.
  - Confirmed sibling package @milkpod/ai uses the same exports shape (. +
    ./* wildcard with types → dist, default → src), so the pattern is
    repo-standard. Next.js resolves the src/*.ts via its built-in
    workspace-package transpilation.
  - Confirmed `replicache` is NOT in pnpm-workspace.yaml's catalog (only
    apps/web declares it at ^15.3.0). The sync package's devDep pins the
    same version manually. Not a problem for this task, but noted.
  - Read packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts:7-16 —
    imports from @milkpod/sync are type-only (CommentCreateArgs, etc.),
    so backend consumers don't need replicache at runtime. This is
    exactly why `peerDependenciesMeta.replicache.optional: true` is
    correct: backend pulls schemas/types without bringing replicache.

Diff scope: packages/sync is entirely new on this branch (no pre-existing
files to regress). 6 files total: package.json, tsconfig.json,
tsdown.config.ts, src/index.ts, src/keys.ts, src/types.ts, src/mutators/
{index,comments,moments,notifications}.ts.

Issues noted (no fix needed):

  1. Task 26 brief suggested `rg "@milkpod/db" packages/sync/src → zero`
     as the first check. It passes. For completeness I also ran the check
     against packages/sync/package.json and tsconfig — zero references in
     either. No server-only package leaks through the dep closure.

  2. `replicache` is not in pnpm-workspace.yaml's `catalog:`. apps/web
     declares it as a direct dep at ^15.3.0 and packages/sync's devDep
     mirrors that version. If web bumps replicache, sync's dev/peer
     ranges may drift. Adding `replicache` to the catalog would eliminate
     the drift risk. Out of scope for Task 26 (which is scoped to
     client-safety), noting for future hygiene.

  3. `peerDependenciesMeta.replicache.optional: true` is unusual but
     correct given the dual-consumer pattern (web uses clientMutators
     with a runtime replicache; backend uses type-only MutatorName /
     mutatorArgsSchemas without replicache). Setting `optional: false`
     would nag apps/server at install time with "missing peer". Leaving
     as-is.

  4. The `as unknown as import('replicache').ReadonlyJSONValue` casts in
     the three mutator files (moments.ts:49, comments.ts:46/74,
     notifications.ts:33/65) are justified double-casts. Replicache's
     `ReadonlyJSONValue` is a union that excludes non-JSON types
     (functions, symbols, Date). Our `SyncedMoment` / `SyncedComment` /
     `SyncedNotification` interfaces are structurally JSON-safe but
     TypeScript can't prove it without an unsafe assertion. This is a
     documented pattern for writing to Replicache. Same reasoning for
     the `as SyncedX | undefined` on `tx.get(key)`.

  5. notifications.ts:52-54 uses `for await (const entry of tx.scan(...)
     .entries())` to iterate + mutate. Task 25 replaced a similar
     for-await with `.toArray()` in the hooks.ts subscription body, but
     that was collecting values for a read. Here the loop is a write
     path: each iteration conditionally calls `tx.set`. Using
     `.toArray()` would read all entries into memory first, which saves
     nothing (we still need to walk + write) and loses the streaming
     benefit of the native iterator. Leaving as-is — idiomatic Replicache
     mutator usage.

  6. The `docs/package-boundaries.md` rule "Keep client-safe modules free
     of server imports. If a file is reachable from apps/web … it must
     not import @ai-sdk/openai, @ai-sdk/google, drizzle-orm, or any
     @milkpod/db module" — packages/sync satisfies this transitively.
     Every file is reachable from apps/web via the barrel; every file is
     clean.

  7. tsconfig.base.json `lib: ["ESNext"]` (no DOM). The mutators and keys
     don't reach for DOM APIs — all operations are via Replicache's
     WriteTransaction / ReadonlyJSONValue abstractions. Compatible with
     both browser and Node (server-side validation via zod schemas).

Library-leverage review:

  Replicache v15.3.0:
    - `WriteTransaction` import is type-only, correct.
    - `tx.set` / `tx.get` / `tx.del` / `tx.scan(opts).entries()` all used
      idiomatically. Alternatives considered:
        - Replicache's `deepClone` for building the updated object —
          unnecessary, spread + override reconstructs the shape fine.
        - `experimentalDiff` — lower-level primitive, not a mutator
          concern.
    - No library primitive is reimplemented. Schemas (zod) and Replicache
      tx methods compose cleanly.
  Zod v4:
    - Schemas use `.min()` / `.max()` bounds (strings and numbers),
      `.nullable()`, `.optional()`, `.enum()`, `.int().positive()`. All
      first-class zod APIs; no hand-rolled validators.
  Drizzle / Elysia / AI SDK / Eden Treaty / Better Auth: N/A. Absent by
  design — that's what Task 26 verifies.

Checklist verification:
