Task 27 — Verify sync mutator purity and client/server parity ✓
================================================================================

Files reviewed:
  - packages/sync/src/mutators/moments.ts
  - packages/sync/src/mutators/comments.ts
  - packages/sync/src/mutators/notifications.ts
  - packages/sync/src/mutators/index.ts
  - packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts
  (+ cross-ref to pull.ts, push.ts, authz.ts, keys.ts, types.ts, the three
  touched DB schemas, and every apps/web call site for `.mutate.*`)

Scope recap: packages/sync is 100% new (Task 26 already verified it's
client-safe). Task 27 is the last piece: prove the mutator pipeline itself
is coherent — pure locally, name-equivalent on both sides, and convergent
after a pull re-syncs optimistic state with authoritative state.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Purity — `Client mutators have no side effects outside the Replicache tx`
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Grep sweep across packages/sync/src/mutators/:
  - fetch, XMLHttpRequest, WebSocket: 0 matches
  - console.log, console.error, console.warn: 0 matches
  - process.*, globalThis.*, global.*: 0 matches
  - localStorage, sessionStorage, indexedDB: 0 matches
  - window.*, document.*, navigator.*: 0 matches
  - Date.now / new Date: 0 matches (client passes readAt from caller)
  - setTimeout, setInterval, queueMicrotask: 0 matches

Every mutator body communicates with the outside world exclusively through
the WriteTransaction parameter (tx.get / tx.set / tx.del / tx.scan). The
three files that do any work — moments.ts, comments.ts, notifications.ts —
are pure data transformations against the Replicache store.

PURITY: VERIFIED.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Name parity — `Every client mutator has a server counterpart with matching
name`
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

clientMutators (packages/sync/src/mutators/index.ts):
  momentCreate, momentUpdate, momentDelete,
  commentCreate, commentUpdate, commentDelete,
  notificationMarkRead, notificationMarkAllRead.
  (8 names)

serverMutators (packages/api/src/modules/replicache/server-mutators.ts):
  momentCreate, momentUpdate, momentDelete,
  commentCreate, commentUpdate, commentDelete,
  notificationMarkRead, notificationMarkAllRead.
  (8 names)

Dispatch glue (push.ts):
  - `isKnownMutator` narrows via `name in mutatorArgsSchemas` — if a client
    adds a new name to clientMutators without adding a schema to
    mutatorArgsSchemas, the compiler rejects (both maps share
    `keyof ClientMutators` ≡ `keyof typeof mutatorArgsSchemas`).
  - `serverMutators[mutatorName]` is cast to a tx/args/ctx thunk;
    `MutatorName` is `keyof ClientMutators`. A server name missing from
    `ClientMutators` would be unreachable (no MutatorName key to index it).
  - A client name missing from serverMutators would be a compile error at
    the cast site (property access on the typed object fails). Verified via
    `pnpm check-types`: clean.

NAME PARITY: VERIFIED.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Input-shape parity — `matching name + input shape`
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Schema-by-schema comparison (schemas come from @milkpod/sync, servers live
in server-mutators.ts):

  momentCreateArgsSchema:
    { id, assetId, preset, title, rationale, startTime, endTime, score,
      source, createdAt, authorId, authorName, authorImage, authorEmail }
  Server momentCreate writes:
    { id, assetId, userId=ctx.userId, preset, title, rationale, startTime,
      endTime, score, source, createdAt=new Date(args.createdAt) }
  Divergence (acceptable): server ignores authorId/authorName/authorImage/
    authorEmail; derives userId from the auth session. Rationale — the
    author metadata is purely for the optimistic row's display layer so the
    UI renders avatar+name without a second fetch; on the next pull
    `serializeMoment` (pull.ts) rebuilds these fields from a `user` table
    join, overwriting any stale/spoofed client-provided value. The server
    is therefore authoritative on author identity, and a malicious client
    can't persist a fake authorId — the DB userId column is always session-
    derived. See pull.ts serializeMoment L107–130.

  momentUpdateArgsSchema:
    { id, assetId, title?, rationale?, isSaved? }
  Server momentUpdate patch:
    title?, rationale?, isSaved?
  PARITY: exact match on optional fields, early-return if all undefined.

  momentDeleteArgsSchema:
    { id, assetId }
  Server momentDelete:
    UPDATE ... SET deletedAt=NOW() WHERE id=? AND assetId=?  (soft delete)
  Client momentDeleteClient:
    tx.del(momentKey(...))  (hard delete, locally)
  PARITY: see Behavior parity below — the split is intentional and
    self-healing via Replicache rebase.

  commentCreateArgsSchema:
    { id, assetId, body, startTime, endTime, source, evidenceRefs?,
      createdAt, authorId, authorName, authorImage, authorEmail }
  Server commentCreate writes:
    { id, assetId, userId=ctx.userId, body, startTime, endTime, source,
      evidenceRefs ?? null, createdAt=new Date(args.createdAt) }
  Divergence: same author-fields pattern as momentCreate — accepted,
    documented in the same way.

  commentUpdateArgsSchema: { id, assetId, body? }
  Server commentUpdate patch: body?
  PARITY: exact.

  commentDeleteArgsSchema: { id, assetId }
  Server: soft delete. Client: hard delete. PARITY: see Behavior parity.

  notificationMarkReadArgsSchema: { id, readAt }
  Server notificationMarkRead: sets readAt WHERE id=? AND recipientId=?
    AND readAt IS NULL.
  Client: early-return if existing.readAt != null; else set readAt + bump
    rowVersion.
  PARITY: exact. Both gate on "currently unread".

  notificationMarkAllReadArgsSchema: { readAt }
  Server: UPDATE ... SET readAt=? WHERE recipientId=? AND readAt IS NULL.
  Client: scan all notification/* keys, skip n.readAt != null, else update.
  PARITY: exact. Both gate on "currently unread" and both set the caller-
    supplied readAt so the optimistic timestamp matches the server write.

INPUT-SHAPE PARITY: VERIFIED (with one documented-acceptable divergence
on author fields for create-mutators, corrected on next pull).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Behavior parity — `same invariants enforced both sides; server is
authoritative; client is optimistic`
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A. Delete semantics (hard-local vs soft-remote).
   Client: `tx.del(momentKey(...))` / `tx.del(commentKey(...))`.
   Server: `.update(...).set({ deletedAt: new Date() })`.
   Reconciliation path:
     1. SUCCESS: server soft-deletes, trigger bumps rowVersion, next pull
        evaluates `isNull(deletedAt)` predicate (pull.ts L246–248, L257–259)
        → row is absent from `currentMoments`/`currentComments` → the
        CVR-diff "was in prev, absent from next" branch (L370–378) emits a
        `del` op to every client that previously saw it. Other users'
        clients drop it.
     2. REJECTION (ACL): savepoint rolls back (push.ts L153–163), DB row
        unchanged, rowVersion unchanged, next pull emits no patch for this
        row. LMID still advances (push.ts L185) so Replicache confirms the
        mutation. Replicache's rebase drops the pending delete and
        recomputes the local view from the authoritative base — row
        reappears without the server sending a `put`. Verified by reading
        replicache's rebase semantics in replicache@15 docs (`sync steps`
        algorithm).
   PARITY: VERIFIED. Hard-local + soft-remote is the standard optimistic
     pattern for tombstones; both success and rejection paths converge.

B. Create semantics — isSaved default.
   Client: explicitly sets `isSaved: false` in the optimistic row.
   Server: relies on DB default `boolean('is_saved').default(false).notNull()`
     (packages/db/src/schema/moments.ts L52).
   PARITY: VERIFIED.

C. Update early-return.  ← issue found & fixed, see below.

D. Update rowVersion.
   Client: bumps `rowVersion: existing.rowVersion + 1`.
   Server: relies on the BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger installed by
     migration 0033/0037 (Task 1 verified). Trigger sets
     `NEW.row_version = OLD.row_version + 1` when any publishable column
     changes.
   Reconciliation: next pull emits `put` whenever `prevRow.v !== m.rowVersion`
     (pull.ts L314 / L325 / L337). Server's authoritative rowVersion
     overwrites the client's optimistic bump. Drift is one value for a
     brief window.
   PARITY: VERIFIED.

E. Auth boundary.
   Server moment*/comment* mutators: `await requireEditor(args.assetId,
     ctx.userId)` — throws MutatorForbiddenError if the caller isn't an
     editor or owner (authz.ts L87–97, Task 8 verified).
   Server notification*: inline WHERE `recipientId = ctx.userId` makes the
     ownership check atomic with the mutation — a caller who somehow knew
     another user's notification id still wouldn't affect that row.
   Client mutators: no auth checks. That's correct for optimistic clients:
     writes succeed locally regardless; the server's rejection is the
     authoritative signal and Replicache rebases the local view on LMID
     advance.
   PARITY: VERIFIED.

F. notificationMarkAllRead ordering.
   Client: streaming `for await` over `tx.scan({ prefix }).entries()`; each
     iteration reads one entry, writes zero or one, yields. Task 26 noted
     this is the idiomatic Replicache pattern for scan+write loops
     (streaming ≻ toArray here because writes are interleaved).
   Server: single UPDATE with compound WHERE. Atomic.
   Both gate on "currently unread" and produce the same net effect on the
     set of unread notifications. The client's per-row stream has a
     trivially longer window where a concurrent server-side mark-read
     could race, but the re-guard `if (n.readAt != null) continue` handles
     it — worst case is a duplicate write of the same readAt, which is
     identical to the server's idempotent UPDATE.
   PARITY: VERIFIED.

G. Post-commit pokes.
   Server (push.ts L202–221): after transaction commits, moments/comments
     mutations poke every member of the affected asset via
     `AssetMemberService.pokeMembers` (asset-member fanout); notification
     mutations (USER_SCOPED_MUTATORS) emit a user-scoped poke so only the
     caller's other sessions rebase. Task 10 verified the after-commit
     ordering.
   PARITY: VERIFIED — collaboration flows propagate; notification flows
     don't leak to unrelated users.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues found and fixed
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. packages/sync/src/mutators/moments.ts `momentUpdateClient`
   packages/sync/src/mutators/comments.ts `commentUpdateClient`
   Divergence: the client mutators used `const { id: _id, assetId: _a,
   ...patch } = args;` and always bumped `rowVersion: existing.rowVersion
   + 1`, regardless of whether any update fields were actually present.
   The server's counterparts (server-mutators.ts L62–66 / L123–125) build
   `patch` field-by-field and early-return when `Object.keys(patch).length
   === 0`.

   Two failure modes:
     a) No-op call (`momentUpdate({ id, assetId })` with all optional
        fields absent): server writes nothing, trigger doesn't fire,
        DB rowVersion stays at N. Client writes `rowVersion: N+1`
        locally. Until some real update moves the server's rowVersion to
        N+1, the client carries an ahead-of-reality counter. The CVR diff
        (`prevRow.v !== m.rowVersion`) sees no change on subsequent pulls,
        so the server never corrects the local drift until a real update
        happens. Cosmetic — not data-corrupting — but real.
     b) `{ title: undefined }` passed explicitly: the rest-spread copied
        `title: undefined` into `patch`, then `...patch` overwrote
        `existing.title` with `undefined`. The SyncedMoment type requires
        `title: string`, so the local row briefly violates its own shape.
        Server's `if (args.title !== undefined)` guard already skipped
        this.

   Fix: mirror the server's gate exactly. Build the patch from defined
   fields only, return early when the patch is empty, and for
   commentUpdate (single optional field) short-circuit with
   `if (args.body === undefined) return`. Client and server are now
   bit-for-bit equivalent on no-op update calls.

   Diff:
     - momentUpdateClient: replaced rest-spread with explicit per-field
       checks for title/rationale/isSaved; added
       `if (Object.keys(patch).length === 0) return` before the rowVersion
       bump and tx.set.
     - commentUpdateClient: replaced rest-spread with explicit body check;
       returns early on undefined body.

   Comments inserted at each fix site document the reasoning (why the
   rest-spread drifted from the server, what the explicit gate buys).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues noted (no fix)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. momentCreate/commentCreate include author* fields that the server
   ignores. Acceptable as documented above: the server derives authorId
   from ctx.userId (authoritative) and pull.ts re-hydrates
   authorName/authorImage/authorEmail from the user table join. A
   malicious client cannot persist a fake authorId — DB column `user_id`
   comes from session only. Tightening this would require either:
     a) removing author fields from the create schemas and having the
        client read the current user from session via a separate mechanism
        to seed the optimistic row — more plumbing for negligible benefit
        since the optimistic row is corrected within a pull RTT;
     b) server-side validation `args.authorId === ctx.userId` — adds a
        brittle failure mode if the client ever has a stale session view.
   Current design (server silently overwrites) is the right trade-off:
   never trusts client identity claims, tolerates client bugs.

2. server-mutators.ts uses `const patch: Record<string, unknown> = {}` for
   momentUpdate and commentUpdate. Drizzle exports
   `PgUpdateSetSource<typeof table>` (from drizzle-orm/pg-core) which is
   a typed shape for `.set()`. Swapping to
   `const patch: Partial<typeof assetMoments.$inferInsert> = {}` would
   give better type safety on the builder side. Did NOT apply —
   `DbTx` is `any` so the final `.set(patch)` call isn't type-checked
   anyway, and the code is short enough that wrong keys would be caught
   at review. Task 27 scope is parity/purity, not typing refactor. Noted
   for a future hygiene pass.

3. `notificationMarkAllRead` client scan uses `for await` on
   `tx.scan(...).entries()` — idiomatic streaming iterator for a
   scan+write. Task 25 migrated read-only scans to `.toArray()`, but that
   pattern doesn't fit here (the write is interleaved with the read, so
   loading everything first saves nothing). Leaving as-is; same reasoning
   as Task 26.

4. `MomentCreateArgs` / `CommentCreateArgs` have no call sites from
   apps/web yet (grep for `.mutate.momentCreate|commentCreate` returns
   zero hits). These mutators exist as infrastructure for the collab
   editor that will add human-authored moments/comments via Replicache;
   until the UI lands they're dead code in the apps/web sense, but the
   server mutators and schemas need to exist so push.ts can dispatch
   pushed mutations when the feature ships. Not a Task 27 concern — Task
   29 (REST/Replicache double-write audit) will revisit when the create
   path goes live.

5. Client mutators don't run zod `.parse()` on args before operating.
   Deliberate: the authoritative validation is server-side (`schema.
   safeParse(mutation.args)` in push.ts L129). Validating on the client
   would add per-mutation CPU on every optimistic write for no safety
   gain — a client writing invalid args just sees the server reject the
   push and Replicache rebase the local view. Not fixing.

6. The `as unknown as ReadonlyJSONValue` double-casts in moments.ts /
   comments.ts / notifications.ts are the same boundary-casts Task 26
   justified. No library primitive supersedes them — Replicache's
   ReadonlyJSONValue is a structural union TS can't prove our interfaces
   satisfy without a full runtime walk. Noted, kept.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Library-leverage review
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Replicache v15.3.0:
  - tx.get / tx.set / tx.del / tx.scan().entries() used idiomatically.
  - Each mutator is a `(tx, args) => Promise<void>` function matching the
    `MutatorDefs` shape Replicache expects; `rep.mutate.momentUpdate(...)`
    on the client invokes the mutator with a WriteTransaction supplied by
    the runtime. No reimplementation of Replicache primitives.
  - No library feature is hand-rolled. Read through replicache@15's
    `packages/replicache/src/transactions.ts` for `WriteTransaction` surface;
    set / del / get / scan are the full set of store operations, and
    that's exactly what the mutators use.

Zod v4:
  - Schemas use `.min() / .max() / .optional() / .nullable() / .enum() /
    .int().positive() / .object()` — no custom refinements, no hand-rolled
    validators.
  - `z.infer<typeof schema>` drives the mutator arg types on both client
    and server via `@milkpod/sync` exports, so the schema is the single
    source of truth (correct pattern per docs/typescript-patterns.md
    "config is the single source of truth").

Drizzle (server-mutators.ts):
  - `.insert(...).values(...).onConflictDoNothing()` on creates —
    idempotent by design, handles Replicache's retry-produces-duplicates
    behaviour (push.ts also guards via LMID but belt-and-suspenders).
  - `.update(...).set(...).where(and(eq(...), eq(...)))` on updates and
    soft-deletes. For notifications, WHERE combines `eq(id)`,
    `eq(recipientId, ctx.userId)`, `isNull(readAt)` — atomic ownership +
    idempotency in a single statement. Correct use of Drizzle's predicate
    composition; no raw SQL needed.
  - `requireEditor` is extracted into authz.ts (Task 8). No duplication
    across mutators.

AI SDK / Eden Treaty / Elysia / Better Auth: not relevant to this task's
files.

LIBRARY LEVERAGE: no hand-rolled replacements found. The mutator layer
composes Replicache+Zod cleanly; the server layer composes Drizzle
predicates cleanly.

Checklist verification
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Backend — Queries & Data Access
  - N+1: N/A. Each server mutator is one INSERT/UPDATE; no loops.
  - Indexes: the WHERE clauses use PK (`id`), `assetId`, `recipientId`,
    which have indexes (Task 4 verified).
  - Pagination: N/A (mutators aren't lists).
  - Transactions: push.ts wraps every mutator in a savepoint (Task 7).
    notificationMarkAllRead is intrinsically one UPDATE — atomic.
  - Parameterized queries: VERIFIED — all via Drizzle builder methods,
    zero `sql\`\`` template usage in these files.
  - Data types: VERIFIED — createdAt passed as ISO string, coerced via
    `new Date(args.createdAt)` at the server boundary (validated by the
    zod schema). No float-for-money / int-for-string antipatterns.

Backend — Security
  - Input validation: push.ts `safeParse(mutation.args)` before dispatch
    (L129). Zod schema defines length bounds / enum constraints.
  - Auth & authorization: `requireEditor(assetId, userId)` on every
    moment/comment mutation; inline ownership WHERE on notifications.
    The `ctx.userId` comes from the authenticated session (index.ts
    guard, Task 12 verified).
  - Rate limiting: Task 13 verified `/api/replicache/push` is in the
    rate-limiter's `categorize()`.
  - Sensitive data in logs: only `console.warn`/`console.error` sites in
    this task's files are in push.ts, and they use `err instanceof Error
    ? err.message : String(err)` — Task 23 / earlier sweeps already
    covered these.
  - No secrets in error responses: server mutators throw via
    MutatorForbiddenError; push.ts catches and logs safely; the PushResponse
    to the client is either `{}` or one of two sentinel error strings — no
    leakage.
  - HTTP status codes: N/A to this task — Task 12 covered route-level
    validation codes.
  - Webhook endpoints: N/A.

Backend — Resilience
  - Timeouts on external calls: N/A (mutators don't call out).
  - Idempotency: `.onConflictDoNothing()` on creates; LMID guard on
    duplicate pushes (push.ts L141–145); notification mark-read is
    idempotent by construction (`isNull(readAt)` predicate).
  - Connection pooling: server mutators run inside the push transaction's
    subTx — Drizzle handles pool lifecycle.
  - Env vars: N/A.

Backend — Code Quality
  - Reuse: `requireEditor` is the shared helper (authz.ts).
    notification inline-WHERE is repeated across the two notification
    mutators but it's one line and the extracted helper would add
    indirection for no benefit.
  - Exhaustive switch: N/A (no switches in this task's files).
  - Magic numbers: N/A.
  - Error handling: mutators throw; push.ts catches.
  - DB migrations: N/A to this task.

Frontend — React Patterns / TypeScript / Tailwind / A11y / Error Handling
  - Files in this task are server or framework-agnostic; see Task 22–25
    for the React sides.
  - TS — no `any` beyond the documented `DbTx = any` in server-mutators
    (same justification as push.ts: driver type varies across versions).
  - Type guards: `isKnownMutator` in push.ts is a custom `is MutatorName`
    type guard — correct pattern from docs/typescript-patterns.md.

Common Patterns (from prior reviews):
  1. console.error full-object logging: N/A here — push.ts already fixed.
  2. fetch/AI-SDK timeouts missing: N/A (no external calls).
  3. New endpoints not rate-limited: covered by Task 13.
  4. opacity-0 without focus-within: N/A (no UI).

     field-by-field conditional assignment is repeated twice in
     server-mutators.ts (momentUpdate, commentUpdate). Could extract
     `buildPatch(args, fields)` helper but only two call sites; left as-is.
  7. Missing exhaustive switches: N/A.
  8. useEffect patterns: N/A.

  10. Billing interval bugs: N/A.

TypeScript Patterns (docs/typescript-patterns.md):
  - Discriminated union state: SyncedNotification uses a `type` discriminant
    (Task 22 handles exhaustiveness on the renderer side).
  - Type guards: isKnownMutator in push.ts.
  - `as const` + `keyof typeof`: `clientMutators as const` +
    `keyof ClientMutators` gives `MutatorName`. `serverMutators as const`
    + `keyof ServerMutators` gives `ServerMutatorName`. Schema keys align.
  - Generic constraints: `DbHandle` in authz.ts uses
    `Parameters<Parameters<...>[0]>[0]` to extract the tx type from
    Drizzle's transaction callback — proper `Awaited<ReturnType<...>>`-

================================================================================
Task 28: Audit assets/service.ts membership migration
================================================================================

Files reviewed (diff vs main):
  packages/api/src/modules/assets/service.ts   (+157 / -32)
  packages/api/src/modules/assets/index.ts     (callers, unchanged by diff)
  packages/api/src/modules/assets/model.ts     (+3 for scope filter)
  packages/api/src/types.ts                    (+AssetRole/AssetOwner/AssetWithAccess)

Scope of the review:
  - Verify every owner-scoped (`eq(mediaAssets.userId, userId)`) query is
    REPLACED where design intent is membership, not merely supplemented.
  - list/get return shapes unchanged for the frontend contract.
  - Members see shared assets; owners see theirs + shared-out.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Per-method audit
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A. create (L160-176) — Transaction wraps the insert + owner membership
   seed via `.onConflictDoNothing()`. Owner identity = authenticated user.
   Owner-scope by construction (`userId` is the creator). CORRECT.

B. list / search / listPage (L178-266) — Joins `assetMembers` on
   `(assetId = mediaAssets.id, userId = caller)`, joins `user` on
   `mediaAssets.userId` to hydrate owner display info. Returns
   `AssetWithAccess[]` (Asset + role + owner). Scope filter via
   `buildSearchConditions` adds `ne(assetMembers.role, 'owner')` when the
   query param `scope === 'shared'`. Every accessible asset appears
   regardless of who owns it; owners keep their own rows because
   migration 0031/0035 backfills seeded owner `asset_member` rows.
   CORRECT.

C. getById (L268-274) — owner-scoped (`eq(mediaAssets.userId, userId)`)
   intentionally. Callers (verified via grep):
     - shares/index.ts:55   (public share link creation — owner only)
     - collections/index.ts:78 (personal collection add — comment:
       "Verify user owns the asset being added")
     - chat/index.ts:30     (chat Q&A — sync-engine-plan.md L72 marks
       viewer Q&A as v2 and out of scope for v1, so owner-only is the
       current contract)
     - assets/index.ts:137  (speaker-name update — transcript metadata
       edit; owner-only pending editor-mode feature)
     - assets/index.ts:169  (retry — owner pays the ingest cost)
   All owner-only by v1 design. CORRECT.

D. getByIdAsMember (L283-299) — new membership-scoped lookup for
   collaborative reads/writes. Callers: moments/index.ts (generate+list),
   comments/index.ts (generate+list). CORRECT.

E. getWithTranscript (L301-329) — BEFORE this review, still owner-scoped
   via `eq(mediaAssets.userId, userId)`. Callers:
     - assets/index.ts:101  (GET /api/assets/:id — library detail view)
     - moments/generate.ts:428 (internal, called after getByIdAsMember)
     - comments/generate.ts:172 (internal, called after getByIdAsMember)
   BUG: every caller is a member-read path. Shared members who saw the
   asset in their library (via the new member-scoped list) would 404 on
   click-through (fetchAssetDetail → /api/assets/:id), and moments/
   comments generate would silently return [] (the guard was
   `!assetData?.transcript`) because the inner owner check swallowed
   non-owner members. FIXED — see Issue #1.

F. getTranscriptLanguage / hasTranscriptSegments / hasEmbeddings /
   deleteEmbeddingsForAsset / getStoredSegmentsForEmbedding (L351-437)
   — operate on assetId only, no userId check. Called from the BullMQ
   worker (ingest-worker.ts) and internal chat handler after an owner
   check upstream. Correct: the caller has already authorized the
   asset; these are pure data accessors.

G. updateSpeakerNames (L439-467) — operates on assetId only. Caller
   (assets/index.ts:137) guards with owner-scoped getById first. Scope
   correctness is at the route, not the helper. CORRECT.

H. update (L469-476) — owner-scoped. Title / source-url edits. Not in
   the v1 collab surface (moments + comments only per
   sync-engine-plan.md L47-51). Owner-only is intentional. CORRECT.

I. remove (L478-484) — owner-scoped. Destroying the asset must be
   owner-only. CORRECT.

/api/assets/:id/search (assets/index.ts:108) — used `getById` (owner).

/api/moments/generate, /api/moments (moments/index.ts) — already

/api/comments/generate, /api/comments (comments/index.ts) — same as

Frontend contract verification

  list / search / listPage previously returned Asset[]. Now return
  AssetWithAccess[] = Asset & { role, owner: { id, name, image } }.
  Additive: every original Asset field is preserved, two new fields

Issues found and fixed
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. packages/api/src/modules/assets/service.ts:getWithTranscript (L301)
   The WHERE predicate was `and(eq(mediaAssets.id, id),
   eq(mediaAssets.userId, userId))` — owner-scoped. Every current caller
   is a member-read path:
     (a) GET /api/assets/:id — library detail view. The new member-
         scoped list surfaces shared assets; click-through hit
         fetchAssetDetail → /api/assets/:id → this method → 404 for a
         non-owner member.
     (b) generateMoments — moments/index.ts already gates with
         getByIdAsMember; the downstream getWithTranscript in
         generate.ts:428 bypassed the gate with its own owner check, so
         non-owner members would pass the gate and then silently get
         `[]` (the guard `!assetData?.transcript` treats 404 as "no
         transcript").
     (c) generateComments — identical issue to (b).

   Fix: replace the `eq(mediaAssets.userId, userId)` filter with an
   inner join on `assetMembers` keyed by `(assetId, userId)`. Owners
   continue to pass because:
     - 0031_backfill_asset_member_owners.sql seeded a role='owner'
       asset_member row for every pre-existing asset;
     - 0035_backfill_orphan_asset_member_owners.sql covered the
       migration gap;
     - AssetService.create now seeds the owner membership inside the
       creation transaction (L170-173) for every new asset.
   Same three callers auto-inherit the fix; no call-site changes needed.
   Added a docstring explaining the scope expansion + why owners still
   pass.

   Sync-engine-plan.md L47-51 and L212 back the design: CVR is keyed
   on asset_members, and "Pull ACL: pull query itself joins
   asset_members, so the CVR snapshot is intrinsically user-filtered."
   The REST read-path must align with that, not hold a stricter owner
   gate.

2. packages/api/src/modules/assets/index.ts:108 (/api/assets/:id/search)
   Used AssetService.getById (owner-only). Transcript search is a
   read-only operation over data the member already has access to
   (moment/comment reads succeed on shared assets). A shared member
   opening the detail view can call moment/comment list endpoints but
   would be rejected from the transcript search endpoint — inconsistent
   and user-visible as a broken search bar on shared videos.
   Fix: switch to getByIdAsMember.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues noted (no fix)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. list() embeds the userId filter in the innerJoin; search()/listPage()
   put it in the WHERE via buildSearchConditions. Functionally
   equivalent — the `(assetId, userId)` PK on asset_members guarantees
   one row per accessible asset in either case — but the style
   divergence is a minor readability snag. Not fixing: consolidating
   would require either threading query params through list() (over-
   generalization) or duplicating the buildSearchConditions prefix
   (DRY violation). The current split is the best trade-off.

2. `buildSearchConditions` returns `SQL[]` — loose typing. Drizzle
   exports the more specific type through `SQL<unknown>[]`. The
   existing file uses `SQL[]` and the compiler accepts it; tightening
   is cosmetic and outside Task 28 scope (the surrounding code already
   uses the same pattern for the cursor filter).

3. `chat/index.ts:30` chat over a shared asset is v2 per sync-engine-
   plan.md L72. Noted for the v2 plan; not a Task 28 concern. When v2
   lands, chat should switch to getByIdAsMember with an asset-level
   `allow_viewer_qa` flag (per the plan's L72 design sketch).

4. `update` (L469) and `remove` (L478) keep owner-scope. If editors
   are ever granted metadata-edit rights in a future phase, those
   paths gain an `AsEditor` variant. Not in scope for v1.

5. The `getByIdAsMember` method exists but `update` / `remove` /
   `updateSpeakerNames` still compose owner-only `getById`. That's
   deliberate per the v1 capability matrix (owner = all, editor =
   moments+comments, viewer = moments+comments reads). Documented
   in the docstring on `getByIdAsMember` (service.ts:276-282).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Library-leverage review
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Drizzle ORM:
  - Owner-info hydration is a straightforward `innerJoin(user, eq(user.id,
    mediaAssets.userId))` on the list queries — could alternately use
    Drizzle's relational query API with `with: { owner: true }` if
    mediaAssets has a declared relation. Searched the schema file; the
    current code uses select+join rather than the relational API
    consistently across the module. Not refactoring here: the explicit
    select shape is equally idiomatic, the join columns are narrow
    (id/name/image only) so there's no "with + transform" penalty, and
    Task 28's scope is scoping correctness, not query-style unification.
    Noted for a future sweep if the `with:` pattern gets adopted
    elsewhere in the module.

  - `getWithTranscript` now composes an innerJoin (asset_members) +
    two leftJoins (transcripts, transcript_segments). Single round-trip;
    no N+1. Drizzle's `.orderBy(desc(transcripts.createdAt),
    desc(transcripts.id), transcriptSegments.startTime)` gives a stable
    sort for the downstream Map-based transcript selection logic.

  - `.onConflictDoNothing()` in create() makes the owner-membership
    seeding safe against concurrent requests (same asset, re-run
    mutation) without needing explicit locking.

Elysia:
  - Route-level `{ auth: true }` on every handler (.post/.get/.patch/
    .delete). Verified Task 12 checked Replicache-route guards; same
    pattern applies here. No opportunity to collapse into a .guard()
    block because every route consumes `user.id` via macro — moving to
    a group guard would not de-duplicate material code.

No hand-rolled logic that could be replaced by a library primitive
was found in this task's scope.

================================================================================
Task 29 — Verify no REST/Replicache double-writes for comments and moments
================================================================================

Scope: packages/api/src/modules/comments/{service,index}.ts,
       packages/api/src/modules/moments/{service,index}.ts,
       packages/sync/src/mutators/{comments,moments}.ts,
       apps/web/src/components/comments/comments-tab.tsx,
       apps/web/src/components/moments/moments-tab.tsx,
       apps/web/src/components/asset/tabs/{comments,moments}-tab-content.tsx.

Goal: confirm that the REST POST routes that still survive in the
branch are NOT issuing a second write for the same logical mutation
that a Replicache mutator is already handling — i.e., no path where
the UI fires `rep.mutate.X()` AND an `api.api.X.post(...)` for the same
entity + operation on the same user action.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Write inventory — what path each mutation takes
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Comments (REST-only, no Replicache UI caller):
  CREATE   : REST batch via `POST /api/comments/generate` →
             `generateComments()` → `CommentService.insertMany` (server-
             side LLM generation). NO caller of
             `rep.mutate.commentCreate` anywhere in apps/web —
             verified via
             `rg 'mutate\.(comment|moment)(Create|Update|Delete)' apps/web`
             (only moments hits returned). The client mutator exists in
             `packages/sync/src/mutators/comments.ts` but is dead code
             from the UI's perspective. No double-write.
  UPDATE   : No update UI at all (body edit not exposed in this PR).
  DELETE   : User clicks dismiss → `comments-tab.tsx:55` fires REST
             `POST /api/comments/:id/feedback {action:'dismiss'}` only.
             No `rep.mutate.commentDelete` caller. No double-write.
  LIST READ: `comments-tab-content.tsx:17` one-shot REST GET populates
             `initialComments`; `comments-tab.tsx` drives the list from
             local `useState`. No Replicache subscription hooked up for
             comments yet, so REST is the single source. No overlap.

Moments (dual-path with strict if/else branching; mutually exclusive):
  CREATE   : REST batch via `POST /api/moments/generate` (same shape as
             comments). No `rep.mutate.momentCreate` caller. No
             double-write.
  UPDATE   : `moments-tab.tsx:118-129` `handleSave`:
               if (rep) { await rep.mutate.momentUpdate({…isSaved:true}); return; }
               await api.api.moments({id}).feedback.post({action:'save'});
             The explicit `return` makes the branches mutually
             exclusive — a call will take EXACTLY one of the two paths
             per click. No double-write.
  DELETE   : `moments-tab.tsx:131-139` `handleDismiss`: same
             if-rep-else-REST pattern with explicit return. Mutually
             exclusive. No double-write.
  LIST READ: `moments-tab-content.tsx:17` REST GET populates
             `initialMoments`. `moments-tab.tsx:79-86` uses
             `useSubscribedMoments(assetId)` when `rep && syncReady`,
             else falls back to `initialMoments`. Single source at any
             given render; handoff is driven by `syncReady`. No
             overlapping writes.

Server-mutator ↔ service-method parity:
  - `serverMutators.momentUpdate` (server-mutators.ts:56-76) directly
    `UPDATE asset_moment SET …` based on the patch shape. It does NOT
    call through `MomentService.saveMoment`, but it also does NOT
    double-insert into `asset_moment_feedback` (noted below).
  - `serverMutators.momentDelete` (server-mutators.ts:78-93) sets
    `deletedAt`, while the REST `dismissMoment` sets `dismissedAt`.
    Different columns, but pull.ts:245-249 filters on `isNull` of BOTH,
    so either path hides the row from sync consumers and the REST list
    filter (moments/service.ts:23-27) was already updated to do the
    same (`isNull(deletedAt)`). Net effect at the list level: parity.

Server-side poke sequencing (re-verified in passing since this task
touches the pipeline):
  - `CommentService.insertMany` / `deleteByAsset` / `dismissComment`
    (service.ts) fire `AssetMemberService.pokeMembers(assetId)` AFTER
    the Drizzle write resolves; the write is auto-committed at that
    point. Same pattern in `MomentService.insertMany` /
    `deleteByAssetAndPreset` / `saveMoment` / `dismissMoment`. Pokes
    run post-commit (Task 10 invariant). No change needed.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues found and fixed
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  None. The dual-path is mutually exclusive on both save and dismiss,
  and every other comment/moment mutation has exactly one write path.
  No code changes made in Task 29.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues noted (no fix needed, or deferred to a later sweep)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. moments-tab.tsx:118-139 REST save/dismiss fallback is effectively
   dead code under normal auth flow.
   Why acceptable: `DashboardLayout` (apps/web/src/components/layouts/
   dashboard.tsx:14-38) wraps every authenticated route in
   `<ReplicacheProvider>`, and asset pages live inside the dashboard
   layout (apps/web/src/app/asset/[id]/page.tsx:26 — "ReplicacheProvider
   is mounted in DashboardLayout"). `rep` is only null for the single
   render tick before the provider's `useEffect` sets the instance.
   A user can't click save/dismiss that fast. Removing the fallback
   would touch the function signature and prop contract of
   `MomentCard`; deliberately leaving as defensive code. Not a
   double-write.

2. Replicache path skips the `asset_moment_feedback` row that the REST
   path inserts via `MomentService.addFeedback`.
   Why acceptable: `asset_moment_feedback` is WRITE-ONLY in the current
   codebase — `rg 'assetMomentFeedback'` across packages/ returns only
   the insert and the conflict-fallback SELECT in `addFeedback`, plus
   the schema and the `MomentFeedback` type re-export. No aggregation,
   analytics consumer, or read query consumes it. Losing the signal
   when Replicache is active doesn't affect any product surface.
   Flagging for future design work: either (a) inline the feedback
   insert inside `serverMutators.momentUpdate` when `args.isSaved`
   flips, or (b) remove the table. Out of scope for Task 29 because it
   is a feature-design decision, not a bug.

3. Comments have no Replicache UI path at all.
   Why acceptable: Task 29 is specifically "no double-writes". Zero
   writes to Replicache from the UI means zero double-writes. The
   client mutators (`commentCreateClient`, `commentUpdateClient`,
   `commentDeleteClient`) exist in `packages/sync/src/mutators/
   comments.ts` but have no caller in `apps/web`. They remain plumbed
   for a future wiring pass. Not a bug.

4. `moments-tab.tsx:118-139` `handleSave` / `handleDismiss` have no
   try/catch — if the Replicache mutator or the REST fallback throws,
   the user sees no error toast.
   Why acceptable here: Task 34 ("Sweep catch blocks for silent error
   swallowing") is the scheduled place for this. Logging it here to
   avoid duplicating work; the fix should go in alongside Task 34's
   systematic sweep. Per Replicache docs, `rep.mutate.X()` only throws
   on client-side validator failures (zod schema mismatch on args) or
   a closed tx — both are programmer errors, not runtime/network
   failures. The real silent-failure risk is the REST fallback's
   undestructured `error` field, which the dead-code fallback reduces
   in practice.

5. List-read divergence between REST and Replicache for moments:
   REST `MomentService.list` filters on
   `eq(assetMoments.userId, userId)` — caller's own moments only.
   Replicache pull.ts:240-250 filters on
   `inArray(assetId, accessibleAssetIds)` — every member's moments on
   every accessible asset.
   Why acceptable: this is the intended collaboration semantics.
   Sharing an asset surfaces every member's moments to every member
   via Replicache. The REST list is only used as the SSR priming
   payload before the subscription takes over (moments-tab.tsx:79-86),
   so the brief window where a user sees "their own" moments before
   "everyone's" is a cosmetic handoff, not a correctness problem. Not
   a Task 29 concern.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Library leverage / composability check
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Drizzle:
  - `CommentService.insertMany` / `MomentService.insertMany` use a
    single batch `db().insert(table).values(rows).returning()` — no
    per-row loop with an insert. Correct batch-insert primitive.
  - `MomentService.addFeedback` already uses
    `.onConflictDoNothing().returning()` + a single SELECT fallback —
    the cleanest expression of "insert-or-lookup" with Drizzle. The
    post-conflict SELECT uses the same columns as the unique
    constraint (`asset_moment_feedback_unique`), matching the
    `onConflictDoNothing` behavior. No change.
  - Server mutators bypass the service classes and write the table
    directly (server-mutators.ts). This is DELIBERATE — the server
    mutator runs inside the Replicache push tx, which already owns
    the Drizzle tx; delegating to `MomentService.saveMoment` would
    open a second tx and break atomicity with the
    `replicache_client.lastMutationId` bump. Keep as-is.

Elysia:
  - `comments/index.ts` and `moments/index.ts` both apply
    `{ auth: true }` per-route. Moving to `.guard({ auth: true },
    app => ...)` was discussed in Task 12 for the Replicache module
    specifically; here each route also pulls `user` off the context
    via the `authMacro`, which is evaluated per-route. Moving to a
    group guard would not change behavior because every route needs
    `user` regardless. No change.
  - No opportunity for `.onError()` consolidation — the only explicit
    catch (comments/index.ts:46-50) converts LLM-generation failure
    into a sanitized 500; the moments/index.ts `/generate` route
    doesn't catch at all (errors bubble to Elysia's default handler).
    Consistent with Task 12's decision to keep generation-specific
    error shape local.

React:
  - `handleSave` / `handleDismiss` use a plain `if (rep) ... return;`
    branch rather than a hook-level abstraction. An
    `useEntityMutator()` that centralized "replicache-or-REST" would
    be over-abstraction for two call sites — three lines of branching
    is not a duplication worth extracting. Leave as-is.

No hand-rolled logic that could be replaced by a library primitive
was found in this task's scope.

Checklist verification
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Backend — Queries & Data Access
  - N+1: insertMany for both entities is a single bulk insert. The
    trailing `for (const assetId of assetIds) { pokeMembers(…) }` loop
    is N=1 or 2 in practice (a single REST call is scoped to a single
    asset), and poke is not a DB query — it publishes to an in-memory
    SSE channel. Not N+1.
  - Indexes: no new queries added in Task 29's scope.
  - Pagination: `CommentService.list` / `MomentService.list` still
    return all rows for an asset. Out of scope for this task (Task 30
    covers the Replicache-vs-list interaction).
  - Transactions: server mutators run inside the push transaction.
    REST service methods auto-commit per write; pokes run after
    commit. Correct.
  - Parameterized queries: all Drizzle builder; no raw sql\`\` with
    interpolation.
  - Data types: jsonb `evidenceRefs` typed via schema inference; other
    columns unchanged in this task.

Backend — Security
  - Input validation: `CommentModel.generate` / `CommentModel.feedback`
    / `MomentModel.generate` / `MomentModel.feedback` validate bodies;
    Task 12 verified Replicache push/pull TypeBox validators.
  - Auth & authorization: REST routes gate on membership via
    `AssetService.getByIdAsMember`; server mutators gate on
    `requireEditor(assetId, userId)` inside the push tx.
    Collaborator-with-viewer-role cannot write (verified in Task 15).
  - Rate limiting: `/api/comments` and `/api/moments` prefixes are
    pre-existing and covered by the rate limiter. Task 13 covered

    the `msg = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)`

  - Timeouts: no new external HTTP in Task 29's scope. `generateComments`
    / `generateMoments` LLM calls were covered in a prior task.
  - Idempotency: `insertMany` relies on `id` collisions to be prevented
    upstream (server IDs come from the LLM pipeline with collision-
    resistant nanoid); Replicache server mutators use

    (used by both services). Shared `AssetService.getByIdAsMember`

    `moments-tab-content.tsx` use a `fetchedRef` guard for
    StrictMode-double-invocation dedupe; no subscriptions or timers
    to clean up. The one-shot fetch resolves into setState — if the
    component unmounts before the fetch returns, the setState warns
    but doesn't crash; acceptable for a brief SSR-prime.
  - Keys: `comments.map` uses `comment.id`, `rows.map` uses
    `moment.id`. Stable.
  - SSR guards: `fetchedRef` + `useEffect` ensure fetch only fires
    client-side. `api` is Eden-typed, browser-safe.
  - No dangerouslySetInnerHTML: comment/moment bodies flow through
    React text nodes. Safe.
  - Dead code: mutator files exist for comments but have no caller.
    Documented as (3) above; not dead within the sync package itself
    (pull/push tests + server parity depend on them).

Frontend — TypeScript
  - No `any`: both components use explicit types from

Frontend — Tailwind v4 / Accessibility / UX

review(task 29): verify no REST/Replicache double-writes for

================================================================================
Task 30 — Library / moments UI handles Replicache first-render undefined
================================================================================

Files in scope:
  apps/web/src/components/library/{asset-card,asset-list,library-tab}.tsx
  apps/web/src/components/moments/{moment-card,moments-tab}.tsx

Library APIs verified:
  - apps/web/src/lib/replicache/hooks.ts:20-51 — usePrefixSubscription
    initializes items via useState<T[]>([]) and ready via useState(false),
    so the wrapper NEVER returns `undefined` to consumers. Replicache's
    rep.subscribe runs the scan against IndexedDB asynchronously inside
    the effect; the initial render returns the [] / false state. No risk
    of an undefined-deref crash on first render.
  - useReplicache() returns null during SSR (provider's useState initial
    is null; the instance is created in useEffect, which only runs on
    the client). Components that use it are 'use client' but render on
    the server during SSR with rep === null. The fall-through to
    initialMoments handles that without crashing.
  - rep.mutate.<name>(args) returns a promise that resolves once the
    LOCAL transaction commits. Server-side rejections are surfaced via
    the pusher's httpRequestInfo and Replicache's rebase/retry, NOT by
    throwing from the mutate call. So a try/catch around rep.mutate
    only catches sync errors (zod validation, IDB write failures).
    Documented; this is the v15 behavior per the @rocicorp/replicache
    .d.ts in node_modules.
  - Eden treaty .post() returns { data, error } and never throws on
    HTTP status codes — error must be checked explicitly.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues found and fixed
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. moments/moment-card.tsx:55-63 (handleAction)
   - Wrong: try/finally with NO catch. If onSave or onDismiss reject,
     the promise rejection bubbles up as an unhandled rejection and the
     user gets ZERO feedback (loading state clears via finally, but the
     bookmark/dismiss visibly does nothing). This violates the "every
     API call's .catch() must give the user feedback" rule from
     review-prompt.md and was the same silent-swallow pattern called
     out as a high-signal nudge (#5).
   - Fix: added a `catch { toast.error(...) }` branch that surfaces a
     readable, action-specific message ("Could not save moment..." /
     "Could not dismiss moment..."). Imported `toast` from 'sonner'.
   - The toast lives in MomentCard rather than the parent because the
     loading lifecycle (actionLoading) is owned here and the same catch
     covers both Replicache mutators (which throw on zod/IDB failure)
     and the REST fallback in moments-tab.

2. moments/moments-tab.tsx:117-139 (handleSave / handleDismiss REST fallback)
   - Wrong: the `if (rep) … else <REST>` fallback path called
     `await api.api.moments({…}).feedback.post({…})` without
     destructuring `error` from Eden's `{ data, error }` return. If the
     server returned 4xx/5xx the call resolved (no throw) and the
     parent's catch never fired — user sees the loading spinner clear,
     no toast.
   - Fix: destructure `{ error }` and rethrow on truthy. Now the
     MomentCard.handleAction catch (fix #1) surfaces a toast. Both save
     and dismiss handlers updated.
   - The fallback path itself is reachable only when MomentsTab is
     rendered outside the ReplicacheProvider — currently never in the
     codebase, but defensively correct.

3. moments/moments-tab.tsx:87 (redundant per-render array allocation)
   - Wrong: `const moments = rows.map((r) => r.moment);` ran every
     render outside the useMemo. Its only consumers were `.length`
     checks (`moments.length > 0`, `isEmpty = … && moments.length === 0`),
     so the entire mapped array was allocated and discarded just to read
     its length.
   - Fix: deleted the local `moments` binding; switched the two call
     sites to `rows.length`. Same observable behavior, one fewer
     allocation per render. Not "redundant state" per se (it was a
     derived const, not state), but trimming it satisfies the
     "no unnecessary re-renders" sweep cleanly.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues noted (no fix needed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

(1) moments-tab.tsx — first-render flash from SSR data → empty sync → populated.
    On a fresh visit (no IndexedDB entry for this asset's moments yet),
    the lifecycle is:
      a. SSR renders rows from `initialMoments` (server-fetched moments).
      b. ReplicacheProvider mounts, creates the instance (rep != null).
      c. useSubscribedMoments runs scan() against an empty IDB → fires
         onData([]) → ready = true → component switches to
         `syncedMoments` = []. Briefly shows the empty state.
      d. The kicked `rep.pull()` returns, populates IDB, subscribe re-fires
         with the real rows.
    A clean fix would require Replicache to expose a "first pull
    completed" signal (it does not via the subscribe API — the only
    callbacks are onData/onError, fired per query result, with no way
    to distinguish "IDB hit" from "pull-then-IDB hit"). Heuristic
    alternatives (delay 200ms, lock in once non-empty, hydrate IDB from
    initialMoments) all have correctness trade-offs:
      - locking in once non-empty inverts to stale-display when the user
        truly empties the list from another device;
      - delays add jitter to the populated case;
      - IDB hydration from props duplicates the server's pull payload.
    Task 30's explicit asks (no crash; no stale fetch overlapping sync)
    are satisfied — this is a polish concern outside the task scope.
    Worth a follow-up plan entry if the flash becomes a real complaint.

(2) library-tab.tsx:50-66 — view toggle uses bare `<button>` elements
    without `role="radiogroup"` / `role="radio"` / `aria-checked` (or
    the `tablist` / `tab` / `aria-selected` alternative). The
    review-prompt's a11y checklist calls for radiogroup roles on toggle
    groups. Adding correct radiogroup semantics requires arrow-key
    navigation and roving tabindex, which is a non-trivial change that
    would also need to be applied consistently to the sibling
    MomentPresetSwitcher (not in this task's scope) to keep the codebase
    coherent. Recommend an a11y-sweep follow-up that converts both
    toggles to a shared shadcn ToggleGroup primitive. Not fixed here.

(3) moments-tab.tsx — `rep` value is null during the brief client-side
    window between hydration and ReplicacheProvider mounting its
    instance. During that window we render initialMoments (correct).
    Mutations attempted in this window would take the REST fallback,
    but the buttons that trigger save/dismiss only render once the row
    list is populated, which after fix #2 is guarded by either the
    Replicache path or a successful REST round-trip with error
    propagation. No bug.

(4) asset-card.tsx:32-34 — `hasAccess` is a hand-rolled type guard
    using `'role' in a && 'owner' in a`. It is justified: Asset is
    structurally a subset of AssetWithAccess (no required fields can
    be runtime-checked to discriminate), so a TS-wide discriminator
    isn't possible without changing the upstream model. This is the
    correct pattern per docs/typescript-patterns.md.

(5) moments-tab.tsx — handleGenerate empty `catch {}` (no err binding).
    Acceptable: the network/upgrade path already invokes
    handleUpgradeError for 402 responses; the catch only fires for
    truly unexpected throws and shows a generic toast. The err object
    is intentionally not inspected (no console.error of the raw
    object — would leak per high-signal nudge #1).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Library improvements made
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

None — the existing implementation already uses the right primitives:
  - Replicache: subscribe + mutate via clientMutators (via the wrapper
    hooks task 25 hardened with onError).
  - TanStack Query: useInfiniteQuery for the non-Replicache asset list
    (assets are not yet sync'd; they remain a REST + SSE source). No
    overlap with Replicache, so no duplication to consolidate.
  - Eden treaty: { data, error } destructuring after fix #2.

The asset-list path is intentionally not on Replicache — it serves
both 'all' and 'shared' scopes via the same query infrastructure with
a scope query param. Lifting assets onto sync would expand the CVR
surface significantly and is a separate plan item, not Task 30 scope.

================================================================================
Task 31 — Sweep `console.error` for full-error-object logging
================================================================================

Scope: every file in `git diff main...HEAD --name-only`. Goal: ensure no
console.{error,warn} site logs the raw `err` value, since unknown
catch bindings can carry connection strings, SQL state, raw response
bodies, or full stack traces (review-prompt.md §Common Patterns #1).

The branch-touched file list was filtered to .ts/.tsx (87 files total
in the diff; 64 .ts/.tsx). All console.error and console.warn calls in
that set were inspected. The fix pattern is uniform:

  console.error('ctx', err)                       -> unsafe
  console.error('ctx', err instanceof Error ? err.message : err)  -> still unsafe (raw err in fallback)
  console.error('ctx', err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err))  -> safe

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues found and fixed
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

(1) apps/server/src/index.ts:31 — recoverStaleAssets catch logged
    `err instanceof Error ? err.message : err`. The non-Error fallback
    forwards the raw `err` (could be a Postgres pool error object with
    connection string in `err.cause` or `err.code`/`err.severity`/
    `err.detail` chained from drizzle/pg). Changed to `String(err)`.
    The branch only added two import lines + two function calls in this
    file (initReplicachePokeBridge + closeReplicachePokeBridge) so the
    log was pre-existing on main, but the file is in the diff and the
    sweep mandates fixing every unsafe site in branch-touched files.

(2) apps/server/src/index.ts:75 — workers shutdown catch logged the
    bare `err`. Same risk class. Changed to safe-format.

(3) apps/server/src/index.ts:88 — Redis shutdown catch logged the bare
    `err`. Same risk class. Changed to safe-format.

(4) apps/server/src/index.ts:96 — DB pool shutdown catch logged the
    bare `err`. Same risk class. Changed to safe-format.

(5) apps/web/src/lib/server/notifications.ts:46 — markNotifications-
    ReadForAsset catch had `err instanceof Error ? err.message : err`.
    Non-Error fallback leaks. Changed to String(err). Same fix as (1).

(6) packages/api/src/events/replicache-events.ts:104 — Redis pub/sub
    init catch had `err instanceof Error ? err.message : err`. The pub
    code path uses ioredis whose error objects carry `command` (raw
    Redis command + args) and host/port. Changed to String(err).

(7) packages/api/src/events/replicache-events.ts:176 — subscribeUser-
    Pokes' subscribe().catch() handler had the same pattern. Same fix.

(8) packages/api/src/modules/notifications/service.ts:89 — Notification-
    Service.poke catch logged bare `err` in the non-Error path. Same
    fix.

console.error/warn was deliberately preserved (not switched to a
silent swallow) because all of these are best-effort fire-and-forget
side effects (poke fanout, shutdown drain, auto-mark-read) where the
operator still wants visibility into transient errors during dev / on
production tail logs — but the visibility must not include raw error
bodies.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Issues noted (no fix needed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

(a) packages/api/src/modules/comments/index.ts:48 — already uses a
    pre-derived `msg` variable (`err instanceof Error ? err.message :
    String(err)`). Safe.

(b) packages/api/src/modules/assets/index.ts:207, 215, 222, 228 —
    retry / pipeline catch sites already use the safe-format inline.
    Safe.

(c) packages/api/src/modules/podcasts/episode-pipeline.ts:27 — only
    logs the episode ID literal, no error object. Safe.

(d) packages/api/src/modules/podcasts/episode-pipeline.ts:130 — uses
    a derived `message` variable computed safely above. Safe.

(e) packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:173 — uses safe-format
    inline. (Already fixed in Task 7.) Safe.

(f) packages/auth/src/index.ts:53 — Resend OTP send failure logs an
    object `{ type, errorMessage }` where errorMessage is derived
    safely. Safe.

(g) packages/auth/src/invite-email.ts:59 — same pattern as (f). Safe.

(h) packages/auth/src/signup-hooks.ts:106 — same pattern as (f). Safe.

(i) apps/web/src/components/notifications/notification-bell.tsx:41,56
    — both use safe-format inline. Safe.

    safe-format inline. Safe.

(k) packages/api/src/modules/asset-members/service.ts:104, 279, 355 —
    all three fire-and-forget catches use safe-format with String(err)
    in fallback. Safe.

(l) packages/api/src/modules/assets/index.ts:159 — deleteStoredUpload
    catch uses safe-format. Safe.

(m) packages/api/src/modules/replicache/push.ts:115, 132, 167, 216 —
    all four console.warn sites either log fixed strings, log
    `parsed.error.issues` (zod validation issues, structured + safe),
    or `err.message` after an `err instanceof MutatorForbiddenError`
    narrow, or `String(err)` fallback. All safe.
